    Authoring Lessons Log¶

    Status: Accepted | Updated: 2026-07-25 Serves: A11 (structural quality tools across the corpus) Validated by: uv run python scripts/check_lessons_log.py

    Note on this first landing. The lessons below were learned building a three-book 16+ gamebook series (the Wyrmreach chain), and several Ref cells cite that series' spec, skeleton, and filled-story artifacts. Those artifacts are not part of this change: they land in a separate follow-up push so the process and tooling work can be reviewed on its own, alongside the other in-flight revisions to story development. Every lesson and every tooling change here stands without them; the story-dependent tests skip until they arrive (6 skips in the suite today), and the two Wyrmreach design and report documents are included because they are the evidence base each lesson cites.

    Every story development run appends here. The point is that a lesson learned once becomes a change to the tooling rather than folklore an author has to rediscover, so each row carries a proposed change and a status that tracks whether it landed.

    How to use this log¶

    When to append. At the end of any authoring run: a new story or series book, a skeleton promotion, a fill pass, or a validator/gate change made in service of authoring. If the run produced no lesson, append nothing; an empty run is a real outcome.

    What counts as a lesson. Something a future author or a future tooling change should act on. Three tests, any one of which qualifies it:

    1. It cost real iteration to discover (a rule interaction, a calibration, an ordering constraint).
    2. The tooling let a defect through, or reported it in a way that did not point at the cause.
    3. It would be re-learned from scratch by the next person, because nothing in the repo records it.

    A one-off typo is not a lesson. "The gate reports band membership, so the floor reads as a pass" is.

    Fields.

    Field Rule
    ID AL-NNN, sequential, never reused or renumbered.
    Date ISO date the lesson was recorded.
    Source The run that produced it (story slug, series book, or workstream).
    Category One of validator, tooling, authoring-craft, scale, metadata, process, docs, product.
    Lesson What is true, stated so it is actionable without reading the source run.
    Proposed change The change that would stop this recurring, or none (craft note) when the lesson is guidance rather than a code change.
    Status open, accepted, applied, rejected, or superseded.
    Ref Commit, PR, file, or doc. Required when status is applied, rejected, or superseded.

    Review cadence. Sweep the open rows whenever a phase is planned. An open row that has survived two phase boundaries is either genuinely low value (mark it rejected, with the reason in Ref) or it is real debt being avoided; either way, decide rather than let it sit.

    Status discipline. accepted means the change is agreed but unbuilt, and it must appear in a plan somewhere. applied means the change is merged and the Ref proves it. Nothing moves to applied on the strength of intent.

    Log¶

    ID Date Source Category Lesson Proposed change Status Ref
    AL-001 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 tooling Compiling the FILL skeleton and the filled story from one spec makes drift between them structurally impossible; two hand-maintained artifacts drift silently. Keep one-spec compilation as the authoring pattern for any multi-artifact story. applied scripts/build_series_book.py
    AL-002 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b2 authoring-craft In a carries_state: true continuation a carried variable initialises true, so every == false condition on it is unsatisfiable and a hard L2-11. Carried state must be read-only; acquisition moves to a new book-local variable. none (craft note); recorded in the series design doc's carried-variable rule. applied wyrmreach-series-design.md section 4
    AL-003 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b2, b3 validator A gate on a new variable placed earlier in the graph than any node granting it is also an L2-11 dead branch. This is the same class as AL-002 but not obvious from the rule text. Add a cause hint to the L2-11 message for both patterns; it also improves the Stage C repair prompt, which gets findings verbatim. applied validator/layer2.py::_dead_branch_cause names carried-variable polarity or grant-order in the L2-11 message; both verified against the real book plus 3 tests
    AL-004 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 authoring-craft Flesch-Kincaid is driven by sentence count, not passage length: at a fixed ~60 words the same content scores FK 5 in four or five sentences, 9-11 in three, 13+ in two. The practical 16+ rule is three sentences per passage. none (craft note); recorded in the series design doc's reading-level section. applied wyrmreach-series-design.md section 6
    AL-005 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 authoring-craft An RL-13 outlier can be retuned without rewriting a word by joining or splitting sentence boundaries; 185 of 212 cleared this way. Two guards are required: only downcase a folded-in leading word if it is seen lowercase in the corpus and never capitalised mid-sentence, and only split where both sides are 8+ words and the right side opens with a subject. none (craft note); the guards are the reusable part. applied wyrmreach-series-report.md N8
    AL-006 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 validator RL-13 scores <<FILL>> directive text, so every skeleton emits one warning per node (746 for a ceiling-scale book), training reviewers to ignore the rule. PL-19 already skips FILL bodies and is the pattern to copy. Skip bodies containing the FILL marker in check_reading_level; hoist the marker constant so both modules import it. applied validator/reading_level.py skips FILL bodies; the ceiling-scale skeleton now emits 0 RL-13 warnings instead of 746, with a regression test
    AL-007 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1 authoring-craft The Layer-2 configuration key includes the visited set of nodes carrying once effects, so each once effect doubles the config space. In an acyclic graph once is redundant, because no node is entered twice on a path. none (craft note): never use once in a DAG-shaped story. applied wyrmreach-series-design.md section 6
    AL-008 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 authoring-craft Declare a carried integer variable's range as what the continuation can actually reach, not what the variable could theoretically hold. Narrowing renown to 3-5 and dropping an unreachable carried flag is the difference between 36,781 configurations and risking the 100k L2-12 cap. Superseded: narrowing is right for the configuration budget but must never narrow below the range the SENDING book can end in, or the client clamps carried outcomes away. Corrected rule in AL-038: narrow toward what the continuation can reach, but the receiving range must contain the sending range. superseded AL-038; renown widened back to 0-5 in data/series/wyrmreach/book3.spec.json
    AL-009 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 scale A ceiling-scale gamebook needs breadth, not length: rooms hung as parallel chains off an act hub and reconverging on an act gate fit 746 nodes inside 74 of 93 permitted hops, and keep a reader's route at 52 nodes. none (craft note); the shape is recorded as the six room shapes. applied wyrmreach-series-design.md section 5
    AL-010 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 process Generate wiring, never voice. Structure was generated from six formal room shapes and every beat, label and ending title was then authored over it through a patch keyed by node id, with an assertion that label count equals choice count. That assertion caught eleven real mismatches that would have shipped as generated text. Keep the two-stage split for any story past the ~460-node hand-authoring ceiling; the label-count assertion is the load-bearing part. applied wyrmreach-series-report.md N7
    AL-011 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 scale L2-13 firing past 460 nodes is the expected and correct output, not a defect: it states that the completed Layer-2 walk, not human review, is the sole correctness guarantee. At that scale the walk has to be treated as the acceptance test rather than a lint pass. Every structural problem in book 3 was found by L2-11 or the budget report, none by reading the spec. none; behaviour is correct as designed. Document it so the warning is not "fixed" by someone later. accepted story-quality-lessons-2026-07.md finding N6
    AL-012 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 metadata metadata.estimated_minutes is ADR-011's fastest-finish clock, and nothing validates it, so on a hand-authored or imported book it is whatever the author typed. All three books were wrong by 3-6x (40/40/90 declared against 8/8/14 canonical). Fixed in the books. Still open: a PL-series advisory comparing the declared value against mutation/identity.py::recompute_estimated_minutes, which already exists and is tested. applied commit 86dafda (books); gate advisory tracked as AL-021
    AL-013 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 validator PL-19 reports band membership only, so the 55 words/node advisory floor reads as a pass when the cell target is 80. Authoring to the floor put books 1 and 2 below their cell's intended whole-world range (77 and 82 minutes against ~110-175) and dragged the fastest-finish clock under its cell target. Report distance from target, not just band membership. Consider a tighter two-sided band for production_eligible stories, keeping the wide band for MVP/test tiers. open story-quality-lessons-2026-07.md finding D
    AL-014 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 tooling No hand-authored skeleton can pass the skeleton-promotion CI gate: check_promotion_bundle.py requires a lineage sidecar unconditionally, and no sidecar exists anywhere in skeletons/. The thirteen existing hand-authored skeletons are green only because CI proves changed files and none has been touched since the workflow landed. Add an authored-bundle proof path (origin sidecar, check_skeleton, theme contract when present, WS-5 anti-clone floor), failing closed when neither sidecar is present. Not an exemption. open story-quality-lessons-2026-07.md finding A
    AL-015 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 validator Nothing checks the within-story ending mix. Book 3 is 118 death endings of 232 (51%) and gates clean; a 95%-death book would too. The normalised kind and valence histograms already exist in diversity/structure.py, used only for between-story anti-clone distance. A PL-series advisory over the existing histograms: warn when one ending kind exceeds a per-band share, or positive-valence endings fall below a floor. applied PL-24 in validator/policy.py: a 60% single-kind ceiling plus a style-aware winnability floor (prose share, gamebook absolute count). Fires on 6 pre-existing books, silent on the wyrmreach three
    AL-016 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b2 process A comparison check is only as good as the independence of its two inputs. A builder bug wrote the prose story to the skeleton path, so check_fill_integrity.py compared a file with itself and passed, silently making an earlier verification vacuous. A check that cannot fail manufactures confidence. Give check_fill_integrity.py a self-test: assert the two inputs are not the same file, that the skeleton contains FILL markers, and that the filled story contains none. Apply the same reasoning to any proof comparing two generated files. applied scripts/check_fill_integrity.py rejects same-file inputs and a marker-less skeleton; both negative cases verified
    AL-017 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b3 docs The rule catalog has drifted from the code it documents, despite stating that rule changes require a revision to it: L2-13 is absent entirely, and RL-13's entry says the grade is "computed by textstat" when the implementation is deliberately vendored and textstat is not a dependency. _MIN_WORDS_FOR_FK (20) is also undocumented and changes which nodes get scored. Add the L2-13 row, correct RL-13, bump the catalog version, and add a test asserting every rule id the validator can emit appears in the catalog. applied validator-rules.md v1.3 adds L2-13, PL-23, PL-24 and a whole missing SR-1..SR-7 section, and corrects RL-13's textstat claim; tests/unit/test_rule_catalog_lockstep.py makes drift a build failure
    AL-018 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 tooling The budget-headroom report is what made ceiling-scale authoring tractable (nodes against the cell, endings and decisions against floors, word mean, depth against the cap, fastest finish against the arc floor, ending mix). It lives in a series-only script; check_skeleton.py prints pass or fail with no sense of proximity to any edge. Move the headroom report into check_skeleton.py, computed from the typed Storybook rather than a spec format, so the generation pipeline and the guardian editor get it too. applied scripts/check_skeleton.py --headroom reports nodes/depth/endings/decisions/words/arc-floor proximity from the typed document; 2 tests
    AL-019 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 product No reader outcome ever reaches an author. Completion, ReadingState.path and Rating all exist and StorybookVersion.skeleton_slug links a version to its skeleton, but Completion is read in five places and every one is a per-child read. Nobody can say which of book 3's 232 endings any reader has reached, or where readers stop. Designed: reader-path retention plus a de-identified engagement rollup, under S12's minimum-population constraint. accepted reader-path-engagement-design.md
    AL-020 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 product The offline client already transmits the full accumulated path on every save (frontend/src/offline/sync.ts::toPutPayload), and the server overwrites it (api/reading.py, row.path = list(body.path)). The route data we want for engagement analysis already arrives and is discarded, so retaining it needs no client change and no request-contract change. Append an immutable trail row on each reading-state write; see the design doc. accepted reader-path-engagement-design.md section 3
    AL-021 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 validator Split from AL-012 so the gate change is tracked separately from the book fix: the declared/derived read-time mismatch is undetectable today. Add the PL-series estimated_minutes advisory comparing declared against recompute_estimated_minutes, tolerance ~25%, advisory not blocking. applied PL-23 in validator/policy.py compares declared estimated_minutes against the derived fastest-finish clock; found read-time drift in 13 pre-existing books
    AL-022 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 product estimated_minutes is the fastest-finish clock, so a 746-node, 42,085-word gamebook whose whole-world clock is 3.2 hours advertises "14 min" to a child choosing a book. Technically correct, practically a broken promise. ADR-011 defines the whole-world clock; the schema surfaces one field. Derive both clocks, add estimated_minutes_whole_world, and show "about 15 minutes to finish, hours to explore" on the kid and guardian surfaces. Owner decision, not an implementation detail. open story-quality-lessons-2026-07.md finding B
    AL-023 2026-07-25 wyrmreach b1-b3 tooling The shipped client never sends choice_path, so the server-side engine replay that exists to stop a forged current_node/var_state/path is dormant (api/reading.py carries the #ASSUME admitting it). Any analytics derived from the client-supplied path is therefore unverified reader-reported data. Enable choice_path in toPutPayload and make the replay authoritative before trail data is used for anything beyond directional signal. Security and data-integrity relevance, not only analytics. open reader-path-engagement-design.md section 6
    AL-024 2026-07-25 adversarial review tooling Committing a 746-node skeleton falsified the stated derivation of a resource cap without breaking anything. api/schemas.py:38-52 derives VISIT_SET_MAX_LENGTH from "the largest currently-authored skeleton is the-ashfall-expedition.json with 505 nodes ... so 505 is the exact real ceiling", and tells the reader not to invent numbers. The largest is now 746. Not a live bug: visit_set resets per read-through (player/engine.ts::start) and shrinks on undo (back() replays from the start), and book 3's depth is 74, so a real read never approaches 505. Nothing tests the constant against the catalog, so the false basis is invisible. Update the derivation and the number to the real largest skeleton, and add a lockstep test asserting the constant matches the largest production_eligible skeleton in skeletons/, so the next oversized book fails a test instead of quietly invalidating a comment. Raising an audit-derived (F8) cap wants the audit owner's sign-off, so recommend rather than change unilaterally. open api/schemas.py:38-52, tests/unit/test_schemas.py
    AL-025 2026-07-25 adversarial review process An adversarial review is only as good as its ability to finish. Four review agents died on a session limit having delivered no findings, and the only value recovered came from two half-formed leads left in their reasoning traces. The reasoning trace, not the final report, was the durable artifact. For any delegated review, ask for findings incrementally (highest-value first) rather than one report at the end, so a truncated run still yields something. Recover partial work from subagents/agent-*.jsonl rather than assuming a failed agent produced nothing. applied this review; leads chased manually and closed
    AL-026 2026-07-25 adversarial review authoring-craft Breadth built from terminating leaves halves the typical read. Reproduced with player/engine.StoryEngine over 3,000 condition-aware random reads: book 1 (305 nodes) has a median read of 11 pages / 650 words; book 3 (746 nodes) has a median of 5 pages / 302 words of a 42,085-word book. Termination density is identical (22.3% vs 22.5% of choices land straight on an ending), so the extra 441 nodes went into width made of failure leaves, not into reader-visible journey. 7 endings sit within two taps of the start, 3 decision nodes have every choice end the book, and 3,000 random reads reached only 94 of 232 endings. This qualifies AL-009: hanging rooms off an act hub does control depth, but paying for breadth in terminating leaves makes the book feel smaller the bigger it gets. Author breadth as reconvergent detours, not terminal leaves: a room should more often cost vigor and rejoin than end the run. PL-17's endings floor actively pushes toward terminal leaves at gamebook breadth, so the floor and this lesson are in tension and the floor's shape should be revisited. applied 45 shallowest failure leaves converted to vigor-costing pass-throughs; median read 5 -> 20 pages, endings reached 39% -> 56%; gate clean, chain clean, endings exactly on the 187 floor
    AL-027 2026-07-25 adversarial review validator Nothing constrains the typical path length. PL-20 floors only the fastest satisfying path (37 nodes for this cell), which book 3 satisfies at 52, while the median actual read is 5 pages. A book can pass every scale rule and still hand most readers a 90-second encounter. Add a scale advisory on expected read length over the walk machinery validator/walk.py already has: warn when the median uniform-walk playthrough falls below a per-cell page floor, or when more than ~20% of a node's choices terminate. Roughly 20 lines on existing infrastructure, and it would have caught AL-026 before the fill pass. open validator/walk.py, validator/policy.py
    AL-028 2026-07-25 adversarial review product K6's "found N of M endings" replay motivator inverts at large M. At M=232 a finished read reports "1 of 232" (0.4%), and EndingsBadge silently drops its dot row above MAX_DOTS = 10, removing the only kid-legible part of the mechanic precisely for the books that most need replay motivation. Part of the denominator is also effectively unreachable: 3,000 random reads found 94 of 232. Stop showing a raw denominator above a threshold: milestone framing ("3 new endings found, lots more to go") with a tier ladder, or scope the denominator to a curated signature-endings subset. EndingsProgress and EndingsBadge must share the threshold so the ending screen and the shelf never disagree. open frontend/src/reader/EndingsProgress.tsx:60-65, frontend/src/library/EndingsBadge.tsx:19-40
    AL-029 2026-07-25 adversarial review product The reader progress bar is visit_set.length / story.nodes.length, so it measures corpus coverage, not route progress. A median book-3 read shows 1% and then snaps to 100% at the ending screen; the bigger the book the more broken the signal. The numeric label is already suppressed as untrustworthy, but the aria-label still announces "5 of 746 pages explored". Make in-read progress route-relative (path length against distance-to-nearest-ending from the current node, precomputable from the walk the validator already does), or show "Page 5" for large books. Corpus coverage belongs on the shelf card, not in the reader chrome. open frontend/src/reader/readerProgress.ts:7-11, ReaderChrome.tsx:88
    AL-030 2026-07-25 adversarial review product Every page turn synchronously replays the entire read from page 1: canGoBack calls back(), which DFS-replays the recorded path, and discards everything but a boolean. Compounding it, nodeIndex rebuilds a 746-entry Map on every engine entry point, so a depth-71 replay performs ~285 rebuilds. Measured O(depth x nodes): 26 ms at depth 70 on a container CPU, and a BACK press pays it three times, so a phone plausibly freezes 300ms+ on the one control built for children who mis-tap. Cost grows with the depth the book is designed to reward. Memoize the node index and intBounds per Storybook in a WeakMap (story identity is stable for the reader's lifetime, satisfying the existing #ASSUME); stop canGoBack recomputing an answer it throws away; short-circuit the common "no" case off the render path. open frontend/src/player/engine.ts:17-19,325-347, reader/Reader.tsx:216, player/machine.ts:99-111
    AL-031 2026-07-25 adversarial review product No response compression is configured anywhere in the stack (no GZipMiddleware in app.py, nothing in either compose file), so a story download ships 405 KB instead of 107 KB and the 3-book series costs 750 KB instead of 202 KB. Separately, list_library and /v1/reading-history select(StorybookVersion) whole-row including the blob JSONB for every book, decoding 768 KB of prose per shelf render to produce three small cards; the reading-history call fires on every ending-screen mount, which at a 5-page median read is constantly. Add GZipMiddleware(minimum_size=1024), a one-line ~4x win on the one step where a child waits with nothing to do. Separately, denormalise title, node_count, ending_count and ending_node_ids onto StorybookVersion at publish time so the listing paths never load prose. applied GZipMiddleware(minimum_size=1024) in app.py; the listing-path denormalisation half stays open under this id
    AL-032 2026-07-25 adversarial review product Reconnecting mid-read can teleport a child backwards to an earlier page and remount the reader, from this device's own queued past. persist() updates revisionRef only on saved, never on queued, so after offline reading the live save carries a stale base revision; the replay-on-reconnect advances the server revision, the next tap 409s, and the 409 handler adopts the server row unconditionally and bumps readerKey to remount. The existing #ASSUME deliberately accepts losing position to another device, but explicitly reasons about "another device advanced this story's row" and does not contemplate this case. A 74-choice offline read queues 74 sequential writes, so the 409 window is proportional to how much the child read offline. Traced end to end, not executed. Collapse the offline queue to the newest state per (profile, storybook) before replay so 74 round-trips become 1; advance revisionRef on queued; and guard the adopt branch so a current_row that is a strict prefix of the local path is recognised as this device's own stale past and re-saved rather than adopted. open frontend/src/reader/ReaderPage.tsx:296,304-305,317-337, offline/sync.ts:277-325, api/reading.py:363-371
    AL-033 2026-07-25 adversarial review validator Safety hole. _screen_all_nodes calls the bright-line classifiers once per node in a sequential loop guarded by if openai_key and openai_reason is None, so the FIRST ClassifierUnavailable disables that classifier for every remaining node, with no retry and no per-node coverage record. At 746 nodes Stage 0 makes 1,492 sequential third-party calls, which is exactly the shape that trips a Perspective ~1 QPS quota or an OpenAI per-minute limit; a 429 at node 50 leaves 93% of the book unscreened for sexual/minors, self-harm/instructions and illicit/violent. The resulting report is indistinguishable from a fully-screened clean one apart from one whole-story ADVISORY, and summary.hard_block/soft_flag are unaffected, so submit and approve both pass. Verified by reading the loop; the docstring states the no-retry behaviour outright. Track per-node classifier coverage and emit a FLAG, not an advisory, naming the unscreened node count and ids, so the story cannot reach in_review clean. Add bounded retry with backoff plus a concurrency limit so a rate limit is absorbed rather than abandoning the book. Make submit/approve refuse a report declaring incomplete Stage-0 coverage. applied moderation/classifiers.py: per-node coverage, bounded retry, circuit breaker, and a gating classifier_coverage_incomplete FLAG; 6 new tests
    AL-034 2026-07-25 adversarial review scale One import of this book is ~2,986 strictly sequential provider round trips (2 classifier calls plus 2 review calls per node, plus 2 whole-story stages), with no gather, semaphore, batching or call budget, all inside one Postgres transaction that also holds SELECT FOR UPDATE on the Storybook row. That is a 40 to 100 minute idle in transaction session against a pooler configured for transaction mode, and any single failure discards the entire run with no checkpoint or resume. Bound the per-node stages with a concurrency semaphore and a per-story call/time budget that records an explicit "moderation incomplete" finding when exceeded. Split the import: commit the draft first, then moderate in short transactions, persisting findings incrementally so a retry re-screens only unscreened nodes. approve already refuses a version with no moderation report, so a committed unmoderated draft is safe to leave behind. open moderation/stages.py:196-268, pipeline.py:98, generation/import_cli.py:92-119
    AL-035 2026-07-25 adversarial review scale run_gate is a measured 3.72 second synchronous CPU burn on this book and is called inline from three async def handlers with no to_thread, so it stalls the whole event loop: every passage edit, an admin rescreen sweep, and validate_version, which is guardian-reachable and therefore a cheap self-DoS on the entire API. Wrap all three in anyio.to_thread.run_sync, cache the gate report per (storybook_id, version) so validate_version returns the stored report, and rate-limit it. applied anyio.to_thread.run_sync around run_gate in api/node_edit.py, api/generation.py, moderation/rescreen.py
    AL-036 2026-07-25 adversarial review product ADR-005 makes a human's recorded approval the only path to a child, and at 746 nodes that surface cannot deliver it: the review view ships the whole blob (measured 444 KB), renders all 746 passages, 232 ending badges and 1,030 choice rows unvirtualized in one DOM, and offers no pagination, no per-node reviewed state, no resume, and one Approve button at the bottom. A reviewer cannot read 42,085 words in one page load, so the recorded approval attests to far less than it claims. The queue and the guardian browse listing separately load every book's full blob to compute a title and a count. Add node-range pagination and stop echoing the whole blob; virtualize the read-through; persist per-node review progress keyed by (storybook_id, version, node_id, reviewer) so the approval record can state what fraction was actually read. Project listings in SQL or denormalise title/ending_count/flagged_count at publish time. open api/review_surface.py:100-117, api/approval.py:222-285, frontend/src/admin/ReviewDetailPage.tsx:534-565
    AL-037 2026-07-25 adversarial review tooling Import silently rewrites the authored series block. embed_series_block replaces the declared series_id, book_index, is_final and series_entry_node with DB-derived values, and nothing anywhere compares the declared book_index to the assigned one, so import order becomes chain order. carries_state is likewise taken from the Series row, whose value is derived from that row's own age band, so a mismatched row silently rewrites all three 16+ books to carries_state: false; SR-6 and SR-7 both still pass, and the campaign degrades to three episodic books with no error at import, approval, or read. --job (the only route that stamps skeleton_slug, runs the Stage 1 fidelity gate, and enables the leaf-diversity guard) and --series-id (the only route that links a series) are mutually exclusive, so no combination produces a correct Tier-2 series. Compare declared against assigned in embed_series_block and raise on mismatch rather than overwrite; preserve declared is_final; check Series.age_band and Series.family_id. Move series linkage into import_filled_story so both routes carry it, and make --job --series-id a hard error instead of a silent ignore. open generation/series_link.py:131-208, generation/import_cli.py:52-119
    AL-038 2026-07-25 adversarial review authoring-craft Narrowing a carried range erases reader outcomes, which qualifies AL-008. Verified from the specs: book 2 declares renown [0,5], book 3 declares [3,5], and startContinuation clamps carried ints into the receiving book's bounds, so every reader who finishes book 2 with renown 0, 1 or 2 arrives at book 3 indistinguishable from a maximum-reputation reader. My AL-008 justification ("a company that shut two doors cannot become unknown") was wrong on book 2's own terms. Two more in the same mechanism: book 2's deep_charts and oath_sworn are dropped without trace because book 3 does not declare them, and book 3 declares second_iron initial true while book 2 earns it from false, so a reader who finishes book 2 without it carries false and reads 746 nodes under a premise the prose contradicts. All of it happens in the client with no server-side cross-check, and no SR rule compares variable names, types or bounds across a chain. Author side DONE (renown widened to [0,5]); the rest is not author-fixable. Validator side: add an SR rule that every variable declared in book N appears in book N+1 with the same type and a containing range, error on narrowing, warn on dropped. Make startContinuation record a per-variable carry audit. And gate the continuation offer on a satisfying ending: get_series_next offers book 3 after ANY book-2 ending, which is what lets a reader carry second_iron: false into a book whose premise is two irons. applied SR-8 in validator/series.py (ERROR on a narrowed range or changed type, WARNING on a dropped variable), catalogued and tested; it flags this chain's 2 dropped variables. Still open: the read-time carry audit and gating the continuation offer on a satisfying ending
    AL-039 2026-07-25 adversarial review scale Two moderation stages are structurally impossible at this size and fail open. The soft-gate auto-repair must return the entire revised story but is capped at 32k output tokens against a ~101k-token blob, so it always truncates, always fails to parse, and always returns None after burning a ~101k-token call; on the Ollama leg it can trip a byte ceiling and raise, discarding the whole import. The Stage 1 fidelity gate builds a single ~120k-token prompt (746 beats plus 1,030 label pairs) and asks for a 512-token verdict, with every parse failure returning "pass"; it also embeds untrusted generated prose raw, without the <untrusted_passage> wrapper and instruction-hierarchy suffix that moderation/stages.py documents as #CRITICAL: security. Make both per-node or per-batch with a required verdict per batch, treating a missing verdict as a violation rather than a pass. Skip repair with a recorded finding when the blob exceeds the output budget. Wrap fidelity prose in _sanitize_delimited and append the untrusted suffix, matching stages.py. open moderation/repair.py:93-108, moderation/fidelity_review.py:226-261

    AL-054 2026-07-28 PR #435 skeleton-promotion repair tooling The skeleton-promotion gate had never once gone green, and the cause was a policy gap rather than a bug: check_skeleton rejects any shell declaring metadata.production_eligible: false unless passed --allow-mvp, while the CI job feeds it the raw changed-file list of any PR touching skeletons/**.     AL-056 2026-07-28 ADR-023 Stage A sentinel-survival measurement tooling Running scripts/measure_sentinel_survival.py from a fresh worktree failed through three sequential environment layers before producing data: (1) Settings in core/config.py declares no env_file, so credentials in .env are invisible unless the process environment carries them (VS Code injects them in the main tree; a worktree shell does not); (2) OLLAMA_CA_BUNDLE=certs/homelab-ca.pem is cwd-relative, so the same env fails in any directory without a certs/ symlink; (3) the Anthropic leg then died on an account-billing 400 that only a one-trial probe surfaced cheaply. Each layer cost a full script start to discover because nothing validates the environment before the paid loop begins. Document the worktree recipe (source .env with set -a, symlink certs/) in the script docstring. General rule: an instrument that spends money must verify its environment with one cheap probe per dependency layer before entering the paid loop; per-layer failure discovery multiplies startup cost by the number of layers. open Execution plan Task A1 notes updated (this branch); script change not yet made
    AL-057 2026-07-28 ADR-023 Stage A sentinel-survival measurement tooling Measured first-attempt sentinel survival through the production fill route (openrouter:anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5, 30 stories, 4 slots each, fill_bound.md "Verbatim tokens" instruction present): 1/30 clean (3.3%). Dropped dominates by 8x: the model writes prose without the token despite an explicit copy-verbatim instruction, so this is not a delimiter or wording defect to iterate; the model treats an inline token as guidance, not payload. A one-retry policy at this rate adds ~97% to fill spend. Do not build features that depend on an LLM preserving inline markers through a creative rewrite; the design plan's G1 gate correctly fired STOP, and the deterministic post-fill re-insertion fallback (match the generic default word in filled prose, re-wrap it server-side) becomes the primary design for ADR-023 Stage B+.     AL-058 2026-07-28 PR #449 review tooling SENTINEL_RE matches only well-formed {~SLOT:Word~} tokens, so it fails OPEN on a bisected one: truncating before stripping leaves {~HERO:Expl, which no later strip can ever match. The invariant is therefore strip-BEFORE-truncate ordering, not strip-presence, and ordering is invisible to any test whose fixture is shorter than the cut. PR #449 fixed this ordering in covers/prompt.py but left the identical shape unfixed in three sibling call sites (story_requests/anchoring.py, moderation/fidelity_review.py, flywheel/reguide_draft.py), two of which feed LLM prompts, so a fragment would have taught the next book's model that {~HERO:Expl is ordinary story text. Two changes. (1) When fixing an ordering bug, grep the repo for every other [:N] slice applied to blob-derived text rather than fixing the reported site alone. (2) Every regression test for this class must place the sentinel so the cut bisects it, AND assert that the pre-fix ordering leaked on the same input (assert "{~" in strip_sentinels(body[:N])), so the test fails loudly if a constant change later moves the cut and stops exercising the bug. applied tests/unit/test_anchoring.py::test_sentinel_straddling_the_title_cut_is_stripped_before_truncation, tests/unit/test_flywheel_reguide_draft.py::test_story_context_sentinel_is_stripped_before_the_600_char_cut, tests/unit/test_fidelity_review.py::test_sentinel_bearing_beats_and_body_never_reach_the_review_prompt
    AL-059 2026-07-29 ADR-023 re-insertion prototype measurement tooling Deterministic strip-all-then-reinsert scored only 10% story-clean against the strict every-expected-node multiset, but the per-slot decomposition showed the metric was wrong, not the transform: named third-party slots (OPERATOR, LISTENER, HUB, FOUNDER) re-insert at 94-99%, while HERO sits at 42% node coverage with zero prose occurrences in 11/30 stories, because the corpus is second-person and the protagonist is the addressee, named only in vocative dialogue. The skeleton's per-node expectation model prescribes a name where the narrative voice structurally cannot put one; no model quality or prompt wording can satisfy it. A further 154 of 1,026 misses were sentence-initial capitalization or inflected forms of lowercase descriptive values (the pup, the grown-up). Derive the at-rest sentinel expectation from what deterministic re-insertion actually finds in the prose (round-trip-verified), instead of prescribing per-node presence from beats; keep name coverage as a soft quality floor with the P8 dedication overlay guaranteeing one personalized surface. Make the matcher sentence-start case-aware and inflection-aware for lowercase multi-word values. General rule: when a measured failure clusters on one slot or structural position, suspect the expectation model before the generator. applied Commits 3fbf0807 and a63c06e9 (derive-not-prescribe re-insertion promoted into storybook/ and wired into the fill path; manifest derived from what re-insertion found, round-trip-verified) and 8916c2db (sentence-start case-aware matching); the inflection-aware half was deliberately not built after AL-060 proved the plural bucket was a classification artifact (0 real occurrences); dedication overlay made mandatory (Task C5) per the Stage R re-plan
    AL-060 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage R matcher hardening (Task R1) tooling The "72 inflected-form misses" bucket in the AL-059 analysis was an artifact of the crude prefix heuristic used to classify misses: exact re-measurement with a dedicated plural counter found 0 bare-plural occurrences across the whole 30-story corpus, because the heuristic had been counting possessives, which the word-boundary matcher already wraps correctly (Explorer's becomes {~HERO:Explorer~}'s). Only the sentence-start capitalization bucket was real (76 of 1,027 misses recovered; 120 widened occurrences wrapped). Building the speculative plural matcher first would have been dead code justified by a phantom number. Before implementing a matcher or repair policy sized from a bucketed failure analysis, re-derive the bucket with an exact counter for the specific phenomenon; a classification heuristic that merely prefixes or fuzzy-matches will conflate adjacent phenomena and inflate the case for the wrong fix. Ship the counter as a permanent report field so the decision stays checkable on future corpora. applied Commit 8916c2d (plural_occurrences report field, sentence-start widening, possessive test); design plan section 3.4 "MATCHER HARDENING RE-MEASURED 2026-07-29" block
    AL-061 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage R confirmatory run (Task R4) tooling Per-slot re-insertion coverage swung materially between two 30-story runs of the same pipeline (HERO 42.4% then 26.8%, FOUNDER 94.6% then 78.6%, CHAPERONE 63.9% then 30.6%), while the transform-correctness metric (verify_manifest) held at exactly 100% on both. Coverage is a property of each LLM fill, not of the code, so a single-run coverage number quoted as a stable property will mislead the next decision that cites it; only invariants of the deterministic transform are stable enough to gate on. When a measurement mixes model-behavior metrics with code-correctness metrics, report the model-behavior ones as cross-run ranges and gate only on the code-correctness ones; exit records and plans must quote coverage as a range with run count, never a point estimate. applied Execution plan Task R4 "CONFIRMED 2026-07-29" block; design plan section 3.4 "G1-R CONFIRMED 2026-07-29" bullet (both state ranges, not points)
    AL-062 2026-07-29 ADR-023 vocative nudge experiment (Stage R optional item) tooling A one-sentence fill-prompt nudge meant to raise HERO name coverage instead cut it 5x (26.8-42.4% baseline to 4.9%), because the sentence ended with an explanatory rationale clause ("second-person narration alone never surfaces the name") that the model executed as an instruction: it suppressed narration mentions and confined the name to dialogue it then rarely wrote. The A/B measurement caught the regression before the edit was ever committed. Fill-prompt edits must be imperative-only and additive (one behavior per sentence, no trailing rationale clauses, phrased "in addition to" existing behavior, never "instead of" framing or negative examples), and every prompt-template change that targets a measured metric must be A/B-measured with the saved-fills harness before it is committed. applied Design plan section 3.4 "VOCATIVE NUDGE MEASURED AND REJECTED 2026-07-29" block; execution plan Stage R optional bullet marked rejected; template edit reverted uncommitted
    AL-063 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage R+B PR review (PR #466) tooling A drift gate that declares one file authoritative but checks only a downstream copy of it leaves the authoritative file unguarded, and it drifts. scripts/check_er_diagram.py called er-diagram.puml "the authoritative source" in its own header while comparing only the two Mermaid copies against the ORM; the .puml had silently accumulated three missing columns (child_profile.reduce_motion, device_grant.expires_at, story_request.interpretation) that predate this branch and that a green gate reported as no drift.     AL-064 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage R+B PR review (PR #466) process Restating a measurement in prose is a claim about evidence, not evidence. Every Stage R number in the design and execution plans cited a run directory under results/sentinel-survival/, which .gitignore excludes, so a reader could see the figures but had no artifact to check them against and no way to tell a transcription slip from a real result.     AL-065 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage R+B PR review (PR #466) tooling A test that fails when you break the thing it guards is not yet proven useful; it also has to fail for the RIGHT reason. The new _prefetch_personalizable_slots empty-sweep test used a bare AsyncMock session, so deleting the guard killed the helper on TypeError: 'coroutine' object is not iterable several lines before the assertion ran. The break-it check went red, which looks like proof, but the red said nothing about the guard: any later refactor that changed the mock plumbing would have flipped it green while the defect stayed open. Separately, a scoped -k filter used to measure coverage excluded the very tests that covered the module, producing a confident "80%, 18 uncovered new lines" reading for a file that is actually at 100%. Extend the non-vacuity check from "does it go red" to "does the failure message name the break". When it does not, wire the double well enough that the code under test runs to completion and fails on the real assertion. For coverage measurement, never conclude a gap from a -k-filtered run: filters silently exclude covering tests, so scope by test FILE (or intersect missed lines against the diff's changed lines) before believing a number. applied Commit fe4b53c5, tests/unit/test_rescreen_unit.py::test_prefetch_personalizable_slots_never_queries_for_an_empty_sweep (docstring records the failure-mode reasoning); measurement correction recorded in the same commit message
    AL-066 2026-07-29 ADR-023 Stage C client resolution (Task C0) tooling The values payload was keyed by slot type (protagonist_first_name) while prose sentinels carry the slot id ({~HERO:...~}), and nothing in any response joined the two, so the client resolver specified in two plan documents was unimplementable as designed.     AL-080 2026-08-05 Three blind deep-research reports reconciled against JHM 2019; validator gap analysis tooling The policy gate measured story shape on one axis only. PL-17 counts decision and ending nodes anywhere in the graph (breadth); nothing measured what a reader meets walking a path (depth). PL-20 came closest but counts NODES on the shortest satisfying path, not decisions along it, so a story could satisfy every floor while the reader walked a corridor. The sweep proved the gap was live, not theoretical: the-tide-pool-rescue opens with five consecutive single-choice nodes (385 words) before a 5-8 reader gets any agency, and eight prose skeletons offer a choice every 1.1-1.9 nodes against a measured corpus mean of 3.28. Both shapes passed every rule the gate had.     AL-081 2026-08-05 PL-25 calibration against JHM 2019 Table 4 process The first draft of the PL-25 band table set the 3-5 ceiling at 3 and the 5-8 ceiling at 4, against a source corpus whose MEDIAN pages-to-first-decision is 4 and whose range runs to 8.25. Those thresholds would have made median-paced storytelling a blocking ERROR, and they blocked three skeletons on first run. The values were invented for the bands the research does not cover, then applied more strictly than the bands it does cover, which is backwards: the least-evidenced thresholds were the tightest. A second instance survived to the test-writing step, where the 8-11 ceiling of 8 still sat under the corpus maximum of 8.25. When a threshold is derived from a measured corpus, assert the calibration invariants as tests rather than trusting the table: no threshold may block the corpus median, and any band the corpus covers must admit its full measured range. Where a band is outside the corpus, say so in the table comment and set the threshold looser than the measured bands, not tighter. applied test_band_profile.py::test_first_decision_ceiling_never_below_the_corpus_median and ::test_first_decision_measured_bands_span_the_corpus_range, both of which failed against the first draft
    AL-082 2026-08-05 Validator gap analysis, validator/band_profile.py tooling BandProfile.reconvergence_ceiling is declared on the frozen dataclass, defaults to None, is set on no band, and is read only by mutation/operators.py (the offline catalog tool). No validator rule consults it, so the request path has no reconvergence constraint at all while the field's presence on the profile implies one. This is the same shape as an inert rule: a reader auditing BandProfile sees a reconvergence policy that does not exist. Either wire a reconvergence rule into validate_policy and populate the per-band values, or move the field out of BandProfile into the mutation layer that actually reads it. Deciding between them needs an owner ruling on whether reconvergence gets a floor as well as a ceiling; Fridkin and Hurry (2025, DOI 10.1007/s12144-025-07685-3) found perceived choice raised comprehension at d=0.52 with the same underlying text, which argues reconvergent structures are defensible on evidence rather than only on budget. open

    AL-084 2026-08-05 PL-26 calibration review during the first full-library sweep validator PL-26 shipped as a symmetric window and every one of its 8 library hits was on the LOW side, the opposite of the corridor shape the rule was built to catch. The cause is a selection effect: density was measured along a SHORTEST path, which is biased toward decision nodes (out-degree >= 2 makes a node likelier to sit on a fast route), while the JHM 3.28 anchor it was compared against was measured corpus-wide. Comparing them on the low side compares two different quantities, structurally the same category error as judging a gamebook by a prose window, which the same rule had already been fixed for once. A uniform sign across every hit is the tell: real defects scatter, artifacts do not. Low bound removed; PL-26 is a ceiling only and now fires on nothing in the library, which is correct for a guard aimed at generated stories rather than hand-authored ones. If a 'choice gauntlet' guard is wanted, it must measure WHOLE-GRAPH density, not path density. Generalised check: before trusting a new threshold's hits, look at whether they share a sign, and confirm the measurement scope matches the anchor's scope. applied UW-C28, src/cyo_adventure/validator/band_profile.py _NODES_PER_DECISION_CEILING
    AL-085 2026-08-05 PL-25 severity review after the first full-library sweep validator PL-25's ceiling shipped as a blocking ERROR and immediately unpublished a committed, production-eligible skeleton on a ONE-NODE margin (depth 6 against a ceiling of 5), breaking 6 tests across 4 files. The gate's ERROR tier means unpublishable, and the house precedent was already there to read: PL-20 blocks on its floor (too short to be a story) and only warns on its ceiling (a long arc). A new craft rule applied retroactively to an existing library should not block on a margin narrower than its own calibration confidence. PL-25 regraded to two tiers: WARNING past the band ceiling, ERROR only past ARC_CEILING_MULTIPLE x ceiling, reusing the measured constant rather than inventing a margin.     AL-088 2026-08-06 Depth-headroom measurement across all 61 skeletons while fixing AL-087 validator The MVP fallback depth cap is not calibrated to the MVP node envelope it governs. mvp_node_budget pairs a band-independent 8..45 node range with the band's legacy max_depth, so a 45-node MVP story in the 3-5 band is capped at depth 4 while ADR-011's own 3-5 cells allow 15 (10-23 nodes) and 18 (23-45 nodes) for the same sizes.     AL-091 2026-08-06 Test-fixture fallout across the pipeline suites tooling Adding one node to the mock story broke tests that had nothing to do with pacing, and every break was a literal standing in for a derived value: assert len(book.nodes) == 7 (measuring the fixture, not the round-trip invariant it names), seven blob['nodes'][0] lookups that silently retargeted to a different node, and a review_batch_size=3 chosen so 7 nodes would split 3+3+1 and exercise the single-node path. That last is the sharpest: the remainder became 2, the single-node path stopped being exercised entirely, and only an unrelated count assertion made it visible. A rule change is a fixture change, and a fixture coupled by literals fails far from its cause. Each literal replaced by the derived value: compare against len(_CANNED_STORY['nodes']), look nodes up by id through a _node() helper, and set the batch size to _NODE_COUNT - 1 so the remainder is one BY CONSTRUCTION. Generalised check: when a test depends on an arithmetic relationship with a fixture (divides evenly, leaves a remainder of one, has N nodes), derive the constant from the fixture instead of writing the number, or the relationship silently stops holding while the test stays green. applied tests/unit/test_moderation_pipeline.py _PARTIAL_CHUNK_BATCH_SIZE; tests/unit/test_node_edit.py _node; tests/unit/test_worker.py node-count assertion
    AL-092 2026-08-06 Derived-artifact sweep after the 20-skeleton cold-open split process Changing 20 skeletons put 26 committed documents derived from them under the escalated PL-25 floor, and they surfaced one test failure at a time over several runs because only SOME derived artifacts are gated. Sweeping the whole derived tree through run_gate directly found the rest at once, and re-rendering it through the real producer found something the gate could not: of 45 WS2 bound theme-swaps, only 14 still reproduce from their own skeleton plus binding. 19 had drifted in nodes unrelated to any of this work, and 2 can no longer be rendered AT ALL because their skeleton gained slots (COMPANION_NAME, ROUTE_C_KEEPER/ROUTE_D_KEEPER) the committed binding cannot fill. That tree had been silently desynchronized from the catalog long before this change. Two fills were also missed on the first pass for a second reason: they sit in a strict-xfail quarantine, so a sweep keyed on a clean gate result skips them. Fixed only what is live: the gate-tested pilot parameterized skeleton, the two pilot fills (registered CATALOG_ENTRIES, and catalog import gates every entry), and the diversity panel corpus, re-baselined via --update-baseline because R5 is a deliberate fixture-tamper detector; every diversity delta landed in the third or fourth decimal, so the detector kept its calibration. out/ws2/ was left frozen: re-rendering it would re-baseline a completed workstream's evidence against a catalog that has moved on, and 2 of them cannot be re-rendered without authoring new binding entries. Generalised check: reproducing a derived artifact through its real producer is a STRONGER test than gating it, because it catches drift in everything the gate does not measure; run it before deciding whether an evidence tree is stale or frozen. open UW-C31
    AL-093 2026-08-06 Regenerating skeleton diagrams rewrites 5 SVGs whose source did not change tooling scripts/render_skeleton_diagrams.py regenerates every diagram, not only the stale ones, and its SVG step re-measures text with whatever fonts the running machine has. 21 .puml files changed because their skeletons gained an establishing node; 26 .svg files changed, and the extra 5 differed only in geometry (textLength 69.6035 -> 61.0547, canvas 1507x1058 -> 1360x1062) with no structural or label delta. --check compares committed .puml content only and inspects SVGs solely for a PlantUML error card, so byte drift in an SVG is invisible to the freshness gate in both directions: it never fails on it, and it never warns that a content commit is carrying it. Left alone, 5 files of pure environment noise would have shipped inside a semantic change, and the next author would have had to re-derive that they meant nothing. Proposed change: teach the renderer a --only-stale mode that skips rendering when the .puml it would render is byte-identical to the committed one, so the noise is never produced rather than produced and reverted. Generalised check: when a generator emits two artifacts and the gate covers only one, assume the ungated one carries environment state, and diff it for semantic content before deciding it belongs in the commit. open UW-C31
    AL-094 2026-08-06 PR #635 review, the PL-26 corridor guard validator One computed shortest path was shared by two rules, and the sharing was documented as the safety property: "PL-20 and PL-26 both read this one result, so they can never disagree about which walk is the fastest finish". That reasoning WAS the defect. PL-20 reads only the path LENGTH, identical across every minimum-length path, so any tie-break is sound for it. PL-26 reads the DECISION COUNT along the path, which is not invariant, so _shortest_path_to's tie-break (sorted(graph.successors(...)), i.e. node-id alphabetical order) silently became part of the rule's semantics: renaming two routes flipped the verdict from 7.0 nodes per decision (WARNING) to 3.5 and SILENT, with zero structural change. Determinism was mistaken for correctness; it guarantees the same arbitrary answer every run, not the right one. Blast radius measured by sweeping the catalog: 19 of 58 PL-26-eligible skeletons had their density mismeasured, a third of the corpus, and 2 were under-reported across the ceiling. _fewest_decision_shortest_path, a layered dynamic program over the shortest-path DAG (BFS levels, then one forward sweep where edge u->v is in the DAG exactly when level[v] == level[u]+1), returning the minimum-decision path among the minimum-length ones. Worst case rather than canonical case, because PL-26 is a CEILING and must fire when ANY equally fast walk is a corridor. O(V+E), deliberately not enumeration: shortest-path count is exponential (a chain of parallel two-way detours doubles it per link) and this runs in the request path. _decision_node_ids extracted so PL-25, PL-26 and the search cannot drift on what a decision is. Generalised check: when two rules share one derived artifact, ask which PROPERTIES of it each reads. Sharing is safe only while every reader depends on an INVARIANT of the artifact; the moment one reader depends on a property that varies among equally valid artifacts, the tie-break has become that rule's semantics and must be chosen by the rule, not inherited from the search. State the invariant in the docstring instead of citing the sharing. applied validator/policy.py _fewest_decision_shortest_path / _decision_node_ids / _breadth_first_levels; verified against a brute-force nx.all_simple_paths oracle over 2,614 random digraphs, 0 mismatches on walk validity, endpoints, minimum length, and minimum decisions
    AL-095 2026-08-06 PR #635 review, the band_profile PL-25 comment validator The block comment that declares itself the single source of truth for the PL-25 window documented BOTH tiers backwards: "Below the floor is a WARNING, not an ERROR" and "Above the ceiling is an ERROR", while the code does the exact opposite and validator-rules.md had it right. The comment sits directly above the table it describes, so it is what an author actually reads, and it carried an #ASSUME: data-integrity marker asserting its own authority. Nothing compares a comment's severity claim against the code that consumes the table, so the two-place split (comment near the data, catalog far from it) diverged with every gate green. Generalised check: a comment asserting a rule's SEVERITY is a testable claim living in an untested medium. When a rule grades in tiers, name the consuming function rather than restating its verdicts, so there is one statement of the tiering instead of two that can disagree. applied validator/band_profile.py _FIRST_DECISION_DEPTH comment; every #VERIFY test name it now cites confirmed to exist

    AL-098 2026-08-06 PR #635 review, the repo-wide ruff format gate tooling ruff format --check . fails on origin/main, and has for some time, on a Python fence inside docs/planning/story-personalization-stage-c-implementation-plan.md: ruff formats Python inside markdown code fences, so a planning document is a formattable artifact. Nothing reports it, because no gate runs the formatter over the WHOLE tree. The pre-commit ruff-format hook sees only STAGED files, so a doc-only commit never presents the fence to it, and Python CI is delegated to the org-level reusable workflow, which scopes its format check to the code directories. The frontend was assumed to have closed the identical gap already; it has not, and its gap is larger (AL-100). The cost is asymmetric: the command CONTRIBUTING tells a contributor to run (uv run ruff format .) is broader than any gate, so the first person to run it picks up an unrelated file in their diff and has to work out whether it is theirs. Added a format-tree job running uv tool run --no-build ruff@0.16.0 format --check ., wired into ci-gate with NO skip allowance. The first draft of this row claimed the frontend had already closed the identical hole; checking that claim falsified it and produced AL-100, so this job is the FIRST tree-wide format gate in the repo, not a mirror of an existing one. The offending fence was NOT reformatted: it is a fragment shown at its real indentation inside a function body, and ruff's formatting of it dedents to column 0 and collapses the await call, turning in-function code into what reads as top-level code (verified by running the formatter on the fence in isolation). Retagged python to text instead, with prose stating the indentation is deliberate, so the fence is honestly declared an excerpt rather than silently mangled. Placement was moved out of the contract job a first pass had put it in, for two reasons: a format failure surfacing under the name "OpenAPI Contract Drift" makes a required check report something other than what it checked, and contract skips on merge_group and on release PRs, which would have narrowed the gate to a subset of runs. Generalised check: when a repo holds two language toolchains, a gap closed for one is a LIVE gap for the other until proven otherwise, and the thing to compare is SCOPE: if the gate runs over staged files or over code directories while the documented command runs over the tree, the difference is exactly the set of files that can drift with every gate green.     AL-101 2026-08-06 ADR-025 implementation plan Task 3 fix review (branch feat/persistent-characters) tooling import_catalog._needs_legacy_normalization carried its own independent copy of the schema-version acceptance rule instead of delegating to Storybook._check_schema_version/is_supported_schema_version, so the two enforcement points could silently disagree: a same-major document at an unsupported minor (or already past the accepted range) was misclassified as legacy, rewritten by _normalize_legacy_fill, and stamped back to "2.0" rather than rejected. Under ADR-025's accepted-range rule, a future minor such as 2.1, or a 3.0 document, would have been silently normalized and admitted instead of parsed and rejected. This is a gate letting a defect through by rewriting the evidence it should have refused, and the contradiction survived a first plan self-review. The pre-fix check itself was a literal != "2.0" comparison, not a same-major-only comparison, so it was equally broken for a higher major (for example 3.0) as for a future minor; describing the defect as "same-major unsupported minor" understated its own scope, and that same understatement is what scoped the Task 3 fix too narrowly, leaving the higher-major and topology-absent branches unaddressed until a later final-review pass (C1) caught them. Gave _needs_legacy_normalization its own explicitly narrower predicate (parse_schema_version directly, not is_supported_schema_version), so it answers only "is this pre-SCHEMA_MAJOR"; a same-major, out-of-range document now falls through to run_gate to be rejected loudly instead of silently normalized. General rule: when two enforcement points check the same version or format contract, either share one predicate or make each predicate's narrower scope explicit and tested, so a duplicate copy cannot silently disagree with the source of truth; and when describing a defect's scope, state the actual failing comparison (here, a literal string inequality with no major-awareness at all) rather than a narrower category that happens to cover the case in hand, since the narrower description can itself under-scope the fix. applied Fix in PR #636 (src/cyo_adventure/generation/import_catalog.py::_needs_legacy_normalization), branch feat/persistent-characters: originally commits 42439204/0b32ddd6, widened and completed by the C1 final-review fix in db6de7db, which covered the higher-major and topology-absent quadrants the original fix left open.     AL-104 2026-08-06 L1-8 field-minor floor review fix wave (branch feat/persistent-characters), established by mutating gate.py and re-running the L1-8 tests validator validator/gate.py::run_gate's blocked prefix tuple ("L1", "L2", "PL") is unreachable for Layer 1: removing "L1" from the tuple and re-running the L1-8 suite left every test passing, because run_gate early-returns blocked=True whenever l1_report.ok is False (any Layer-1 ERROR of any rule id), long before execution reaches the prefix-tuple computation. So for every Layer-1 rule, that tuple entry is dead code, and a test asserting result.blocked is True on a Layer-1 ERROR proves the outcome but not the mechanism. The contrast is the useful half: there is no equivalent short-circuit after the parse, so a rule running after Layer 2 or Policy reaches the final blocked = any(...) computation and depends entirely on its prefix being in that tuple. Add a test that pins the mechanism, not just the outcome, for Layer 1: assert directly that a Layer-1 ERROR short-circuits before the prefix tuple is ever consulted (for example, by asserting on the early-return path itself), or restructure so Layer-1 blocking is computed through the same prefix-tuple path as Layer 2/PL, removing the dead special case. This is a finding from a fix-wave review, not a shipped change; do not change gate.py to close it without its own scoped review. open src/cyo_adventure/validator/gate.py:113-120 (early return) and :160-164 (prefix-tuple computation); no PR yet, tracked by UW-C37 in unscheduled-work-register.md
    AL-105 2026-08-06 Task-5 CH-rule review fix wave (branch feat/persistent-characters), Finding 0 from a controller-supplied review (not in the review file itself) validator The task-5 design spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-05-persistent-reader-characters-design.md section 5.1) and the shipped CH-6 both stated only the opt-out half: "a book that does not declare accepts_character may not declare a variable whose name is in the canonical vocabulary". Neither the spec nor the code quantified over the opt-in side: a book that opts in and covers only some canonical names in its envelope could still declare a second canonical-named variable outside that envelope, pass CH-1 cleanly (CH-1 only ever walks envelope -> variable, never the converse), and be seeded by G3's name-match carry over states this book's own Layer 2 walk never proved for that variable. This is a spec gap, not an implementation gap: the shipped code faithfully implemented the spec's narrow statement, and the spec itself needed the wider one. Broadened CH-6's statement in both the spec and docs/planning/validator-rules.md to the full bidirectional form: a canonical name may be declared only by a book that opted in AND covered it in the envelope. Catalog scan (grep across skeletons/ and tests/fixtures/) confirms zero existing stories declare a canonical name outside an opted-in envelope, so the broadened check introduces no regression against the committed catalog.     AL-107 2026-08-06 Task 6 implementation (CH-3b per-state regression signature, branch feat/persistent-characters) validator The task-6 brief specified CH-3b's baseline-diff signature as the literal tuple rule_id \| node_id \| choice_id. None of L2-9 (dead end), L2-10 (trapped), or L2-14 (all-fatal decision) ever populate choice_id on their findings, so that signature collapses every finding for a given rule on a given node to one bucket regardless of which branch or variable state produced it. A falsification test (two structurally distinct dead-branch defects on the same node, reachable only in different envelope states) proved the brief's literal signature could not distinguish them: weakening the shipped signature back to the brief's exact form made test_ch3b_distinguishes_two_dead_branches_on_one_node fail immediately, then restoring it made the suite pass again. The fix extends the signature with the finding's message (which embeds the differing var_state for these three rule ids), deliberately diverging from series.py::_l2_error_signatures's existing signature helper, whose own docstring explains the opposite choice is correct for its own caller (SR-9 continuation-entry diffing, where message text is expected to vary harmlessly run to run). A task-6-review.md correction on the residual this widened signature leaves behind: an earlier draft of this record described the risk as a coincidental message collision suppressing an unrelated finding, but widening a signature can only narrow which findings match, so it can only suppress less than the brief's literal three-part key, never more; a coincidental collision was never the direction this change could fail in. The real residual is the opposite kind of noise: because the signature embeds the raising state's own values, a baseline defect is suppressed only for the state that exactly reproduces it, so a structurally identical defect at a different entry state re-reports rather than staying suppressed (harmless, since any such ERROR already blocks the gate through the "L2" prefix, but worth naming correctly). When a brief specifies a de-duplication or diff signature as a literal field tuple, verify it against the actual fields the compared findings populate before implementing it verbatim, and prove the choice by falsification (deliberately weaken the signature, confirm a test that should fail does fail, then restore it) rather than by inspection alone. Where two signature helpers in the same codebase make opposite choices about including free-text message in a signature, document why in both docstrings so neither reads as an oversight of the other. open src/cyo_adventure/validator/character.py::_signature (contrasted explicitly against src/cyo_adventure/validator/series.py::_l2_error_signatures in its own docstring); falsification recorded in this session's verification log; commit in this branch's Task 6 series, feat/persistent-characters. Unmerged as of 2026-08-06, so the status stays open rather than applied until the squash SHA lands; tracked by UW-C40 in unscheduled-work-register.md

    AL-109 2026-08-06 Task 7 review fix (CH-8 arity Critical, branch feat/persistent-characters) validator CH-8's original implementation computed arity = span.max - span.min from the accepts_character envelope's own declared bounds, which are schema-valid, attacker-influenced values from a generation-pipeline artifact: nothing upstream ties either bound to the canonical archetype count (CH-1 checks only the variable's type against the vocabulary; CH-2 checks only that the envelope equals the declared variable, never that the declared variable equals the canonical 0..6). A book could declare both its archetype variable and its envelope span as 1..6, schema-valid and passing CH-1/CH-2 cleanly, while an in-story build node still set any of the same six real archetype values a full 0..6 declaration would allow; reading the narrower span computed arity 5 instead of 6, moving the rule's own threshold from 16,666 to 20,000 configurations and letting a real 19,236-config book (the-flooded-quarter) through with zero findings from any rule, with run_gate returning blocked=False. The general lesson: a threshold derived from a document-controlled value is not a guard, even when that value is schema-valid and passes every check written so far; validating a value's shape (min <= max, correct type) is not the same as validating that it means what the rule's arithmetic assumes it means. Derive arity from the canonical vocabulary (len(ARCHETYPE_ROSTER), a module-level Final tuple the caller cannot influence) rather than from the declared span, and add a regression test that reproduces the exact bypass (a 1..6 declared span on the-flooded-quarter) so the formula's soundness is pinned, not just its arithmetic. When any future rule derives a threshold or multiplier from a declared, attacker-influenced field, check first whether a canonical, code-owned constant already carries the true value; prefer that constant over the declared field even when the two are expected to agree, because "expected to agree" is exactly the gap CH-2's range-equality check does not close (it enforces internal consistency between two declared values, never consistency with the canonical count). open src/cyo_adventure/validator/character.py::_check_ch8_build_node_cost, src/cyo_adventure/storybook/character_vocabulary.py (ARCHETYPE_VARIABLE_NAME), tests/unit/test_character_rules.py::test_ch8_rejects_a_narrow_declared_span_that_still_means_six_archetypes; no PR yet (unmerged, branch feat/persistent-characters), tracked by UW-C42 in unscheduled-work-register.md

All four were fixed with boundary-adjacent or direction-naming pinning tests, independently re-mutated and re-verified in PR #636's task-7 re-review. The common shape: a mutation chosen far from the real decision boundary, or a fixture set that never reaches an edge case, produces a passing mutation-kill report that certifies nothing about the boundary or the untested branch. When writing a mutation-testing pass for a threshold or a boolean-branch guard, deliberately choose the mutated value or branch closest to the real boundary (an off-by-one on a comparison, the flipped return on an unexercised branch) rather than an arbitrary large change, and require at least one fixture that lands exactly on the boundary so the pinning test fails for the right reason instead of an unrelated one. open PR #636 (task-6 review I4; task-7 review I1/I2/I3; task-7 re-review independently re-mutating I1/I2, both confirmed); src/cyo_adventure/validator/character.py::build_node_headroom; tests/unit/test_character_rules.py::test_ch3b_distinguishes_two_dead_branches_on_one_node, ::test_build_node_headroom_boundary_pins_the_inclusive_comparison, ::test_build_node_headroom_is_fail_closed_on_a_capped_walk; tracked by UW-C46 in unscheduled-work-register.md

    AL-115 2026-08-06 PR #636 review (C1), docs/superpowers/specs/ tooling A design spec added under docs/ used 13 relative markdown links into the source tree (../../../src/cyo_adventure/...). MkDocs validation.links.not_found defaults to warn, and mkdocs.yml sets strict: true, which promotes every one of those warnings to an abort: Aborted with 13 warnings in strict mode. The failure is invisible locally because nothing in .pre-commit-config.yaml builds the docs, and docs.yml runs only on paths: docs/**, so the author sees a clean commit and a red CI job. The near-miss that makes this worth logging: the obvious fix, rewriting them as absolute blob/main URLs, would have passed mkdocs while handing lychee 13 new live targets that rot as line numbers move. Two link checkers with opposite preferences, and satisfying one is how you break the other. Reference source files from docs prose as plain code spans, never as relative links out of the docs tree, matching the dominant docs/planning/ convention. Add a cheap pre-commit check that fails on a markdown link from docs/** resolving outside docs/, so the abort is caught at commit time rather than in CI, and record the absolute-URL alternative as rejected so it is not re-proposed. open
    AL-116 2026-08-06 PR #636 review (I12), skeletons/ catalog measurements process Two documents in the same PR cited different totals for the same directory, and both were internally defensible: the spec's §2.1 said "Skeletons total | 124" and ADR-025 said "all 61 committed skeletons/*/*.json files". skeletons/*/*.json really does match 124 files, but 47 are .contract.json and 16 are .lineage.json sidecars on their own versioning schemes; only 61 are skeleton documents. When a doc quotes a catalog measurement, quote the artifact count and name the filter that produced it, never a bare glob match.     AL-117 2026-08-06 PR #636 review (I7), validator/gate.py::_parse_storybook tooling The handler for "L1-1 passed but Pydantic still rejected the document" carried an #EDGE marker asserting the path "should not occur in practice because L1-1 validates against the schema exported from the same Pydantic models", and a #VERIFY instructing the reader to keep schema_export.build_schema() in sync with models.py. Both were false, and the same PR falsified them: Storybook's checks are model_validator(mode="after"), which JSON Schema cannot express at all, so schema/storybook.schema.json types schema_version as a bare {"type": "string"} and every unsupported version clears L1-1 and lands here. The prescribed remedy could never work, because no amount of syncing makes JSON Schema carry an after-validator. The finding message then blamed "(schema drift?)", so the report misdirected the reader too. The marker was written when the claim was true and rotted when the validator layer grew. A RAD marker asserting a path is unreachable must name the mechanism that makes it unreachable, and the #VERIFY must be an action that could actually detect the claim going false. When adding a model_validator(mode="after"), check for existing markers that assume schema-export equivalence, since each new after-validator falsifies them a little more. Cover the reachable path with an unmocked test: the two existing tests patched model_validate to fake the failure, which can prove the handler runs but never when it runs. open

    AL-124 2026-08-08 The D-2 RAD-citation checker's delivery and Task 9's re-review, persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling Checking that a #VERIFY citation names a test that exists is the automatable half of the problem, and it is not the half that matters. This workstream shipped nine non-discriminating or fabricated #VERIFY citations before scripts/check_rad_citations.py existed; the new checker resolves and grandfathers all nine into rad-citation-baseline.toml without complaint, because every one of them names a test that is real, passes, and proves nothing about the claim it is attached to. The ninth is the clearest case: Reader.tsx's flowed-and-seeded branch cited stops.test.ts, which proves stops.ts forwards a parameter it is handed, never that Reader.tsx hands it one; deleting seed from that branch left all scoped tests green. A green check_rad_citations.py run must never be read as "the citations are sound", only as "the citations are not fabricated"; whether a cited test would actually fail if the tagged assumption broke is a review-time judgment call this or any mechanical checker cannot make. Add a line to the RAD section of root CLAUDE.md and to docs/response-aware-development.md stating plainly that citation-existence checking is necessary but not sufficient, and that a reviewer must ask "would this test fail if the assumption were false?" for every #VERIFY it reads, not just "does this test exist?". open UW-C59
    AL-125 2026-08-07 Tasks 6, 7, 10, and 13 of the persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) process Code and paths supplied inside a task brief need the same skepticism as code from any other source, because a brief is not a verified artifact, it is a plan author's best guess written before the implementer opened the files.
