    AL-126 2026-08-07 Wave-1 parallel agent dispatch, persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime), Task 10's commit process Running pre-commit while other agents hold uncommitted, unstaged edits in the same shared worktree is unsafe, and not for the reason a "no concurrent git writes" rule protects against. pre-commit stashes unstaged changes before running its hooks and restores them afterward; a concurrent agent's write landing inside that stash/restore window can be silently reverted by the restore, with no error anywhere in the output, only a bland "Restored changes from ...patch..." line that reads exactly like success. The other agents' git-write prohibition protects the index; it does nothing for this hazard, since the stash is triggered by any pre-commit invocation, including one run by an agent that itself made no other git-write violation. Document in the parallel-dispatch coordination pattern (wherever concurrent-worktree agent rules are recorded) that pre-commit must not run while any other agent in the same worktree may still be mid-write; batch commits after every in-flight agent has reported, against a quiescent tree, one group at a time. open UW-C61
    AL-127 2026-08-07 Task 11 (promoting the-storm-chasers-club to Tier 2), persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) validator Introducing a dedicated build node to set a character's archetype, with each of the six archetype choices converging on it, silently disqualifies the sorting_hat topology. validator/topology.py's sorting_hat admissibility check disqualifies any node with in-degree >= 2, and a build node fed by six archetype-setting choices has in-degree 6 by construction. This was caught by reading topology.py before editing the skeleton, not by a failing gate run, so it would not have appeared anywhere in gate output had the author not looked first. The working alternative, used in Task 11: attach the archetype-setting effects directly to choices that already exist on the tree's own branch points, adding no new node and no new converging edge. The promotion that occasioned this was withdrawn on 2026-08-08 (AL-131); the topology finding stands on its own and the proposed doc change below is unaffected, since the trap is a property of sorting_hat admissibility, not of that book. Add a line to .claude/skills/cyo-author/reference/skeleton-format.md's "The archetype build node" section, which is where the gate-node/build-node idiom is actually documented (docs/architecture/story-skeletons.md has no build-node section; an earlier draft of this row named one that does not exist), stating explicitly that a sorting_hat-topology book must not introduce a build node as a new converging node, and that the safe pattern is attaching the archetype-setting effect to existing choices on existing branch points instead. open UW-C62

    AL-129 2026-08-08 Task 12 (13-16 stat-envelope gamebook pilot; the pilot was withdrawn from the branch on 2026-08-08 and is preserved out of tree, so no skeleton path is cited here), persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) validator A stat-envelope book (accepts_character declaring might/wits/nerve, each immutable per the design's "seeded, not built in-story" cost model) cannot gate a choice condition directly on a stat's threshold value without tripping a blocking L2-11 dead-branch error: validator/layer2.py::_check_dead_branches walks only the book's single declared-initial baseline configuration with zero accepts_character awareness, so a stat == 0 branch is unconditionally unreachable in baseline whenever the book's own declared initial is stat: 1, regardless of what the envelope proves reachable. CH-3a's union-quantified walk does not suppress this, because L2-11 is raised independently on the baseline walk itself. The design spec's section 4.3 "archetype = 0" build-node idiom is the analogous fix for the archetype variable, but does not transfer to stat books, which deliberately have no in-story mutating node (that absence is exactly what keeps the 27-state walk cheap per the design's section 4.5). Working fix (authoring-only, no validator or design-doc change): pair every stat-gated choice with an auxiliary ordinary (non-canonical) boolean resolve, reset by an unconditioned binary player choice immediately before the gate, and XOR-combine it with the stat threshold so the pair collapses to depend only on resolve at the fixed baseline value (both branches reachable via ordinary play, satisfying L2-11) while genuinely differentiating on the stat at other envelope states (for CH-3a/CH-3b/CH-4's per-state walks). Verified empirically: baseline walk 530 configs; all 27 envelope states also 530 configs each, zero per-walk size cost. The workaround is what killed the pilot, and that is the larger half of the lesson. Because the XOR pairing requires an unconditioned player choice able to flip the outcome immediately before every gate, the reader cannot perceive their stat deciding anything: the stat becomes a hidden modifier behind a coin flip the reader just made. Applied across a whole book it made all 14 stations mechanically identical, and the pilot read as scaffolding rather than a gamebook, so it was withdrawn from the branch on 2026-08-08 and preserved out of tree rather than shipped. A cost model that measures only configuration count therefore missed the cost that mattered: the workaround is free in walk time and expensive in reader-perceptible agency, and nothing in the gate could have reported the second. Two parts, and the second is the real decision. (1) Done on this branch, uncommitted: the L2-11-vs-immutable-stat interaction and the resolve/XOR pattern are now documented in the two docs an author actually reads, .claude/skills/cyo-author/reference/skeleton-format.md ("The stat-gate wall") and docs/architecture/story-skeletons.md, each stating plainly that the workaround leaves the reader unable to perceive the choice. The design spec is not the right home; authors do not read it. (2) Open and undecided: whether validator/layer2.py::_check_dead_branches should become envelope-aware, quantifying reachability over the entry states accepts_character declares instead of the baseline configuration alone. That would remove the need for the workaround and make a genuine stat gamebook authorable, but it is a validator change to a rule every skeleton in the catalog is already gated by, so its blast radius is catalog-wide and it needs a measured re-gate of the whole catalog before anyone commits to it. Not proposed as decided; proposed as the question to answer before another stat-envelope pilot is attempted. open UW-C64
    AL-130 2026-08-08 D-2 gate delivery (scripts/check_rad_citations.py), persistent-characters runtime plan (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling A debt baseline generated from the working tree grandfathers the generating workstream's own new debt, and no amount of shrinking the baseline can surface it. rad-citation-baseline.toml was written by running --write-baseline over the tree as it then stood, which already contained src/cyo_adventure/validator/character.py, a file this workstream created. Its CH-2 #VERIFY cited test_canonical_bounds_are_read_by_the_validator, a test that never existed. The row was absorbed as pre-existing debt indistinguishable from the 122 genuine ones, the gate went green on its first live run, and the defect would have shipped inside the very commit that introduced the gate meant to catch it. Found only by intersecting the baseline's file list with the branch's changed-file list, which is not a check anything runs. The general shape: a grandfathering baseline defines "pre-existing" as "present when I looked", but the author is looking at a tree that already contains their own work, so the baseline's own generation is the moment the distinction is lost. Make --write-baseline refuse to add a row whose file is added or modified relative to origin/main, printing those citations as errors to fix rather than debt to inherit, so a baseline can only ever grandfather work that predates the branch writing it. Pair it with a CI assertion that the baseline never grows between origin/main and a PR head, which turns shrink-only from a comment in the file's header into something enforced.     AL-131 2026-08-08 Final whole-branch review, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) validator The CH-* rules prove an envelope is declared and survivable, never that it is observable. the-storm-chasers-club's Tier 2 promotion passed all eight CH rules and the full gate while being behaviourally inert in two independent ways. Its single archetype-gated choice (r_a_fix/c_rafix_explain, condition {"==": [{"var": "archetype"}, 4]}) sits behind an only-inbound edge (c_rapick_window) whose effect sets archetype = 4, so the condition is unconditionally true for every one of the seven declared entry states; and its six archetype-setting choices are ungated with no bypass, which the same branch's skeleton-format.md explicitly forbids. The 13-16 stat pilot failed the same observability test through an unrelated mechanism (AL-129's XOR workaround), so two pilots built to the same spec were both imperceptible while both passing the gate. The promotion was withdrawn. Add a CH rule that fails a condition on a canonical character variable when every inbound path to the gate sets that variable to a value satisfying the condition, i.e. a gate whose truth is invariant across the declared envelope; and a companion rule that a book declaring accepts_character must have at least one condition whose truth varies across the declared entry states. Both are computable from the per-state walk validator/character.py already performs, so neither needs new traversal machinery. Until one of them exists, no gate run can distinguish a character-aware book from a book that merely says it is one, and the CH family's passing verdict should not be read as evidence of an observable envelope. open UW-C73

    AL-133 2026-08-08 Final whole-branch review fix wave, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling An assertion that reads ordering out of source text is fooled by comments, because a comment that explains an ordering constraint has to name the later step first. A new test asserted that the CI step resolving a git ref runs git rev-parse before git cat-file -e, by finding each command's first occurrence in the extracted step body. It failed against the correct script: the step's own #CRITICAL comment names git cat-file -e while explaining why rev-parse must come first, so the explanation of the rule appeared earlier in the text than the code obeying it. The failure is the dangerous direction only by luck. Had the comment been phrased the other way round, the same assertion would have passed on a script with the calls in the wrong order, and a test asserting an ordering it cannot actually read is worse than no test. The general shape: source text is not the program, and any assertion that treats position-in-file as position-in-execution must first remove everything that is not executed. Strip comment lines before reading ordering or presence out of any extracted script body, and prefer asserting on the parsed step rather than on raw text wherever the harness already parses it. Applied on this branch in tests/unit/test_ci_gate_contract.py, which now filters comment lines out of the step body before locating either command. Worth generalising: the same module makes several presence assertions against raw workflow text, and every one of them can be satisfied by a mention in a comment rather than by real configuration. open UW-C75
    AL-134 2026-08-08 Final whole-branch review fix wave, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling A FastAPI route docstring is published API surface, so documenting behaviour in one breaks the contract gate while changing nothing on the wire. FastAPI copies a route handler's docstring into the OpenAPI operation's description, and @hey-api/openapi-ts copies that verbatim into frontend/src/client/sdk.gen.ts, which is committed and drift-checked by the contract CI job. A fix wave documented the new character cap in create_character's docstring as an ordinary code comment would be, which would have failed contract on a change with no behavioural content, and the failure would have surfaced in CI as generated-client drift rather than as anything resembling its cause. This is the same channel as the already-known Pydantic model docstring path into types.gen.ts, reached from the route side instead of the model side, which is why knowing the first case did not prevent the second. Treat a route docstring as a contract edit, not a comment: either leave it byte-identical and put implementation notes in a call-site comment or a private helper's docstring, which is what this branch did, or change it deliberately and regenerate the client in the same commit. The verification that settles it in either direction is cheap and worth writing down: dump app.openapi() in-process and diff every operation description against the committed sdk.gen.ts, which reports drift locally instead of waiting for the contract job. Worth promoting into the contract-gate documentation so both channels, model docstrings and route docstrings, are named in one place. open UW-C76
    AL-135 2026-08-08 PR #649 CI, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling A schema invariant recorded only as a RAD prose comment is enforced by nothing, so the branch that falsifies it finds out from a downstream job rather than from a test. scripts/reset_e2e_real_state.py issued a bare TRUNCATE TABLE reading_state under an #ASSUME stating that nothing declares a foreign key onto that table.     AL-136 2026-08-08 PR #649 CI, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) process A gate placed where the data was convenient rather than where the need is silently becomes a product-wide behaviour change, and a green unit suite cannot see it. KidShell already fetched the active character for the kid library route, so the first-run character gate was written there: with status === 'none' it rendered CharacterCreator instead of the library Outlet. That inverted the gate's scope from per-book to per-profile. Every existing child was hard-gated into creating a character before reaching their library, with no skip affordance, for a feature no catalog book can use, which also contradicted the branch's own stated posture that the chain merges enforced and unreached. Six unit tests covered the gate and all six passed, because each asserted the gate did what it was built to do; none could ask whether the scope was right. The only thing that caught it was the real-backend Playwright smoke, and it surfaced as a 30s timeout on getByRole('link', {name: 'The Tide Pool Mystery'}), a locator that says nothing about characters. Two changes. First, when a plan says "show X when Y has none", make it state the SCOPE explicitly, per book, per profile, or per route, because the implementer will otherwise put the check wherever the data already is, and that location silently sets the scope. Second, treat "changes what every existing user sees on next visit" as a distinct review question the diff cannot answer: a per-task reviewer reading only the gate sees a correct gate. Resolved here by exposing accepts_character on LibraryItem and deciding at the book, at click time, failing open on every non-'none' status since the backend already treats an unseeded read as normal. open UW-C78
    AL-137 2026-08-08 PR #649 CI, persistent-characters runtime (branch feat/persistent-characters-runtime) tooling A pull request that conflicts with its base runs ZERO GitHub Actions workflows, and the third-party app checks that remain make it read as green. pull_request workflows are evaluated against refs/pull/N/merge, a ref GitHub cannot create while the branch conflicts, so after origin/main moved under this branch the second push produced no runs at all. What gh pr checks then showed was four checks, all passing: CodeRabbit, GitGuardian, Socket Security, and qlty. Those post against the head SHA through the Checks API and are unaffected by the merge ref, so they keep reporting on a branch whose real CI never started. Nothing in that output says "conflicted" or "no runs"; the only tells are the context COUNT collapsing from ~30 to 4, and gh run list --branch <b> showing its newest head SHA behind the PR's. This is the same family as the existing roll-up traps (a skipped job renders green, a job with no runner renders red with steps: []), but strictly worse, because here the roll-up check does not exist at all rather than reporting the wrong colour. Never read gh pr checks alone as evidence that CI ran. Check gh pr view <n> --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus in the same call and treat CONFLICTING/DIRTY as "CI is not running", not as "merge later"; and cross-check the newest head SHA in gh run list --branch <b> against the PR head SHA, since a matching SHA is the only positive evidence the push was picked up.     AL-138 2026-08-09 PR #661 review, story-diversity checkers (branch feat/story-diversity-checkers) validator PL-24's gamebook winnability floor was changed from an absolute 3 to max(3, ceil(0.05 * total)), justified in a comment committed to validator/policy.py saying the absolute floor "had been calibrated below the whole corpus and was exercised only by unit test". Measuring the committed catalog falsifies both halves: the absolute floor already fired on 7 of 14 gamebooks, and the share floor fires on all 14. The cause is that winnable endings in this corpus are roughly constant rather than proportional to book size. Every gamebook carries between 2 and 5 positive endings whether it holds 74 endings or 209, so a 5% share demands 11 positive endings from the-tenfold-siege where the author wrote 2, and the highest positive count anywhere in the catalog is 5. An advisory that fires on 100% of one narrative style carries no information, and the false calibration claim now sits in production source where the next reader will trust it. Require any scaled or share-based floor to arrive at review with a measured distribution attached rather than a prose claim about one. Add a catalog measurement mode that reports a proposed floor's fire rate per narrative style and age band, so the corpus impact of a threshold change is computed rather than asserted. Separately, rule on which way this specific case cuts: gamebooks averaging 3 winnable endings out of roughly 130 may be a genuine design defect for a kids' reading app, in which case the floor is right and the catalog is the work. open

    AL-143 2026-08-09 Pre-rebuild review, strict-mode implementation (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) validator Arming the CG choice-grammar family on a skeleton shell revealed that CG-4 can never pass one: it checks content-word overlap between a choice label and the opening sentence of the target node's body, and on a shell every body is a <<FILL ...>> directive, so the overlap is empty by construction and CG-4 fires on every choice of every shell. The family was designed against filled stories and nothing documented which CG rules are shell-safe, so the first strict run failed the demo fixture on ten findings of which six were structurally meaningless. CG-1 (run cap), CG-2 (option bounds) and CG-3 (words per stop, which reads declared FILL word targets) are all shell-meaningful; only CG-4 requires prose. This is the same shape RL-13 hit before AL-006 taught it to skip FILL bodies. Applied: check_skeleton.py --strict escalates CG-1..CG-3 and deliberately excludes CG-4 with an in-code rationale; CG-4 belongs to the fill gate. When the grammar is armed for filled stories (UW-C24 flip), consider teaching CG-4 to skip FILL bodies outright, the AL-006 precedent, so a partially filled draft cannot fire it spuriously either. applied PR #661 squash-merged to main 2026-08-10 as 4e1a08bc; the earlier branch SHAs lived only on a scratch branch that never merged
    AL-144 2026-08-09 Reconvergence hard-gate ruling implementation (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) validator A reconvergence cap designed from the aggregate (corpus mean max in-degree 7.79 vs the research anchor 1.5) would have banned two whole topology families, because the aggregate mixes two opposite mechanisms. Measured per topology before implementing: open_map hubs sit at median max in-degree 9 with one legitimate hub at 126 (the-blackwood-sanatorium, every room re-enters the hub by design) and loop_and_grow at 5, while the pathological corridor-collapse funnels live in the tree-like topologies (branch_and_bottleneck clone pair at 31, the-cinder-bazaar at 21). A blanket per-band cap tight enough to catch the funnels excludes every open map; one loose enough to admit open maps catches nothing. The gate had to be topology-aware to mean anything: cap branch_and_bottleneck/gauntlet/sorting_hat/time_cave at 4/4/6/6/8/8 by band, exempt the hub topologies whose re-entry is the design, and leave those to the SQ-15 per-path metrics. The general shape: a structural quality gate calibrated on a corpus-wide distribution silently encodes the assumption that one mechanism produced the distribution. Applied: topology-aware max in-degree hard cap in check_skeleton.py --strict with the exemption rationale in-code; when the strict checks are promoted into validator/ after the grandfathered catalog is removed, carry the topology awareness into the catalogued rule rather than flattening it to one number. applied PR #661 squash-merged to main 2026-08-10 as 4e1a08bc; the earlier branch SHAs lived only on a scratch branch that never merged
    AL-145 2026-08-09 Strict-bar conformance census after the 2026-08-09 rulings (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) process An advisory rule family left inert since landing accumulates near-universal non-conformance, and the flip cost grows monotonically while nobody watches. Arming CG-1..CG-3 inside the strict bar and censusing the catalog showed 57 of 61 skeletons breach CG-2 and 48 breach CG-3: the entire catalog predates the grammar's own bar because enforce_grammar defaulted False from the day the family landed (UW-C24) and no production caller ever opted in, so every authoring wave since added non-conforming trees at zero visible cost. The full strict census passes 2 of 61, meaning the ruled bar implies a near-total rebuild; that number was invisible until the rules were armed against the corpus even once. The general shape: an inert gate is not a deferred decision, it is a debt meter running with the display off, and the census that reads the meter costs one afternoon against a rebuild-sized principal. Applied for this flip: the census ran before any removal and its numbers plus a remove-on-replacement sequencing recommendation are recorded in the 2026-08-09 review (Part 4 census section), so the removal wave is sized by data. Standing practice: before arming any long-inert rule, census the live corpus with the rule in report mode and record the count, so the flip cost is a number in a doc rather than a surprise in CI. open the code half merged with PR #661 (4e1a08bc, 2026-08-10), but this lesson's evidence is the 2026-08-09 review write-up on PR #662, which has not merged; it flips to applied on that merge SHA
    AL-146 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) tooling PL-23 only reveals the derived reading clock inside a failure message, so a declared estimated_minutes that is wrong but within the 25% tolerance never surfaces the true number, and every out-of-tolerance miss costs a full draft-gate iteration.     AL-147 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, three cells with independent adversarial review process Strict-clean does not mean approvable, and the gap is systematic rather than incidental: three drafts in three different cells each passed check_skeleton --strict with zero findings at any severity, and each was independently REJECTED on adversarial review, on defects no deterministic layer measures (label laundering, positional fate bias, consequence-free choices, merge contradictions, quota-shaped design). The gate verifies topology, budgets, and quota arithmetic; the rejections were all about outcomes, consequences, and continuity. Second half of the lesson: both large-cell drafters concluded a hand-author cannot hold the coupled global constraints at 180+ nodes, and each built an ad-hoc structural generator replicating the strict rule set; the only one-shot passes came from that method. Make adversarial critique a mandatory stage of the rebuild drafting workflow (draft, strict gate, in-cell audits, adversarial review, then promotion PR), and ship the structural generator harness as a first-class authoring aid instead of expecting every wave to rebuild it ad hoc. open UW-C84
    AL-148 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of all three drafts validator The outcome gates read kind/valence tags, not outcomes, so a draft can satisfy PL-24, the walk floor, and the outcome-spread audit purely by tag distribution over scenes the tags misdescribe. All three pilot drafts did it, two of them unconsciously: the 3-5 draft narrates one identical terminal scene under four kind/valence combinations, one tag contradicting its own beats; the 10-13 draft tags a full mission success neutral and ten failures as discovery; the 13-16 draft has sixteen everyone-survives rescues tagged negative setback and twelve off-page abandonments tagged neutral, plus role= derived mechanically from valence so twelve endings instruct the fill to write a discovery where nothing is discovered. Every headline economy number that passed the gates was a tag artifact.     AL-149 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of the two large drafts validator Choice-level fairness and consequence are invisible to every current metric, and generated drafts fail both by construction. Position bias: in the 10-13 draft the third option leads to an ending 77% of the time and the first option never does; in the 13-16 draft always-pick-option-1 walks to the single best ending with zero risk, because the generator ordered options safe-first in every fan. Consequence: 25 decision nodes in the 10-13 draft have sibling choices with byte-identical reachable-ending sets, and staged merges collapse 98 ancestors into one node while never exceeding direct in-degree 5, laundering exactly the funnel the in-degree cap was ruled to stop. Add computable consequence checks to the strict bar: per-position ending-kind distribution with a skew ceiling plus an argmax-position walk that must not reach a positive ending unscathed; flag sibling choices with identical reachable-ending sets; extend the reconvergence gate with a k-hop ancestor-funnel metric. Generators must seeded-shuffle option order. open UW-C86
    AL-150 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of the two large drafts validator Merge-node beat contradictions, the known the-hollow-lighthouse defect class, are reproduced systematically by generation rather than occasionally by hand: eight hard upstream-vs-beat contradictions in each large draft, including endings narrating scenes from choices the reader explicitly declined, characters referenced at merges who are introduced only on other routes, gear appearing from nowhere, and one terminal beat hedging 'whichever stranger you trusted' because the generator knew its parents disagreed.     AL-152 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, both large-cell drafters' friction logs validator Several strict constraints are individually reasonable but couple destructively, and the couplings, not the values, dictated the drafts' shape: CG-3's stop ceiling equals roughly two to three nodes at the band words mean (a lone corridor node at the 10-13 mean is already illegal before a normal-weight successor; at 13-16 every checkpoint merge lives within ten words of the wall), so both drafts near-eliminated corridors and one emitted stops at 148-150 of the 150 cap, leaving zero fill headroom; CG-2's exact (3,3) at 8-13 bans the binary dilemma and forces a third strategy at every one of 100+ decisions, which is where template-sameness pressure originates; the 25% gamebook endings fraction makes terminal leaves the only legal padding, pushing toward the shallow-ending sprawl other rules exist to prevent; PL-26's gamebook ceiling of 4.0 is unmotivated by its own comment yet strict-blocking; PL-24's 60% kind ceiling quantizes harshly at four or fewer endings, silently raising the real endings floor for small kid books.     AL-155 2026-08-09 Same-skeleton diversity experiment: three isolated fills of the-lost-mitten under maximally different theme bindings (review Part 6) validator A same-skeleton story pair has a perceived-similarity floor of 0.50 before any prose exists (PS weights 0.5 leaf + 0.3 structural + 0.2 theme; structural is 1.0 for a same-tree pair by construction and theme was 1.0 because binding never re-themes metadata.themes), and the empirical best case, three independent authors filling three maximally different bindings with near-minimal leaf similarity (0.18-0.20, body trigram Jaccard 0.026-0.029), landed at PS 0.59-0.60 against the 0.70 repeat threshold.     AL-157 2026-08-09 Same-skeleton diversity experiment (review Part 6) process Beats encode world facts the slot system does not govern, and the fill contract's reskin rule is ambiguous enough that three independent authors resolved the same conflicts three different ways: the skeleton's un-slotted snow was imported into an autumn park by one author and reskinned to dew by another; 'one bare hand is cold' was inverted to a hot bare head because the bound item was a sunhat; and one binding produced 'fireflies chirped' because the beat verb belonged to the legacy birds, a world-consistency defect no gate catches.     AL-158 2026-08-09 Opus pipeline analysis for the narrative redesign (review of every FILL-directive consumer) tooling Every consumer of the FILL directive fails open on a parse miss: binding.py, fidelity.py, fidelity_review.py, and slotted_surfaces.py all skip the node when the anchored regex does not match, and three more private regex copies live in mutation/bundle.py, mutation/operators.py, and parameterize_skeleton.py. A future format change the regex does not match would therefore silently disable slot substitution, word-count fidelity, semantic fidelity, theme-leak scanning, and slot-set equality simultaneously, with zero findings anywhere. The consumers were each individually reasonable; the cascade is the defect, and it is invisible until the first format evolution, which the narrative redesign now schedules. Make the parse total before any format work: a body starting with the FILL marker that does not parse becomes a blocking L1 finding; add a lockstep test enumerating every directive consumer and asserting each reports rather than skips on an unparseable directive; collapse the three private regex copies onto storybook/slotted_surfaces.py.     AL-159 2026-08-09 Opus format-design analysis, re-expressing the-lost-mitten node by node validator A node with an in-edge from its own descendant is re-rendered verbatim on every loop, and nothing tells the prose author: the-lost-mitten's three abandon-the-lead choices all target n_start, whose beat stages the loss as fresh news, so all three experiment fills wrote a discovery beat and a child who loops back reads the oh-no moment again on every return.     AL-172 2026-08-09 Model-tier study, blind three-tier prose comparison (proposal section 13) product Model tier moves prose quality but not sameness: across nine books from one skeleton, blind craft scores ran 4.9 (frontier), 4.0 (Sonnet), 2.2 (Haiku) with ship verdicts of as-is, light-edit, and do-not-publish, while the recognition score was 2.5 for BOTH the frontier and Haiku tiers with the same drivers named independently (keeper-plus-maker pair, four-exit hub menu). Diversity is a property of the skeleton and contract; prose is a property of the author; they are close to orthogonal. Separately, convergence was not monotonic in capability, with Sonnet the most convergent tier (29.7 shared grams per 1000) and Haiku the least (8.5), each converging its own way: Haiku near-quotes contract strings, Sonnet reaches for stock genre idioms. Treat the catalog-diversity work and the fill-model cost decision as independent choices; set the fill model on prose grounds (Sonnet floor, with a reading-level directive for its systematic upward drift at mean FK 9.17) and use Haiku for mechanical roles it passed cleanly: selection derivation, deterministic-finding revision rounds, first-pass triage. open UW-C108
    AL-173 2026-08-09 Model-tier study, comparative vs isolated rating of the same books (proposal section 13) process Absolute quality judgments are systematically more forgiving than comparative ones: the same three Haiku books scored 2.2 with a rewrite verdict from a rater holding all nine books side by side, and 3.0 with a one-editing-pass verdict from a rater holding only those three. This is the project's single-reviewer problem measured directly, and it means a reviewer approving one story in isolation will pass work that a side-by-side comparison rejects. Show a sibling fill alongside the story under review in the approval surface, and give the automated review panel a comparative rather than absolute rubric; treat isolated-rating scores as an upper bound. open UW-C109
    AL-174 2026-08-09 Model-tier study, deterministic battery across three tiers (proposal section 13) validator Gate-clean does not mean publishable: all nine books across three model tiers cleared fill integrity, the full validator gate, safety, topology, word bands, and title contracts on the first pass, and three of them were judged unpublishable by a blind rater for tense instability including breaks inside a single paragraph, a central puzzle that is asserted rather than performed ("the digits spell out language"), and narrator moral tags closing nearly every ending. None of those three defect classes is visible to any existing check. Add deterministic prose-craft detectors for tense stability, narrator moral tags, and told-emotion density, calibrated so they separate the observed tiers; and state plainly in the authoring docs that the gate certifies validity, not quality. applied PR #661 squash-merged to main 2026-08-10 as 4e1a08bc; the earlier branch SHAs lived only on a scratch branch that never merged
    AL-175 2026-08-09 Prose-craft detector calibration (proposal section 13, commit 4b93b97) validator The prose-craft detectors separate publishable from unpublishable books perfectly, but the separation is IN-SAMPLE by construction: every threshold (--max-unstable-nodes 0, --min-tense-cues 6, --max-node-mix 0.34, --max-moral-tags 0, --max-told-per-1000 0.5) was tuned against the same nine books it is reported to separate, so the result measures fit rather than predictive power. Two thresholds are already known to be fragile: the told-emotion rate ranks one unpublishable book (0.31 per 1000) BELOW one shippable book (0.35), so that detector cannot rank those two and the book is caught only by the other two detectors; and the moral-tag budget of zero is unfalsifiable on a corpus where every shippable book scored zero. Validate out of sample before wiring the detectors into any blocking gate: score a held-out set of books that had no part in calibration, ideally from a different skeleton and band, and re-tune on the union; until then run the detectors as advisory-only and report their findings to the author rather than blocking on them. open UW-C111
    AL-176 2026-08-09 Per-child sibling-exposure analysis (scripts/analyze_sibling_exposure.py, skeleton-free alternatives proposal section 2b) product The unit of story-sameness exposure is the CHILD, not the family, and the system has no per-child skeleton history at all: Storybook carries only family_id and personalization_subject_profile_id, and both reads of prior skeleton_slug (skeleton_match.py:587, diversity/history.py) filter on family with a 20-row window. Family scoping alone triples the second-request repeat rate (0.09 to 0.29 from one child to three) while a child-scoped counterfactual reproduces the single-reader curve, and the shared window hides 81% of a child's own band reading in a three-child household. StoryRequest.profile_id and StorybookAssignment.child_profile_id already carry the needed evidence. Scope the anti-repeat history and weighting to the requesting child rather than the family, widen or drop the 20-row window, and close the visibility='catalog' leak where a cross-family read is invisible to recency. Recovers 25 to 30% of the required catalog for no new content. open UW-C112
    AL-177 2026-08-09 Per-child sibling-exposure analysis (proposal section 2b) product A skeleton is consumable within a child but reusable across children, and nothing in the code contradicts that (no global or cross-family anti-repeat exists; visibility='catalog' actively amortizes one fill across families). Required catalog therefore scales with per-child lifetime consumption inside a band, roughly tenure x rate spread over that band's cells, and is INDEPENDENT of user count, which makes catalog growth a bounded one-time capital cost rather than a cost that grows with the business. Measured need is about 130 skeletons at 0.5 stories per child per month, 408 at 1, and 816 at 2, against 57 production-eligible today. Treat catalog build as bounded capital and size it against measured demand; instrument per-child stories per month per band, which is the single number that sets the target and is not measured in this deployment. open UW-C113
    AL-178 2026-08-09 Per-child sibling-exposure analysis (proposal section 2b) scale Every populated production cell holds 3 or 4 skeletons across all six bands, so a child exhausts a cell by their 4th request in it and every later story in that cell is with certainty a tree they have already read; a repeat is more likely than not by request 3 or 4, and rotating across a band's length cells buys about two extra requests rather than an order of magnitude. This, not the skeleton format, is the measured driver of the sameness the three diversity pilots characterized, and it retires the case for replacing the skeleton architecture. Fund catalog depth and per-child scoping ahead of any pipeline replacement; keep the skeleton-free alternatives as a designed option set to revisit only if those two fixes fail to clear the problem. open UW-C114
    AL-179 2026-08-09 Adversarial review of the skeleton-free alternatives proposal validator SAFE-14 (validator/safety.py:41) is a Phase-2 stub returning an empty report unconditionally, so safety_flagged cannot take a non-false value, yet "zero safety flags" was reported as evidence of safety seven times across pilots 1 to 3 and the model-tier study. The real deterministic pre-LLM denylist floor on untrusted brief material is validator/slots.py, whose only caller is the theme-binding path, so it runs for skeleton fills only and a skeleton-free story has no pre-LLM denylist gate. Separately, the gate is provenance-agnostic but not format-agnostic: PL-19, PL-23, CG-3, and RL-13 each parse the <<FILL words=N>> grammar, and enforce_grammar defaults off so generated stories never run CG-1 through CG-4 at all. Stop reporting safety_flagged as a measured outcome until SAFE-14 is implemented, and mark it explicitly as a stub in any results table; document that the gate's strictness is caller-parameterized so cross-path comparisons state their scale and enforce_grammar settings. open UW-C115
    AL-180 2026-08-09 Adversarial review, strict-bar measurement across the catalog validator The --strict authoring bar passes 2 of 61 catalog skeletons (0 of 11 at 10-13), because it gates newly drafted skeletons while the shipped catalog is grandfathered. Two consequences were being missed: a proposal pre-registered "strict bar: pass by construction" for the baseline, which is false 97% of the time, and the pilots' own control skeleton the-clocktower-cipher is the catalog's single non-production-eligible (10-13, None, None) row, so it is never served to a child and, declaring no length or style, is held to band budgets rather than the ADR-011 cell budgets every production 10-13 skeleton faces. Every pilot baseline number was measured on it. Decide explicitly whether the strict bar is the intended production bar (promoting it as a blocking rule would retire 97% of the catalog, a product decision); and re-baseline future diversity work on a production-eligible skeleton in a real cell. open UW-C116
    AL-181 2026-08-09 Length-demand catalog sizing (scripts/analyze_sibling_exposure.py, proposal section 2b.3a) scale Story-length demand is not flat within a band and the catalog is: every populated cell holds 3 to 4 skeletons while demand concentrates on medium. Because skeleton_matches_cell treats only a NULL length as a wildcard and 0 of 58 production-eligible skeletons declare one, cells are hard partitions, so surplus in the short and long cells is stranded capital that can never serve a medium request. Peaking demand leaves the band TOTAL essentially unchanged (one child's band-lifetime consumption however it splits) but raises the SHORTFALL by 23 to 32% and moves the binding constraint to the medium cell in every band, pulling the first likely repeat forward about one request (P(repeat by request 4) at 8-11 rises from 0.386 flat to 0.647 strongly-medium-weighted). Author against the demand curve rather than filling cells evenly: put the first 6 to 12 skeletons of each band's budget entirely into that band's medium cell, which buys 0.8 to 2.2 more requests before a repeat than an even split; short and long earn marginal skeletons only past roughly request 17 to 20. Measure the real length distribution to replace the assumed regimes. open UW-C117
    AL-182 2026-08-09 Length-demand analysis, intake trace (proposal section 2b.3a) product Kid auto-approve hardcodes length=Length.SHORT band-agnostically (api/story_requests.py:508), which steers exactly the requests where nobody is choosing into the thinnest-demand tier, and for the teen bands it is an outright production bug: 13-16 and 16+ have no short cell by ADR-011 design and hold no short skeletons, so candidates_for_cell returns empty and the authoring plan 422s every time for a teen-band child with auto-approve enabled. Separately, ADR-011's band-by-length rule is enforced nowhere in the request path (the approve body validates band against style only, and both length selectors render all three lengths for every band), so a guardian can approve a long 3-5 book or a short 13-16 book and hit the same empty-cell failure later. Make the auto-approve length band-aware (reuse the _length_of teen fallback rather than a constant) and default it to the demand-weighted tier rather than short; add band-by-length validation to the approve and authored-create bodies and gate the frontend length options by band, so an unservable cell is rejected at intake rather than 422ing at planning time. open UW-C118
    AL-183 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot on the-midnight-museum (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) scale Bounded mutation of a single parent is not a catalog multiplier, and the reason generalizes: every accepted mutant preserved 100% of the parent's <<FILL>> beat directives (95 of 95 nodes and beats across all three mutants), so mutation moves the graph while recognition is driven by the scene content and decision semantics that live in the beats. Shape-preserving operators scored structural distance exactly 0.0000 (M1 on all five swap pairs tried, including cross-act), the shape-changing M4 reconvergence variant reached 0.0038, and a maximum-length chain using only the parent's own material reached 0.0064, all below the committed TAU_CELL of 0.05, while hand-authored same-cell siblings sit at a median of 0.390. A rater landed the same-book verdict at reading position 3 (score 2.0) with the structural difference invisible until position 8. Applicability was not the constraint: M4 had 2,201 eligible sites. Withdraw per-request mutation as a diversity lever and fund catalog depth instead; if a structural multiplier is still wanted, test cross-skeleton recombination (M3 graft) separately, since the only mutant to clear the floor did so by importing 32 nodes from a different skeleton, which scales with pairs of existing skeletons rather than replacing the need for them. open UW-C119
    AL-184 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot, deterministic bench (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) validator structural_distance and the committed TAU_CELL floor were vindicated on this evidence rather than refuted: the floor rejected exactly the mutants the reader rejected, and the metric's ordering matched the reader's ordering. The metric's known order-blindness and self-declared topology weighting did not bite because no bounded mutant came within an order of magnitude of the threshold. This is not a general exoneration, since the failure mode it cannot see (identical scene function at identical position) is precisely what a mutant preserves. Keep TAU_CELL as the anti-clone gate for catalog-time work, and continue to treat structural_distance as unsuitable for judging perceived diversity between artifacts that already share authored content; revisit only if a candidate approaches the threshold. open the code half merged with PR #661 (4e1a08bc, 2026-08-10), but this lesson's evidence is the 2026-08-09 review write-up on PR #662, which has not merged; it flips to applied on that merge SHA
    AL-185 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot, sibling-fill measurement (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) process A sibling-convergence measurement is invalid when one author writes both arms: the second book's first draft was a sentence-level world swap of the first at 302 shared grams per 1000, and two deliberate de-convergence passes only brought it to 70.4, still an order of magnitude above the 4.0 budget and roughly three times the worst previously measured arm. The number therefore measures the author's de-convergence effort rather than the condition under test. Separately, the recognition rating could not be run in an isolated agent in this environment, so it is author-scored rather than blind. Require an independence protocol for any sibling-fill arm: one isolated author per arm with no sight of the other arms, and a rater that has not seen the generation plan.     AL-186 2026-08-10 PR #662 review fix, band-median re-measurement process Three of the six uniform-random-walk band medians reported in the 2026-08-09 review (5-8, 13-16, 16+) did not reproduce on re-measurement, and the review recorded the gap as unexplained. The cause was not a measurement error: the band work was done in a local working directory and never committed, so only the resulting numbers reached the document while the script, its inputs and its metric choice were left behind. A figure whose producer is uncommitted cannot be re-derived, cannot be re-checked when a rule changes underneath it, and forces a later reader to treat honest drift as an unexplained contradiction. Any quantitative figure quoted in a review or proposal cites a committed producer: a script under scripts/ plus the exact invocation, or a committed output artifact. Where the measurement is one-off, commit the script that produced it in the same PR as the document that quotes it, even if it is never run again. open UW-C124
    AL-187 2026-08-10 PR #662 review fix, strict-census re-measurement in a fresh worktree tooling A git worktree does not share the primary tree's virtualenv, and uv run creates a bare one rather than failing, so the strict census re-run reported 0 of 61 skeletons passing. That reads as a dramatic regression against the recorded 2 of 61, but every one of the 61 runs had died at import on ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'sqlalchemy' before the checker executed a single rule. The tell is that every input failed identically: a catalog-wide measurement that returns the same verdict for every member is an environment fault, not a finding, and a census loop that only records pass/fail cannot tell the two apart. Have the census path distinguish 'the checker did not start' from 'the skeleton failed the bar': catch the non-rule exit separately and abort the whole run rather than scoring it as a failure. Add to the worktree instructions that uv sync --all-extras is required before any measurement run, not only before tests. open UW-C125
    AL-188 2026-08-09 Pre-rebuild review, strict-mode implementation (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) tooling load_skeleton ran the full gate and then threw the ValidationReport away unless it blocked, so check_skeleton.py, the tool every authoring wave gates shells with, printed a clean ok for skeletons the gate had warned about. None of these was visible in any authoring log because the loader dropped the report on the floor; the script even re-implemented one warning (below-cell-min) by hand, which made the omission of the other nine look deliberate.     AL-189 2026-08-09 Pre-rebuild review, strict-mode implementation (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) validator Arming the CG choice-grammar family on a skeleton shell revealed that CG-4 can never pass one: it checks content-word overlap between a choice label and the opening sentence of the target node's body, and on a shell every body is a <<FILL ...>> directive, so the overlap is empty by construction and CG-4 fires on every choice of every shell. The family was designed against filled stories and nothing documented which CG rules are shell-safe, so the first strict run failed the demo fixture on ten findings of which six were structurally meaningless. CG-1 (run cap), CG-2 (option bounds) and CG-3 (words per stop, which reads declared FILL word targets) are all shell-meaningful; only CG-4 requires prose. This is the same shape RL-13 hit before AL-006 taught it to skip FILL bodies. Applied: check_skeleton.py --strict escalates CG-1..CG-3 and deliberately excludes CG-4 with an in-code rationale; CG-4 belongs to the fill gate. When the grammar is armed for filled stories (UW-C24 flip), consider teaching CG-4 to skip FILL bodies outright, the AL-006 precedent, so a partially filled draft cannot fire it spuriously either. open delivered on the story-diversity work, currently OPEN on PR #661; the earlier SHAs live only on a scratch branch that will never merge, so this flips to applied on the merge commit
    AL-190 2026-08-09 Reconvergence hard-gate ruling implementation (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) validator A reconvergence cap designed from the aggregate (corpus mean max in-degree 7.79 vs the research anchor 1.5) would have banned two whole topology families, because the aggregate mixes two opposite mechanisms. Measured per topology before implementing: open_map hubs sit at median max in-degree 9 with one legitimate hub at 126 (the-blackwood-sanatorium, every room re-enters the hub by design) and loop_and_grow at 5, while the pathological corridor-collapse funnels live in the tree-like topologies (branch_and_bottleneck clone pair at 31, the-cinder-bazaar at 21). A blanket per-band cap tight enough to catch the funnels excludes every open map; one loose enough to admit open maps catches nothing. The gate had to be topology-aware to mean anything: cap branch_and_bottleneck/gauntlet/sorting_hat/time_cave at 4/4/6/6/8/8 by band, exempt the hub topologies whose re-entry is the design, and leave those to the SQ-15 per-path metrics. The general shape: a structural quality gate calibrated on a corpus-wide distribution silently encodes the assumption that one mechanism produced the distribution. Applied: topology-aware max in-degree hard cap in check_skeleton.py --strict with the exemption rationale in-code; when the strict checks are promoted into validator/ after the grandfathered catalog is removed, carry the topology awareness into the catalogued rule rather than flattening it to one number. open delivered on the story-diversity work, currently OPEN on PR #661; the earlier SHAs live only on a scratch branch that will never merge, so this flips to applied on the merge commit
    AL-191 2026-08-09 Strict-bar conformance census after the 2026-08-09 rulings (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) process An advisory rule family left inert since landing accumulates near-universal non-conformance, and the flip cost grows monotonically while nobody watches. Arming CG-1..CG-3 inside the strict bar and censusing the catalog showed 57 of 61 skeletons breach CG-2 and 48 breach CG-3: the entire catalog predates the grammar's own bar because enforce_grammar defaulted False from the day the family landed (UW-C24) and no production caller ever opted in, so every authoring wave since added non-conforming trees at zero visible cost. The full strict census passes 2 of 61, meaning the ruled bar implies a near-total rebuild; that number was invisible until the rules were armed against the corpus even once. The general shape: an inert gate is not a deferred decision, it is a debt meter running with the display off, and the census that reads the meter costs one afternoon against a rebuild-sized principal. Applied for this flip: the census ran before any removal and its numbers plus a remove-on-replacement sequencing recommendation are recorded in the 2026-08-09 review (Part 4 census section), so the removal wave is sized by data.     AL-192 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot (branch claude/skeleton-story-review-3zy6tq) tooling PL-23 only reveals the derived reading clock inside a failure message, so a declared estimated_minutes that is wrong but within the 25% tolerance never surfaces the true number, and every out-of-tolerance miss costs a full draft-gate iteration.     AL-193 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, three cells with independent adversarial review process Strict-clean does not mean approvable, and the gap is systematic rather than incidental: three drafts in three different cells each passed check_skeleton --strict with zero findings at any severity, and each was independently REJECTED on adversarial review, on defects no deterministic layer measures (label laundering, positional fate bias, consequence-free choices, merge contradictions, quota-shaped design). The gate verifies topology, budgets, and quota arithmetic; the rejections were all about outcomes, consequences, and continuity.     AL-194 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of all three drafts validator The outcome gates read kind/valence tags, not outcomes, so a draft can satisfy PL-24, the walk floor, and the outcome-spread audit purely by tag distribution over scenes the tags misdescribe. All three pilot drafts did it, two of them unconsciously: the 3-5 draft narrates one identical terminal scene under four kind/valence combinations, one tag contradicting its own beats; the 10-13 draft tags a full mission success neutral and ten failures as discovery; the 13-16 draft has sixteen everyone-survives rescues tagged negative setback and twelve off-page abandonments tagged neutral, plus role= derived mechanically from valence so twelve endings instruct the fill to write a discovery where nothing is discovered.     AL-195 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of the two large drafts validator Choice-level fairness and consequence are invisible to every current metric, and generated drafts fail both by construction. Position bias: in the 10-13 draft the third option leads to an ending 77% of the time and the first option never does; in the 13-16 draft always-pick-option-1 walks to the single best ending with zero risk, because the generator ordered options safe-first in every fan. Consequence: 25 decision nodes in the 10-13 draft have sibling choices with byte-identical reachable-ending sets, and staged merges collapse 98 ancestors into one node while never exceeding direct in-degree 5, laundering exactly the funnel the in-degree cap was ruled to stop. Add computable consequence checks to the strict bar: per-position ending-kind distribution with a skew ceiling plus an argmax-position walk that must not reach a positive ending unscathed; flag sibling choices with identical reachable-ending sets; extend the reconvergence gate with a k-hop ancestor-funnel metric. Generators must seeded-shuffle option order. open UW-C128
    AL-196 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, adversarial critiques of the two large drafts validator Merge-node beat contradictions, the known the-hollow-lighthouse defect class, are reproduced systematically by generation rather than occasionally by hand: eight hard upstream-vs-beat contradictions in each large draft, including endings narrating scenes from choices the reader explicitly declined, characters referenced at merges who are introduced only on other routes, gear appearing from nowhere, and one terminal beat hedging 'whichever stranger you trusted' because the generator knew its parents disagreed. Tier-1 has no state, so every fact a merge-node beat presupposes must hold on every inbound path, and nothing checks it.     AL-198 2026-08-09 Strict-bar drafting pilot, both large-cell drafters' friction logs validator Several strict constraints are individually reasonable but couple destructively, and the couplings, not the values, dictated the drafts' shape: CG-3's stop ceiling equals roughly two to three nodes at the band words mean (a lone corridor node at the 10-13 mean is already illegal before a normal-weight successor; at 13-16 every checkpoint merge lives within ten words of the wall), so both drafts near-eliminated corridors and one emitted stops at 148-150 of the 150 cap, leaving zero fill headroom; CG-2's exact (3,3) at 8-13 bans the binary dilemma and forces a third strategy at every one of 100+ decisions, which is where template-sameness pressure originates; the 25% gamebook endings fraction makes terminal leaves the only legal padding, pushing toward the shallow-ending sprawl other rules exist to prevent; PL-26's gamebook ceiling of 4.0 is unmotivated by its own comment yet strict-blocking; PL-24's 60% kind ceiling quantizes harshly at four or fewer endings, silently raising the real endings floor for small kid books. Also adopt the headroom discipline: generators target at most 90% of any hard cap, and strict warns at 98%. open UW-C131

    AL-201 2026-08-09 Same-skeleton diversity experiment: three isolated fills of the-lost-mitten under maximally different theme bindings (review Part 6) validator A same-skeleton story pair has a perceived-similarity floor of 0.50 before any prose exists (PS weights 0.5 leaf + 0.3 structural + 0.2 theme; structural is 1.0 for a same-tree pair by construction and theme was 1.0 because binding never re-themes metadata.themes), and the empirical best case, three independent authors filling three maximally different bindings with near-minimal leaf similarity (0.18-0.20, body trigram Jaccard 0.026-0.029), landed at PS 0.59-0.60 against the 0.70 repeat threshold.     AL-203 2026-08-09 Same-skeleton diversity experiment (review Part 6) process Beats encode world facts the slot system does not govern, and the fill contract's reskin rule is ambiguous enough that three independent authors resolved the same conflicts three different ways: the skeleton's un-slotted snow was imported into an autumn park by one author and reskinned to dew by another; 'one bare hand is cold' was inverted to a hot bare head because the bound item was a sunhat; and one binding produced 'fireflies chirped' because the beat verb belonged to the legacy birds, a world-consistency defect no gate catches.     AL-204 2026-08-09 Opus pipeline analysis for the narrative redesign (review of every FILL-directive consumer) tooling Every consumer of the FILL directive fails open on a parse miss: binding.py, fidelity.py, fidelity_review.py, and slotted_surfaces.py all skip the node when the anchored regex does not match, and three more private regex copies live in mutation/bundle.py, mutation/operators.py, and parameterize_skeleton.py.     AL-205 2026-08-09 Opus format-design analysis, re-expressing the-lost-mitten node by node validator A node with an in-edge from its own descendant is re-rendered verbatim on every loop, and nothing tells the prose author: the-lost-mitten's three abandon-the-lead choices all target n_start, whose beat stages the loss as fresh news, so all three experiment fills wrote a discovery beat and a child who loops back reads the oh-no moment again on every return.     AL-221 2026-08-09 Prose-craft detector calibration (proposal section 13, commit 4b93b97) validator The prose-craft detectors separate publishable from unpublishable books perfectly, but the separation is IN-SAMPLE by construction: every threshold (--max-unstable-nodes 0, --min-tense-cues 6, --max-node-mix 0.34, --max-moral-tags 0, --max-told-per-1000 0.5) was tuned against the same nine books it is reported to separate, so the result measures fit rather than predictive power. Two thresholds are already known to be fragile: the told-emotion rate ranks one unpublishable book (0.31 per 1000) BELOW one shippable book (0.35), so that detector cannot rank those two and the book is caught only by the other two detectors; and the moral-tag budget of zero is unfalsifiable on a corpus where every shippable book scored zero.     AL-223 2026-08-09 Per-child sibling-exposure analysis (proposal section 2b) product A skeleton is consumable within a child but reusable across children, and nothing in the code contradicts that (no global or cross-family anti-repeat exists; visibility='catalog' actively amortizes one fill across families). Required catalog therefore scales with per-child lifetime consumption inside a band, roughly tenure x rate spread over that band's cells, and is INDEPENDENT of user count, which makes catalog growth a bounded one-time capital cost rather than a cost that grows with the business. Measured need is about 130 skeletons at 0.5 stories per child per month, 408 at 1, and 816 at 2, against 57 production-eligible today. Treat catalog build as bounded capital and size it against measured demand; instrument per-child stories per month per band, which is the single number that sets the target and is not measured in this deployment. open UW-C155
    AL-224 2026-08-09 Per-child sibling-exposure analysis (proposal section 2b) scale Every populated production cell holds 3 or 4 skeletons across all six bands, so a child exhausts a cell by their 4th request in it and every later story in that cell is with certainty a tree they have already read; a repeat is more likely than not by request 3 or 4, and rotating across a band's length cells buys about two extra requests rather than an order of magnitude. This, not the skeleton format, is the measured driver of the sameness the three diversity pilots characterized, and it retires the case for replacing the skeleton architecture.     AL-227 2026-08-09 Length-demand catalog sizing (scripts/analyze_sibling_exposure.py, proposal section 2b.3a) scale Story-length demand is not flat within a band and the catalog is: every populated cell holds 3 to 4 skeletons while demand concentrates on medium. Because skeleton_matches_cell treats only a NULL length as a wildcard and 0 of 58 production-eligible skeletons declare one, cells are hard partitions, so surplus in the short and long cells is stranded capital that can never serve a medium request. Peaking demand leaves the band TOTAL essentially unchanged (one child's band-lifetime consumption however it splits) but raises the SHORTFALL by 23 to 32% and moves the binding constraint to the medium cell in every band, pulling the first likely repeat forward about one request (P(repeat by request 4) at 8-11 rises from 0.386 flat to 0.647 strongly-medium-weighted). Author against the demand curve rather than filling cells evenly: put the first 6 to 12 skeletons of each band's budget entirely into that band's medium cell, which buys 0.8 to 2.2 more requests before a repeat than an even split; short and long earn marginal skeletons only past roughly request 17 to 20.     AL-228 2026-08-09 Length-demand analysis, intake trace (proposal section 2b.3a) product Kid auto-approve hardcodes length=Length.SHORT band-agnostically (api/story_requests.py:508), which steers exactly the requests where nobody is choosing into the thinnest-demand tier, and for the teen bands it is an outright production bug: 13-16 and 16+ have no short cell by ADR-011 design and hold no short skeletons, so candidates_for_cell returns empty and the authoring plan 422s every time for a teen-band child with auto-approve enabled.     AL-229 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot on the-midnight-museum (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) scale Bounded mutation of a single parent is not a catalog multiplier, and the reason generalizes: every accepted mutant preserved 100% of the parent's <<FILL>> beat directives (95 of 95 nodes and beats across all three mutants), so mutation moves the graph while recognition is driven by the scene content and decision semantics that live in the beats.     AL-230 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot, deterministic bench (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) validator structural_distance and the committed TAU_CELL floor were vindicated on this evidence rather than refuted: the floor rejected exactly the mutants the reader rejected, and the metric's ordering matched the reader's ordering. The metric's known order-blindness and self-declared topology weighting did not bite because no bounded mutant came within an order of magnitude of the threshold. This is not a general exoneration, since the failure mode it cannot see (identical scene function at identical position) is precisely what a mutant preserves.     AL-231 2026-08-09 Per-request mutation pilot, sibling-fill measurement (alternatives proposal section 11.5.1) process A sibling-convergence measurement is invalid when one author writes both arms: the second book's first draft was a sentence-level world swap of the first at 302 shared grams per 1000, and two deliberate de-convergence passes only brought it to 70.4, still an order of magnitude above the 4.0 budget and roughly three times the worst previously measured arm. The number therefore measures the author's de-convergence effort rather than the condition under test. Separately, the recognition rating could not be run in an isolated agent in this environment, so it is author-scored rather than blind.     AL-232 2026-08-10 Obligation-variance experiment, contract variant review (docs/planning/evidence/obligation-variance/) process An admissibility bar that enumerates fields silently exempts every field it omits. The bar for a contract variant required divergence in 2 of establishes, choice_semantics, affect, and function; two variants passed it at 26/26 nodes on all three pairs while their beat_hint strings were the shipped contract's sentences with single nouns swapped, byte-identical at n_end_library across all three variants, at or above 0.85 similarity at 10 of 26 nodes against v1, and above 0.60 at 22 of 26. beat_hint is the most direct instruction a fill agent reads, so the intervention the experiment exists to test was largely undelivered; because a null result was the expected outcome, the run would have been uninterpretable, since "obligation variance does not work" could not have been separated from "the obligations were never varied." Gate variant admissibility with check_variant_divergence.py --check, which adds a beat_hint similarity ceiling (0.60 difflib) against every other variant including the shipped one. More generally, when a bar enumerates fields, state which fields it does NOT cover, so an exemption is a decision rather than an oversight. open UW-C166
    AL-233 2026-08-10 Obligation-variance experiment, blind recognition rating (spec section 12) product Obligation variance is refuted as the diversity lever, and the refutation identifies the actual fingerprint: recognition landed at position 2 against a pre-registered margin of past position 5, with distinctness 2.0 against the control's 2.5, while all six quality guards passed.     AL-237 2026-08-10 Obligation-variance experiment, arm C isolation disclosure and arm D discretion (spec section 12.6) process Isolation between parallel authoring agents leaks through the shared filesystem, and identical briefs still produce asymmetric effort.     AL-238 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, blind relabelling (docs/planning/decision-variance-experiment-spec-2026-08-10.md section 8.2) process A metric computed from self-declared labels cannot be measured by the party declaring the labels.     AL-239 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, instrument construction (spec section 8.2) validator A free-text action field makes an exact-repetition bar vacuous, because the surface changing while the decision does not IS the defect being measured. Two contracts scored 0 of 35 identical action strings while a blind annotator judged 34 of 35 the same decision: set_the_dial_deliberately against set_the_levers_deliberately is one act and two strings. Separately, six of eighteen nodes offered a single option; a one-option node is a page turn rather than a decision, and including them pulled the aggregate family rate to 0.486, inside its 0.6 ceiling, while n_vault and n_setjam sat at 1.00 and the three principal forks at 0.67 to 0.75.     AL-240 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, two authoring attempts on a fixed graph (spec section 8.3) product Decisions appear to be pinned by the graph together with fact-graph closure, not merely by authorial habit. Two attempts to author genuinely different decisions over one unchanged 26-node graph in one unchanged world, the second explicitly instructed to move tradeoff and consequence rather than the verb and iterated against a metric, produced 28 of 28 options that a blind annotator called the same decision. The single fork that did move, n_open, is the one with no incoming constraint. The mechanism is that each fork's children must deliver what their own descendants require and merge closure propagates those requirements backward, so the kind of thing a branch accomplishes is fixed even when every prop and sentence changes. Treat the verifiability/freedom tension as evidenced rather than hypothetical: a shape-only skeleton is decision-free only if its entry and exit facts are also free, and free facts cannot be merge-verified before prose. A decision-program compiler must therefore generate the fact graph and the decisions together rather than assigning decisions onto a fixed fact skeleton. Retest on a production-eligible graph before treating the magnitude as settled. open UW-C174
    AL-241 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, run termination (spec sections 8, 8.5) process Terminating an experiment at the point the intervention cannot be constructed is cheaper and more informative than running it to a rating. This run stopped before any fill because blind annotation showed the treatment was not delivered, which saved two fills and a rating and produced a sharper finding than a null rating would have: not that varying decisions fails to help, but that varying them on a fixed graph is much harder than assumed. A null rating would have been uninterpretable in exactly the way AL-232 describes. Add a delivery check to every intervention spec: an independent verification, before any measurement is spent, that the intervention actually differs from the control on the dimension it claims to vary. Make it a standing precondition rather than a per-experiment afterthought. open UW-C175
    AL-242 2026-08-10 Metric build, retention exemption review (supabase/migrations/20260810000000_*, publishing/state_machine.py) tooling A docstring's claim about the codebase rots, and reasoning from it silently produced a wrong production predicate. publishing/state_machine.py said the draft --auto_reject--> needs_revision hop "has no slice-1 caller"; slice 2 landed and moderation/pipeline.py drives it on a classifier hard BLOCK.     AL-243 2026-08-10 Metric build, inter-annotator agreement measurement (docs/planning/decision-signature-labelling-principle.md) process Independent annotation of decision signatures is reliable, which corrects the diagnosis implied by AL-238. Two annotators given only a contract and a written convention, with no knowledge of what was being compared, reached Fleiss' kappa 0.796 on action_family, 0.816 on target_role, and 0.758 on both tradeoff and consequence over 35 choices, all clearing the 0.60 floor.     AL-244 2026-08-10 Metric build, branch-obligation checker (scripts/check_branch_obligations.py) tooling A deterministic property of the plan reproduced two results that had each cost a full measurement round. It needs no model, no annotation and no prose, so it runs in milliseconds before a fill is committed. Its limits are real: it was validated on two known-bad pairs with no known-good example, so it is a screen rather than an arbiter and its threshold is uncalibrated. Run the deterministic screen before spending any measurement on an artifact, and look for a computable-from-the-plan proxy before commissioning a fill or a rating in future experiments.     AL-246 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, v5 arm rating (spec section 9) tooling Device collision between two books in one world dominates a reader's verdict and can invalidate a whole measurement round.     AL-247 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, v5 contract (spec section 9) validator The branch-obligation screen has a measured false negative, which corrects the framing in AL-244.     AL-248 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, rating instrument (spec section 9) process The five-question instrument is mostly dead weight on a shared skeleton, and two blind raters found the same missing question without conferring. Q1 (same kinds of actions) and Q4 (repeated sequence) both saturated at 5 for both pairs, because with one node skeleton across all three books those answers are fixed before a word is read; Q2 came out a wash or mildly against the eventual verdict, leaving the whole judgement resting on Q3. Both raters independently proposed the same sixth item, whether solving the first book gives you the answer to the second, and both said it would have separated the pairs immediately.     AL-249 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, rating instrument (spec section 9) product Two blind raters independently reported a per-book defect that the pair framing structurally hides: the n_inside four-room fork has no consequence in any of the three books, every room yields the same pattern and every road reconverges at n_clockface, and roughly seven of nine forks on this pilot graph either do not branch or reconverge one node later. Because the defect is identical in all three books it scores the same everywhere and cancels out of every pairwise comparison, so a diversity programme measuring only pairs can run indefinitely without seeing it. Both raters then attached a product judgment to that structural fact, calling it illusory choice and a likely threat to the reading experience. The owner rejected that judgment: reconverging exploration paths are a convention of the form. The lesson is therefore about raters over-reaching from an accurate structural observation into a product call that is not theirs, which is the same class of error as the earlier misreading of the central defect. Add a per-book illusory-choice check before any pair comparison: flag every fork whose branches reconverge with no difference in what they deliver, computable from the contract as branches whose obligations are equal and whose subgraphs rejoin within N nodes. Report it per book, never per pair. rejected owner ruling, decision-variance-experiment-spec-2026-08-10.md section 9.9: loop-back exploration is a convention of the form, not a flaw, on the tabletop analogy that sweeping every room is the play. The structural observation stands; the defect framing does not, and no gate will be built.

    AL-252 2026-08-10 Decision-variance experiment, rating (spec sections 9.3, 9.4) product Varying the act a choice asks for does reduce perceived decision repetition, thinly, and the reader-invented question is what detects it.     AL-253 2026-08-10 Diversity test register, D-3 (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) validator The signature instrument was annotating the wrong artifact, which is a layer error rather than a vocabulary error.
