    AL-254 2026-08-10 Diversity test register, D-3b and D-3c (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) validator Annotating the binding alongside the contract recovers the property readers respond to, and the recovery is real but fragile. With each plan's bound devices attached, reasoning_kind becomes the only field of six that orders the two book pairs as readers did, and the pre-registered primary (that field over the six forks the book's code runs through) passed under both annotators, as did the prediction that the entry forks would invert. But the magnitude collapsed on replication, 1.000 against 0.000 for one annotator and 0.333 against 0.167 for the other, and over the full option set the second annotator ties. The cause is one underspecified vocabulary boundary: kappa on reasoning_kind is 1.000 on the arithmetic plan and 0.919 on the pictogram plan but 0.719 on the rhythm plan, because decoding a symbolic notation sits between COMPUTE and MATCH and the definitions do not say which. That single call decides whether the control's two books share a reasoning kind. Write the COMPUTE/MATCH boundary explicitly for symbolic decoding, and treat the rewrite as a reliability fix only. Re-scoring these same three plans under a sharpened rule cannot re-confirm the hypothesis, because the sharpening determines the decisive item by construction and a motivated author would choose the direction that rescues the result. Validity has to be retested on artifacts the vocabulary has never seen. open UW-C185
    AL-255 2026-08-10 Diversity test register, commit 2b854d5 process Chaining a file edit and a git commit in one shell invocation lets a failed edit commit anyway, and the commit message then describes content that is not in the diff. A Python heredoc doing the edit raised AssertionError on an anchor that no longer matched; because the commit was a separate command in the same invocation rather than chained to the edit's exit status, it ran and pushed three label files under a message claiming to record a result and pre-register the follow-up analysis, neither of which was present. Caught on the next read of the file, corrected by a follow-up commit rather than a history rewrite, since the bad commit was already pushed. Never chain an edit and its commit in one invocation. Verify the staged diff against the intended message before committing, and make anchor-based edits fail loudly enough to stop the sequence. This matters more than ordinary commit hygiene here because these commits are the evidence record for experiments whose results nobody can reproduce from memory. open UW-C186
    AL-256 2026-08-10 D-2 fills, arm P (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) process The presence of an agent's output file is not evidence the agent has finished, and acting on one mid-run silently corrupts the work.     AL-257 2026-08-10 D-2 fills, cross-arm convergence (register, D-2 section) product Same-model idiom convergence reaches proper nouns and titles, not just phrasing, and it survives strict isolation. Three ending titles collided across arms, one of them (Goodnight Through the Door) in all three at the same node.     AL-258 2026-08-10 D-2 guard battery (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product Sharing one narrative contract across books makes the contract's own wording the fingerprint. Three isolated authors writing from one contract and three non-colliding bindings produced 653 and 717 shared 4-grams per pair, 59.2 and 63.8 per 1000 words against a budget of 4.0, and 51 and 41 identical choice-menu openings out of 131. The pilot, whose books scored 1.8 to 2.7 per 1000 and zero shared menus, differed in exactly one respect: its arms read different contracts, whose choice_semantics were 0 of 35 identical, where D-2's three arms read 131 of 131 identical. The first diagnosis, that the contract was lexically over-prescriptive, was tested and refuted: labels reuse a distinctive word from their own semantics at 46.6 percent in D-2 against 54.3 percent in the pilot, so per-book reuse is higher in the non-converging case. It is sharing, not prescriptiveness. Any architecture that reuses one plan across many books must treat the plan's own prose as a convergence source and either neutralise choice_semantics wording, generate it per book, or require authors to diverge from it explicitly. This bears directly on every proposed architecture built on a reusable plan object. For D-2 specifically the arm design was wrong: it mirrored the pilot's arm structure while varying only bindings, where the pilot varied contracts. open UW-C188
    AL-259 2026-08-10 D-2 guard battery, reading level validator Reading level degrades badly at production scale and the gate cannot stop it, because every finding is advisory. Model tier is a candidate but not established: one pilot book written on the stronger tier also sat out of band at 7.45, so length and node count are confounded with tier. Make aggregate reading level a blocking condition rather than 85 advisory warnings nobody reads: a book with four fifths of its nodes outside the band is not age-appropriate whatever the per-node rule says.     AL-260 2026-08-10 D-4, scripts/check_solution_transfer.py validator A rarity threshold calibrated on a large corpus silently inverts when applied to a subset. The device-collision check treats a word used by at most two props as rare, calibrated over 24-prop bindings. Reused inside a 6-prop and then a 2-prop solution chain, the corpus shrinks to four props and very nearly every content word qualifies, so the signal fires on everything. It reported three plainly different waypoint marks, a scratched star, a painted spiral and an inked triangle, as the same device, on the shared words logbook's, mark and margins, which are the contract's own framing for that slot rather than any book's device. Judge rarity over the whole artifact a threshold was calibrated on, never over the subset being scored, and state the corpus explicitly in any measure that inherits a calibrated threshold. Audit every other measure that reuses a calibrated constant on a filtered input. open UW-C190
    AL-261 2026-08-10 D-4, operation tiers against the 101-node bindings validator A word-list classifier for "what kind of thinking does this puzzle ask" ranks on the artifacts it was written from and collapses on any other. It reproduced three known reader rankings on the pilot, then classified 2 of 6 chain props on an unseen contract. Two failure modes, neither fixable by adding words: no negation, so "a page of small hand-drawn icons instead of numbers" scores as arithmetic; and polysemy, so "a short tail" on a drawn symbol scores as rhythm. The one cross-arm match it did report was an artifact of the slot, since every waypoint mark in that world is a drawn symbol. This is the second measure in this programme good enough to rank and not good enough to gate, after the fill-fidelity triage. Stop building lexical proxies for semantic properties in this programme; the expected outcome is now a ranking aid, not a gate. Where a property must be checkable, declare it as a field in the narrative contract rather than inferring it from the prose: the plan schema needs an explicit operation field. open UW-C191
    AL-262 2026-08-10 D-6 rig construction (docs/planning/evidence/d6-contract-sharing/build.py) product Choice semantics cannot be neutralised mechanically from the fact graph, because the fact graph does not contain the decision. Deriving each branch's semantics from the facts its destination presupposes needs no author and so cannot smuggle in a voice, which is why it was tried first. It also destroys the fork: at the pilot's central four-way decision, answering the code, forcing the dial, going round the back and guessing at random all own exactly the same obligation and flatten to one identical sentence. This is AL-247 reached from the opposite direction, and it means the D-2 repair that looked cheapest and most objective is unavailable. Any "flatten the plan's wording" repair must be authored under a stated rule and treated as itself under test, not derived. More generally, stop proposing fact-graph derivations for properties that D-3 and AL-247 have already located above the fact layer. open UW-C192
    AL-263 2026-08-10 Q-1 catalog count (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) process The catalog's depth was being counted in the wrong unit, and nobody had counted either. 61 skeletons over 17 band-by-length cells, 13 of them holding four or fewer, so the exhaustion premise holds. But 2 of 61 skeletons carry a narrative contract, which is the artifact every measure in this programme and every proposed architecture actually needs, at roughly 1.7KB per node of hand authoring. Whether that cost is per skeleton or per book is decided by AL-258, so a research result decides which capital purchase this is. Track narrative-contract coverage as a first-class catalog metric alongside skeleton count, and do not scope any depth purchase until the contract-sharing question is settled. open UW-C193
    AL-264 2026-08-10 D-6 (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product Contract sharing is confirmed as a cause of cross-book convergence, and neither cheap repair fixes it. Holding graph, bindings, model and isolation constant, moving two arms from separate contracts to one moves shared 4-grams from 2.9 to 17.2 per 1000 against a budget of 4.0. The "changing only" framing this row originally used is withdrawn (2026-08-12): two separate contracts differ in premise, obligations and fact set as well as in document count, so the 5.9x measures sharing a plan as a bundle rather than the document-sharing channel alone, and the 2.9 is the pilot's rig rather than a fourth D-6 arm. The cause claim survives; only the isolation claim was too strong. Flattening the shared wording reaches 11.8 and instructing authors to diverge from it reaches 13.6, both still around three times budget; at one pair per condition their ordering flips depending on whether labels are counted, so neither is better, both are insufficient. Figures corrected 2026-08-11: published as 16.9, 11.4 and 12.9, re-derived from the frozen artifacts at body-only scope as 17.2, 11.8 and 13.6, with the 2.9 control reproducing exactly. See AL-296 and the research brief's 16g.1 correction block; the conclusion is unchanged. Treat AL-258 as established and stop proposing wording-level repairs as sufficient. The third repair is no longer untested and the prediction held. D-7 generated everything below a shared stratum per book, the decisional stratum included, and landed at 13.6, still more than three times budget; D-7b then showed the operative variable was the prose in the shared half rather than what was generated per book, reaching 2.3 by deleting 422 words and changing nothing else (AL-282, and AL-284 for the correction to its stated mechanism). Per-book generation of the decisional stratum is therefore ruled out as sufficient on its own, and the open question moved to which shared prose matters. Do not reopen this row's prediction as pending work; the successor experiment is the third arm named in AL-284. open UW-C194
    AL-265 2026-08-10 D-6 gram attribution (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product The convergence leak has at least four channels and choice_semantics is not the main one. Tracing shared grams to the contract field they draw on: choice_semantics under half, beat_hint similar, premise as much or more, and about a quarter tracing to no contract field at all, which is same-model idiom (AL-257) that no plan-level intervention reaches. Categories overlap, so this is indicative rather than a partition. Two consequences: a reusable plan that shares the dramatic question is already sharing the largest traceable channel, and there is an idiom floor bounding what a shared-language guard can ever certify. Move premise into the per-book stratum alongside choice_semantics and beat_hint, and set the shared-gram budget for reusable-plan architectures with the idiom floor measured rather than assumed.     AL-266 2026-08-10 D-6 confound check process A cross-experiment comparison inherited a second difference that nearly invalidated it, and the check that saved it was cheap. The pilot's shells shipped draft choice labels, 13 of 35 identical across its arms, where D-6's authors wrote every label from scratch, so the baseline and the treatment differed in two ways rather than one. Splitting the measure by prose part settled it in one run: labels contribute zero shared 4-grams in every condition including the pilot, so the whole signal is in bodies, which were authored from scratch throughout. Before comparing a new run against a historical baseline, diff the two authoring tasks and not only the artifacts, and where a confound is found try to partition the measure rather than caveating the result. open UW-C196
    AL-267 2026-08-10 D-6 addendum, generator idiom floor product The shared-gram budget of 4.0 was never validated against what the generator can actually achieve, and it turns out to be just above it. Measured on book pairs sharing nothing but the model and the age band (different graph, different contract, different world), the floor is 3.3 shared 4-grams per 1000, range 1.9 to 5.0. Two consequences neither of which was known: the budget is reachable, so no architecture fails it on a technicality; and the pilot's one-contract-per-book design scores 2.9, indistinguishable from books sharing nothing, so not converging is already a solved problem and the only open question is whether reuse can be bought back. D-6's repairs sit at 3.6 times the floor, so they failed with a factor of three of headroom unclaimed rather than against a hard limit. The floor also corroborates the D-6 gram attribution independently: a quarter of 17.2 is 4.3, against a directly measured 3.3. (Both figures were restated on 2026-08-11 from 3.5 and "a quarter of 16.9 is 4.2": the original counts joined each book's bodies into one string and so counted 4-grams straddling body boundaries, which do not exist in any body. The conclusion is unchanged and the direction of the error was to overstate convergence.) Record 3.3 as the measured floor beside the 4.0 budget wherever the budget is cited, and re-measure it whenever the generation model changes, since it is a property of the model rather than of any architecture. open UW-C197
    AL-268 2026-08-10 M-2 deterministic pre-test (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product The catalog's large graphs are linear-with-decorations, not worlds, and it shows in their shape rather than in any reading. Removing the hub from each of the 21 graphs at 200-plus nodes and taking the components of the remainder: 18 have one spoke holding nearly every node (the 677-node graph puts 656 of 677 in a single mass) with the rest being 1-to-4-node early exits, so there is no second region from which to cut a disjoint tour. Only the-skyrail-heist (10-13, 246 nodes, spokes of 83/82/77), the-year-of-four-banners and the-tricameral-city are genuinely world-shaped. A first attempt using max-flow node-disjoint paths returned 1 for all 21 and looked like a total refutation; the single cut node is the hub at depth 1 to 5 in every case, which is what a hub-and-spoke world should have, so strict node-disjointness was the wrong requirement. Scope M-2 to the three named graphs rather than to "the largest", and add spoke-balance to the catalog metrics so world-shaped graphs are identifiable without re-deriving it. When a graph-theoretic test returns a uniform extreme across every input, check what the cut object is before reporting the result. open UW-C198
    AL-269 2026-08-10 Q-3 skeleton-free generation (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product An unaided model builds structurally sound story graphs, and fails only the rules nobody gave it. Six authors with no skeleton, no example story and no validator in the loop produced 6 of 6 graphs with zero dangling targets, unreachable nodes, sinks or trapped cycles, at 27 to 35 nodes. All six were then blocked by the project gate, and every blocking finding violates a constraint absent from the brief: branch-depth budget (4 of 6), first-decision floor (2 of 6), ending-object schema (2 of 6). The brief stated the structural rules and the model met all of them. State the band budgets, the opening floor and the ending schema in any generation brief, then re-run to test whether the gap closes; the current result cannot distinguish model incapacity from an incomplete brief, and the brief is the cheaper thing to fix first. open UW-C199
    AL-270 2026-08-10 Q-3 convergence, read against D-6 product Removing the shared plan entirely does not vary the premise, because the model converges there unaided. Six stories sharing no plan sit at the idiom floor on average (3.5 per 1000 against a floor of 3.3), which is the cleanest evidence that D-2 and D-6's convergence came from the shared contract rather than from the model. But 4 of 15 pairs breach the 4.0 budget, and the worst pairs are premise twins: two coastal lost-signal mysteries, two woodland camp discoveries, and all six are 'children find a thing left by an older person and follow clues to it'. D-6 identified premise as the largest traceable leak channel and concluded a reusable plan must vary it per book; Q-3 shows sharing less does not achieve that. Vary the premise with something that actively repels, not by withholding a shared plan and not by instruction. This is the concrete requirement R1-3's repulsion term should be pointed at, alongside the bound solution chain. open UW-C200
    AL-271 2026-08-10 M-4 contract authoring, first attempt (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) process Instructing an author to write independently produces the surface markers of independence and not the substance. A contract briefed with wording-independence as a hard, itemised experimental constraint came back structurally excellent (26 nodes, 52 facts, zero fact-closure violations under an independent reimplementation) with a different world, a different slug and zero fact-name overlap, and at 126.7 shared 4-grams per 1000 with its reference: beat_hint 260.3, world_recipe+safety_envelope 127.5, choice_semantics 96.2. For scale, D-6's worst fill condition was 17.2 (restated 2026-08-11 from 16.9, per the per-body-unit recount noted on AL-267) and the generator floor is 3.3. This is D-6's diverge condition one layer up and far worse: at fill time the same instruction cut traceable grams 45 percent, at contract-authoring time it achieved essentially nothing. The checkable markers were all satisfied while the property was not. Enforce independence by what the author is not shown, not by instruction: supply the format as a written schema and withhold the reference artifact. Note the consequence for R1-3, whose promotion rested on generating a fresh contract per book: fresh-per-book is not independent-per-book. open UW-C201
    AL-272 2026-08-10 M-4 contract authoring, controlled comparison (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product Independence comes from what an author is not shown, not from what it is told. Same task, same shell, same requirement, one variable: with the reference contract visible plus an itemised instruction to diverge from it, a fresh contract shares 126.7 4-grams per 1000 with that reference; with the reference withheld and the format supplied as a written schema instead, 1.0. A 127-fold reduction. Both artifacts are structurally sound on the same independent checks. Instructing divergence is close to useless at the plan-authoring layer and is the intervention every proposal reaches for first. But the premise converged anyway: the withheld-reference author independently chose a clock tower against the reference's clocktower, so withholding closes the wording channel completely and the premise channel not at all (AL-270 a third time). Generate each book's decisional stratum from the structural stratum alone, never from a sibling book, and treat that as the implementable form of AL-258's third repair. Name it explicitly in the architecture re-specification and in R1-3. Handle the premise with a separate repulsion mechanism, since withholding provably does not touch it. open UW-C202
    AL-273 2026-08-10 M-4 confound check (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product Cross-book convergence does not scale with plan similarity; the threshold is sentence identity. Checking a new contract's similarity to the base before using it as an arm turned up that the pilot's two contracts are 118.4 shared 4-grams per 1000, almost exactly as similar as an attempt discarded as unusable at 126.7, and their fills sat at the floor, 2.9. They share vocabulary heavily while sharing 0 of 35 choice_semantics strings. Two authors handed the same sentence converge on it; two handed different sentences sharing vocabulary do not, even at 118 per 1000. This refutes the proportional reading of AL-258, which was mine, and makes the repair far cheaper: a per-book decisional stratum needs only to be separately written, not independent of its siblings. Limit: the pilot's contracts also differ in premise, so sentence-difference and premise-difference are bundled in the one available comparison. Amend the AL-258 and AL-272 guidance to require separate generation rather than independence, and run the disambiguating test: two contracts sharing a premise but not a sentence, filled and measured. That is the cheapest open question in this line. open UW-C203
    AL-274 2026-08-10 M-4 guard battery, scripts/check_fill_integrity.py validator Every shell in this programme now defers the book title, and the fill-integrity checker fails on that by default. Since AL-257, where a shell id leaked one title into three arms, shells carry <<FILL book_title>>, so a filled title necessarily differs from its shell. check_fill_integrity.py counts the top-level title as non-fillable unless --allow-title-rewrite is passed, and reports FAIL structure: filled story differs from skeleton outside node bodies and choice labels (ids, choices, targets, endings, variables, or metadata), which reads like a serious structural defect and is not one. Verified across D-2 arm P, three D-6 fills and the M-4 arm: all fail without the flag, all pass with it, and an independent graph-level comparison confirms the title is the only difference every time. The flag exists for exactly this case per AL-211; the trap is that its default became wrong once title deferral became standard. Teach the checker to treat any field the shell wrote as a <<FILL ...>> directive as fillable, which removes the flag and cannot drift as more fields are deferred. Until then name the flag in the guard-battery runbook. applied scripts/check_fill_integrity.py
    AL-275 2026-08-10 M-4 rating round 1 (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) process A treatment arm's label_style encoded the treatment, making the book identifiable from its labels alone. The stake-economics contract offered name the cost before the reward among its label_styles; the author picked it and all 35 labels began with the word "Spend", against comparison books using neutral styles (terse imperatives, first-person plural resolves). The first blind rater named it unprompted as "the single most distinctive authorial signature among the three texts" and cited it in the forced comparison, so its verdict cannot separate priced-failure-in-the-fiction from a label template. This is AL-245 in a new place: contamination entering through a field nobody was watching, here one I introduced by letting the treatment contract author its own label styles. Hold label_style constant across arms, or draw it from a shared neutral pool, and never let a treatment contract supply style options that name the treatment. Add a first-word-concentration check to the guard battery: no more than about a sixth of labels sharing an opening word. applied scripts/check_label_template.py
    AL-276 2026-08-10 M-4 rating round 1, blinding procedure process Blinding renamed the files and left the provenance inside them. The two books from the older shells carry "id": "sk_clocktower_cipher" and the new arm carries "id": "d6", and the second blind rater found it unaided and called it "the single most concrete signal in the whole set", explicitly noting it traces two of the three books to one shared origin object. This is AL-257 a second time (a shell id leaking, there into titles, here into a rating) and it biases the comparison in the direction of the observed result. Blinding must normalise every provenance-bearing field, not just the filename: id, metadata, version, and anything else that is not prose the reader would see.     AL-277 2026-08-10 M-4 rating round 1, rater finding on filled_D product A choice label promises one thing and its destination depicts another, in an artifact used as a control in three rating rounds. The option reads "Call the risk not worth it", which in the other two books resolves as declining the crossing; in this book the text has the character attempt it and slip ("Sam's foot went out from under him on the second plank... Idris's hand closed hard on his sleeve"). A child choosing the cautious option gets a near-fall anyway. Found by a blind rater, not by any guard, and it has survived every battery this book has been through.     AL-278 2026-08-10 M-4 rating round 1, rater finding on filled_costly validator Fact-graph closure can hold in the contract and be violated by the prose, and nothing checks that direction. The contract passed a closure walk under two independent implementations, yet the merge node's prose reads "Priya reads the first lever's knot, matches it to the pattern from the loft... Dez does the second, checking it against the ledger's margin notes", naming clues from two of the four rooms when a reader visits exactly one. check_fill_fidelity.py asks whether a node delivered its obligations; nothing asks whether a node assumed more than its entry_state guarantees. This is the mirror image of Q-5's logic_earned finding and is invisible to every guard in the battery. Add an over-assumption check at merge nodes: a model pass asking whether the prose references any fact the node's entry_state does not guarantee. Merge nodes are few (three in this graph) so the cost is bounded, and they are exactly where the error is structurally likely. applied scripts/build_prose_review_worklist.py
    AL-279 2026-08-10 M-4 rating round 2, rater finding traced to the contracts validator A choice promises an object, and the fact representing it survives only on the branch that declines it. At the crossing fork, c_pendulum_oil reads "cross with care and claim the maker's set-aside reward" and its destination carries only the puzzle facts, dropping reward_glimpsed entirely; c_pendulum_giveup ("decline it") is the branch whose destination does carry it. The claiming branch has no fact representing possession, so no author can write the payoff, and none did. A blind rater found the resulting hole in all three books unprompted: "the label promises an object; the text simply drops it", quoting all three. The pattern reproduces in contract_v2, contract_v3 and contract_costly, three independently authored contracts, one of which was written without sight of the others, so it is an error the graph shape invites rather than one author's slip. It is invisible to fact-graph closure, which only forbids assuming too much and treats dropping a fact as always safe, and invisible to check_branch_obligations.py, which computes what a branch owes rather than what it silently drops. Add an asymmetric-drop check at forks: a fact established at a fork and carried by one branch but not another is a candidate defect, and one whose dropping branch's choice_semantics name that fact's subject is a confirmed one. Cheap, deterministic, and it catches a class three guard batteries missed. applied scripts/check_promise_discharge.py
    AL-280 2026-08-10 M-4 rounds 1 and 2 (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product A rating effect that two blind raters agreed on disappeared entirely once two contaminations were removed. Round 1: both raters, opposite orders, ranked the free-failure control pair more decision-repetitive, Q6 gap one point. The arm carried a label template (35 of 35 labels opening "Spend") and a provenance leak (id shared by two of three books), both found and cited by the raters themselves. Round 2, same books with a neutral label style and tool-based blinding: raters split, both call it close, Q6 ties at 5,5, and M-4's own falsifier fires. The caveated version of round 1 would have been reported as a positive with a footnote. Treat agreement between blind raters as necessary and not sufficient; a contaminated round can produce unanimity. Keep AL-241's rebuild-rather-than-caveat rule and cite this as the case that justifies it. Where a design requires holding a variable constant, check whether that constant is itself a similarity channel before reading a null. open UW-C210
    AL-281 2026-08-10 Instrument audit across three rating rounds (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) process One item of the six-question instrument has never varied, and the only item that ever carried a result has now saturated too. Across the three rounds with recorded per-question scores, twelve cells each: Q4 (sequence repeats) is 5 in 12 of 12 and separated the pairs in 0 of 3 rounds; Q3 never leaves a two-point band at the bottom; and Q6 (solution transfer), which carried every earlier result, tied at 5,5 in the one uncontaminated round. Do not schedule another rating round on this graph until either the graph varies its central puzzle structurally between arms, which is why Q6 saturates, or a discriminating measure exists that is not at ceiling.     AL-282 2026-08-11 D-7 and D-7b (docs/planning/diversity-test-register.md) product A plan can be shared across books without them converging, but the prose the shared half carries has to be the right kind. This row originally read "no prose at all"; AL-284 measured the passing arm and found 473 words of free text still in it, so read that row before acting on this one. D-7 shared a stratum of topology, obligations, fact names and 32 one-line fact glosses, generating everything else per book: 13.6 shared 4-grams per 1000 against a budget of 4.0, exactly where D-6's diverge landed. D-7b removed the glosses and changed nothing else, verified byte-identical on every other key: 2.3, under budget and below the 3.3 generator floor. 422 words of prose in the shared artifact was the difference between 13.6 and 2.3. Two corrections, both 2026-08-11. This row first read 12.9 and 3.2, the label-inclusive pair; the body-only scope the programme standardised on gives 13.6 and 2.3, and the effect is unchanged (see AL-284). It also asserted "62 percent of shared grams tracing to the glosses"; that figure is retracted in the research brief, which re-traces it strictly as 5 of 40, or 12.5 percent, and withdraws rather than repairs it because the original method was never recorded. The retraction does not touch the deletion's effect, which is what this lesson rests on: the glosses were still the only thing that differed, and the mechanism the re-trace supports is convergent elaboration rather than copying. The stratification proposed in the architecture re-specification is therefore sound and its contents were wrong: I classified prose glosses as structure. Define the shareable stratum by exclusion rather than enumeration: it may contain identifiers, relations and enumerated categories. Do not state the exclusion as "no free text of any kind"; AL-284 measured 473 words of free text in the arm that passed, so that form of the rule describes no artifact we have built. Scope it to prose attached to the fact vocabulary the nodes reference, which is what the deletion removed; prose instructing the binding process was present in both arms and is not implicated at this volume. Keep the exclusion form, because enumerating prose fields has now missed some twice (constraints and mechanisms in AL-265's follow-up, then facts). Amend the architecture re-specification's section 2 table accordingly. open UW-C212
    AL-283 2026-08-11 D-7b fact readings product Bare fact names bind two independent authors to the same obligation without a gloss. The worry was that stripping definitions would make the shared plan too vague to be a plan. Measured: two authors given only names, node function and their own binding wrote 0 of 32 identical reading sentences and 0 of 35 identical choice semantics, chose different engines, and agreed in meaning on every fact sampled (logic_earned as "one real, usable piece of the tide arithmetic needed to set the dial" against "one working piece of the peal-cipher's logic, enough to actually attempt a setting"). A name plus a node function plus a binding is a sufficient specification. Drop fact glosses from the shared stratum as standard, and require each book's plan to record its own fact_readings, which makes interpretation drift measurable per book instead of assumed away. open UW-C213
    AL-284 2026-08-11 Second external review, question on device categories, checked against the D-7b artifact process We published a rule describing an artifact we had not measured, and the artifact contradicts it. D-7b was reported as sharing a plan with "no free text at all". Measured directly: the shared kernel still carries 473 words of free text (world_recipe binding notes, per-node invention notes, eight title_contract.must_not clauses, the affect ceiling), which is more than the 422 words the experiment deleted (895 to 473). The measured result is unaffected and still holds at the corrected body-only scope, 13.6 to 2.3, but the stated mechanism cannot be right: "no free text at all" is not what the passing arm implements. The reviewer reached the same objection from the document alone, by reasoning that device categories carry no prose yet still steer the puzzle; the artifact was worse than their inference. Note this also weakens AL-282, whose figures are the superseded mixed-scope pair and whose "no free text of any kind" rule this corrects. State the rule as: free text attached to the fact vocabulary nodes reference drove convergence; free text instructing the binding process did not, at this volume. Whether the operative variable is what the text describes or how often it is re-read is open and settled by a third arm that deletes the 473 and keeps the 422. Standing process change: before publishing any claim of the form "the shared artifact contains no X", assert it programmatically against the artifact rather than against the build script's intent. open UW-C214
    AL-285 2026-08-11 Second external review, question 1 on rater provenance process The brief calls model raters "human". Section 14 reads "a deterministic decision-overlap score on top of blind human annotation". Every rater, annotator and "blind reader of the finished books" in Parts I and II was an LLM agent instance; no human and no child has read any book in this programme for rating purposes, and the reported Fleiss kappas (0.96, 1.00) are inter-model agreement. Part I's methods appendix says "a rater agent" correctly, so the document contradicts itself, and every other use of "rater", "annotator" and "reader" is unqualified and therefore ambiguous. An external reviewer had to ask which it was before they could weight any result, which is the cost. Correct section 14, and add one provenance statement covering every rated result in both parts, stating that rater verdicts are hypotheses about reader response rather than reader evidence. Adopt as a standing rule: name the rater's nature at every site where a verdict is reported, never once in an appendix. open UW-C215
    AL-286 2026-08-11 Fourth external review, A1.1 attack on 16l, run rather than argued product The 16l pair passes every lexical gate and sits at the top of our observed range on shared cognitive operation. A reviewer objected that 16l measures verbatim overlap while the defect is decision repetition, and that the pair had never been measured for the second. Run on the existing artifacts, so the cost was zero generations: solution transfer over the shared chain gives answer 0.167 (passes the tier-1 gate), operation 0.500, solution 0.467. Correction, same day: my first reading of this was wrong and the known-bad battery caught it. The operation tier is not monotone with badness, because the tiers are exclusive and the strongest wins: a prop matching by answer never counts as an operation match, so a literally identical binding scores operation 0.000. Ranking must use the composite. On the composite solution_transfer, scored against a battery built for this purpose, the ordering is: identical binding 1.000, same-chain-different-dressing 1.000, the known-contaminated pair 1.000, relocated chain 0.700, the 16l pair 0.467, most distinct real pair 0.225. So the 16l pair is mid-range, not the worst we hold: four of its six chain props transfer at some tier, which is materially short of clean and well short of catastrophic. Separately, on aligned choice positions in the two filled books: zero identical labels and 11 of 35 sharing their first content word, several of them the same act with a swapped noun ("Turn Back Together" against "Turn back now"; "Stay and Read" against "Stay and read the logs"). Caveat that must travel with this: the operation tier is advisory and was not the tier that reproduced rater orderings in D-4, only tier 1 was, and n is 6 chain props on one contract. Stop reporting 16l as a distinctness result. It is a convergence result, and the first cheap test of the property 1.3 actually defines comes back unfavourable. Add a decisional sibling to the exclusion rule: sharing a structural kernel must also schedule the reasoning kind per fork, not merely leave it to the binding. Validate the operation tier against a constructed known-bad battery before it is allowed to gate anything, and never quote 0.500 as a gate result. open UW-C216
    AL-287 2026-08-11 Fourth external review, catalog-median erratum docs Two different medians are used interchangeably without saying which is which. The brief cites 151 in 7 and 149 in 16i and 16o, and a reviewer read them as an inconsistency. Measured: the catalog median across all 61 skeletons is 151; 149 is the median of the 10-13 band, which is the pilot's band.     AL-288 2026-08-11 Q-3b, the constrained rerun of skeleton-free generation product Stating a constraint works for anything the author can verify by tracing its own artifact, and does nothing for anything requiring a statistic it cannot compute. Six mutually isolated authors, no skeleton, one pass, no repair round and no self-review prompt: 6 of 6 structurally clean, and the entire band-budget failure class that sank the first run (opening-decision floor, arc floor, ending schema) went to zero of six simply by being written down. Two independent checkers agree. In the same prompt, an explicit Flesch-Kincaid 4-6 target produced 5 of 6 books over the 7.0 band edge, with no book placing more than 48 percent of its nodes inside the band. The model can trace reachability and path length; it cannot count syllables. Split the constraint set by self-verifiability when designing any generation prompt. Structural and budget constraints: state them and expect compliance. Reading level: a per-node repair loop against the deterministic checker is mandatory, not an optimisation, because the prompt provably does not reach it. This also prices every drafting pipeline in the candidate architectures, since all three propose model-drafted artifacts checked by program. open UW-C218
    AL-289 2026-08-11 Q-3b premise inspection product Six authors sharing nothing produced five coastal lighthouse-or-lantern stories with fog or a storm and an elder keeper. One outlier. This is worse than the first run reported, because the convergence is on a specific setting archetype rather than a plot engine, and it happened with no shared plan, no sibling visibility and no common prompt beyond an age band. Convergence is not lexical: median 1.56 shared four-grams per 1000 bodies-only, 11 of 15 pairs at or below the 3.3 floor, so wording independence is free when nothing is shared. But 4 of 15 pairs breach the 4.0 budget and every breach is a same-archetype pair, which is the content-driven breach the first run predicted and this one reproduces. Treat premise scheduling as a mandatory shared service in every candidate architecture rather than a property any one of them provides. Removing the plan buys wording independence and nothing else. The scheduler must allocate from a curated enumerated space, since the failure is in the model's own distribution and no amount of withholding touches it. open UW-C219
    AL-290 2026-08-11 Q-3c, premise mode across model tiers product Two independent instances of the same model, sharing no context and reading no file, invented the same place name and produced titles differing by one preposition: "The Lantern Under Marrow Hill" against "The Lantern Beneath Marrow Hill". Four of five words identical. The probe held task and prompt constant and varied the model tier, to test whether AL-289's lighthouse archetype was one model's mode. It is not: pooled across three tiers, 8 of 10 independent generations put a light-or-signal beacon at the centre, most coastal or fog-bound, several with an elder keeper. The two outliers were a kite race and a forest threatened by a shopping mall. It delivers wording independence and nothing above it, and the collision here is on an invented proper noun, which is as far below the plan layer as a collision can get. Premise allocation from a curated enumerated space is now a precondition for every candidate architecture rather than one architecture's feature. Fund a genuine cross-vendor replication before quoting any idiom floor or diversity margin as general. open UW-C220
    AL-291 2026-08-11 Q-3d, constraint-stated generation at 100+ nodes product Structure survives the scale jump and one-pass generation does not. Two isolated authors at 101 and 103 nodes: 2 of 2 structurally clean, zero failures, every stated budget met, which answers the question both external architecture rankings turned on. But neither managed it in one pass: one built a scripted build-validate-patch loop, the other's first draft produced a longest path of 43 against a ceiling of 24 and needed trimming and repadding. Cost ran 210k and 337k tokens at 60 and 269 tool calls, against roughly 85k and 3 calls for a 30-node graph, so generation cost is dominated by the repair loop rather than by node count.     AL-292 2026-08-11 Q-3d reading level, the two arms diverging validator Same prompt, same explicit reading-level target, opposite outcomes, decided by whether the author chose to instrument it.     AL-293 2026-08-11 PR #685 review, auditing what the guard battery actually gates tooling A guard invoked without its gating flag was counted in the gating denominator, so the battery's headline number overstated coverage. run_guard_battery.py recorded every per-book guard gating=True, but invoked check_prose_craft.py bare. That script's main() is if args.check and breached: return 1 followed by return 0, so without --check it cannot return non-zero for any input: a guard that could not fail was contributing to "N of M GATING guards clear". The trap is that the flag is not uniformly required. check_fill_integrity.py and run_story_gate.py were invoked bare in the same tuple and are correct that way, because both already end return 1 if failed else 0. So the defect is invisible to any rule of the form "every gating guard passes --check", and a blanket fix would have been wrong for two of the three. The battery had already been hardened once to report the gating denominator rather than the total, precisely so a green battery could not hide skipped checks (see the comment above the summary line in run_guard_battery.py::main); this defect put a guard that could not fail inside that hardened denominator, which is the same failure one level down. Read each checker's exit contract before recording it gating, rather than inferring the invocation from the table in the docstring. Generalised check: when a battery aggregates heterogeneous sub-commands, the aggregate's honesty depends on a per-command fact (does this exit non-zero on failure, and under what flags) that no aggregate-level assertion can verify; the test therefore has to name the commands individually, including the ones that must NOT carry the flag. applied scripts/run_guard_battery.py (adds --check to the check_prose_craft.py invocation); tests/unit/test_guard_gating.py::test_guard_battery_prose_craft_invoked_with_check_flag, which asserts the flag is present for check_prose_craft.py and explicitly ABSENT for check_fill_integrity.py and run_story_gate.py, and was verified to fail when the fix is reverted
    AL-294 2026-08-11 PR #685 review, scripts/check_reading_level.py unscorable path validator A guard that could not evaluate a book reported it clear, and the case it could not evaluate is the defect it exists to catch. score() returns None when the whole concatenated book holds fewer than the minimum scoreable words; main() then continued past it, leaving breached untouched, printing ok : reading level and returning 0. In a filled storybook that condition means the fill is empty or truncated, so the one book most obviously unfit for a reader was the one book the guard waved through, and run_guard_battery would print a fully green battery for it. This is AL-259 one layer in: that lesson built this guard because reading level degraded at scale with nothing blocking, and the guard shipped with a silent hole on its own unevaluable input.     AL-295 2026-08-11 PR #687 review, re-deriving the stratified convergence row process A correction reversed a correct figure because its denominator came from the prose rather than from the artifacts.     AL-297 2026-08-11 Device-vocabulary workstream, building scripts/check_device_vocabulary.py tooling Headroom computed over totals understates capacity, and the error runs toward ordering vocabulary nobody needs. Had the naive figure driven the widening, the axis would have been credited one book less than it has and an author would have been asked for kinds the binder can never reach. The same arithmetic decides the degenerate case: an axis whose every pick is frozen has no forced-repeat book at all, because no vocabulary size changes an outcome that was never free.     AL-300 2026-08-11 Device-vocabulary workstream, building scripts/check_device_vocabulary.py tooling The two shipped narrative contracts are serialised at different indent widths, so any programmatic edit reformats one of them wholesale. the-lost-mitten.narrative.json round-trips at indent=2 and the-clocktower-cipher.narrative.json at indent=1. A script written against either constant rewrites every line of the other, which buries a small semantic change in a whole-file diff and makes review of a safety-relevant contract change impractical. The end-of-file-fixer pre-commit hook fired on both files during this run, which means it had never run on either since they were added, and it normalised a second divergence (neither carried a trailing newline) while leaving the indent widths untouched. That is the shape of the trap: a formatting hook silently repairs the divergence it knows about and says nothing about the one it does not, so the surviving difference is precisely the one no gate will ever mention. It was caught here by round-tripping each file before editing it, but only because the check was run deliberately. Either normalise both contracts to one indent width and add a serialisation check to the authoring guard battery, or keep the per-file detect-and-preserve step as the documented way to edit them. Normalising is the cleaner end state but its diff must land on its own commit, never alongside a content change, for the same reason the drift matters. open skeletons/3-5/the-lost-mitten.narrative.json (indent=2) versus skeletons/10-13/the-clocktower-cipher.narrative.json (indent=1); the reformat-on-write hazard is why every contract edit in this run was made in place rather than by json.dump round-trip

    AL-302 2026-08-11 Cost-instrumentation workstream, satisfying BasedPyright strict on generation/metered.py tooling Strict type checking proved two defensive guards dead and asked for their deletion, and deleting them would have converted an unmetered call into a crash. The meter reads response.usage off whatever the inner provider returns. Both provider interfaces are structural Protocols, so a non-conforming implementation can return anything, and the moderation stages already depend on being handed exactly what the backend produced. Annotating the intermediate as object is not enough: pyright narrows a variable to the type of the expression assigned to it regardless of the declared annotation, so it proved isinstance(returned, Completion) always true and raised reportUnnecessaryIsInstance on both guards. An author who resolves that error the way the checker suggests removes the fail-safe, and the next degraded response raises on the accounting path rather than being forwarded. Only an explicit cast("object", ...) around the await keeps the guards live. Where a defensive check exists because the runtime type is weaker than the static one, launder the value through an explicit cast to object and say so in a comment; a bare annotation does not survive narrowing. Generalised check: reportUnnecessaryIsInstance on a value crossing a structural-Protocol boundary is a claim about the annotation, not about reality, and should be answered by widening the binding rather than by deleting the check. applied the returned: object = cast("object", ...) binding and its RAD block in src/cyo_adventure/generation/metered.py, covered by tests/unit/test_metered.py::test_a_non_completion_response_is_forwarded_unmetered and ::test_a_completion_with_a_non_usage_payload_is_forwarded_unmetered
    AL-303 2026-08-11 Cost-instrumentation workstream, choosing where per-job accounting is persisted process The obvious home for the new accounting was a field that a retention job deletes. generation_job.report already holds a JSON blob of per-run detail, so adding token counts and cost to it needs no migration and no schema change. It is also the field the ADR-007 retention rule nulls on a Phase 5 pg_cron schedule to shed raw model output. Cost history has to outlive the prose it was derived from: "what has generation cost this quarter" is asked long after the raw output is gone. Accounting placed inside report would therefore be deleted by a rule aimed at something else, on a delay, with the row still present so nothing looks missing. The convenient choice fails months later and leaves no trace of why. Before adding a field to an existing JSON column, check what lifecycle rules already act on that column, and treat a retention or purge rule as a type constraint on what may live there. Generalised check: a column with a scheduled mutator is a different storage class from its neighbours regardless of what its type says, and the mismatch surfaces only after the schedule fires. applied the seven typed columns on GenerationJob in src/cyo_adventure/db/models.py and supabase/migrations/20260811160000_add_generation_job_provider_accounting.sql, whose header records the reasoning; parity proved by tests/integration/test_schema_parity.py
    AL-304 2026-08-11 Cost-instrumentation workstream, building generation/cost.py tooling A cost derived from a job's token totals bills calls at prices they were never charged. One generation job routinely runs the drafting stages on one model and the review stages on another, and can fail over to a third mid-run, so the run's summed input and output tokens belong to no single price. Costing over totals requires picking one rate for all of them; the same 100k output tokens are worth 0.50 USD on Haiku and 1.50 USD on Sonnet, so the error is a factor of three in the direction of whichever model the code happened to name. The aggregate the schema stores is a single number, which also forces the completeness flag to be pessimistic for the whole run: a reader cannot recover per-call detail from one stored amount, so one un-costable call has to mark the entire figure a lower bound. Cost each call at its own model's price and aggregate afterwards, never the reverse, and carry a completeness flag alongside any summed money figure that can be partially derived. Generalised check: whenever a total is computed over a heterogeneous set, ask what per-item attribute the arithmetic silently assumes is uniform. applied estimate_run_cost in src/cyo_adventure/generation/cost.py and tests/unit/test_cost.py::test_each_call_is_costed_at_its_own_models_price, ::test_one_unpriced_call_makes_the_whole_run_incomplete

    AL-306 2026-08-12 PR #701 review fixes, DV-4 in scripts/check_device_vocabulary.py validator An absent key and an explicitly empty list are different declarations, and collapsing them inverted the author's meaning. _envelope returned an empty set both for a missing permitted_device_kinds and for an explicit [], and DV-4's elif permitted and ... is falsy for both.     AL-307 2026-08-12 PR #701 review fixes, generation_job.cost_usd tooling Precision and scale fail in opposite ways in a NUMERIC column, and a value that overflows one of them can erase the failure record it was supposed to accompany. Excess SCALE is rounded away silently; an out-of-range INTEGER part raises numeric field overflow, and raises it at COMMIT rather than at assignment. Both writers of the accounting columns commit, and one of them is the _record_failure the worker's interrupt guard calls to record that a job died, so an uncapped amount faults twice: the second raise displaces the first and the row is left in queued/running with no failure recorded at all, defeating the guard precisely when it is needed. Reachable from a malformed provider response, since token counts are bounded below but not above. Bound a derived money value against its column in memory, before assignment, and reuse the existing completeness flag to mark the capped figure a lower bound rather than inventing a second signal. Generalised check: any function called on a failure-recording path must be total; verify it by reading for raises through its whole call tree, not by wrapping it in a handler, because the handler is what a linter will later ask you to justify. applied fit_cost_to_column in src/cyo_adventure/generation/cost.py, tests/unit/test_cost.py::test_an_amount_past_the_column_maximum_is_capped_and_incomplete and tests/unit/test_worker.py::TestProviderAccounting::test_a_cost_past_the_column_maximum_is_capped_before_the_driver
    AL-308 2026-08-11 Handoff item 3.1, re-deriving D-6's published rates from the artifacts process Three published rates in one table did not reproduce, and the control row in the same table is what proved it was the figures and not the harness. D-6's 16g.1 table published 16.9, 11.4 and 12.9 for the three one-contract conditions and 2.9 for the different-contracts control, asserting every row was body-only. Measured off docs/planning/evidence/d6-contract-sharing/ with the primitives in scripts/check_sibling_fills.py, the three treatment rows are 17.24, 11.75 and 13.57; the control reproduces exactly at 9 grams over 3,073.0 words, 2.93. The harness was validated first against the four cells of the brief's own D-7 re-derivation and matched all four to the gram and the tenth of a word, so "I cannot reproduce this" was upgraded to "this figure is wrong" before anything was edited. Two further facts fell out that a scope story does not explain: all three published values sit below their body-only re-derivation, where a mixed-scope error moves figures in both directions, and the same three values had already survived a correction pass that added the words "all rows are body-only" without recomputing them. The wrong figures then propagated into AL-264, the experiment register in six places, the architecture re-specification and UW-C194. Before citing or correcting any published rate, re-derive it from the frozen artifacts, and in the same run re-derive one figure in the same table that is not in dispute. The undisputed figure is the control that separates a wrong number from a wrong harness, and it costs one extra call. Second, a correction pass that restates a table's scope must recompute every row it relabels, because relabelling is a claim about all of them: AL-295's pass relabelled these three and recomputed only the two it came for. Third, when a figure moves, grep the register, the lessons log and the architecture documents for it before committing, since a rate published once is quoted an average of four more times. open UW-C224
    AL-309 2026-08-11 Handoff item 3.1, auditing the metric while re-deriving D-6 tooling The convergence metric manufactures four-grams that exist in none of the text it measures, by joining separate units into one string before gramming. _leaf_text in scripts/check_sibling_fills.py concatenates every node body and choice label with a space and then takes four-grams over the result, so any four words spanning a join are counted as shared prose. Measured on D-6: labels share zero four-grams when grammed per label, in all three conditions and the control, exactly as the brief reports; yet moving from body-only to label-inclusive scope adds seven shared grams in the flattened arm, six of the seven straddling a boundary (drop down inside inside, a label's last three words meeting the next body's first); the seventh, keep it between us, is a genuine within-label match. The same defect runs the other way too, and the diverging arm loses a gram when labels are interleaved, because interleaving breaks a junction the body-only concatenation had itself invented. Body-only scope is contaminated too, in one of the four pairs: per-unit counts are verbatim 48, neutral 34, control 9, all identical to the joined counts, but diverge is 37 per-unit against 38 joined, which moves that arm's published body-only rate from 13.6 to 13.2 per 1000. So the correction is not confined to the label-inclusive column, though that column is where it is large. Compute grams per unit and union the sets, rather than gramming a joined string: _grams(body) per node and _grams(label) per choice, unioned. That makes scope a filter over units instead of a rewrite of the text and kills the junction artifact at both scopes. Expected regression-test values, established before the migration so it cannot be graded against its own output: body-only per-unit counts of 48 / 34 / 37 / 9 for verbatim / neutral / diverge / control, three of them matching today's joined counts and diverge one lower. Until that lands, no table should quote a label-inclusive rate without saying it includes junction grams. open UW-C225
    AL-310 2026-08-12 PR #703 review, reconciling this branch against concurrent PR #701 tooling Every id space this project hands out collided between two concurrent PRs on the same day, and no branch's own CI could see any of it. Three collisions between #703 and #701: the Supabase migration prefix 20260811160000, the lessons ids AL-308/AL-309, and the register row UW-C224, each holding different content on each side. Both branches were internally consistent and both passed every gate alone, because every uniqueness check in the repo is scoped to the branch plus origin/main. Two further findings make this worse than AL-072 recorded. First, the guard AL-072 proposed, that a new migration prefix be strictly greater than the newest on origin/main, catches none of these: both colliding prefixes were greater than main's newest, which is exactly what concurrent same-day branches produce. Second, the obvious mitigation of yielding the contested range is closed off: check_lessons_log.py rejects gaps in the AL- sequence, so #703 cannot renumber to AL-308/AL-309 until #701's AL-308..AL-307 actually land. Merge order therefore decides who renumbers, and the second PR to merge must renumber under a conflict rather than having been able to avoid it. That resolved on 2026-08-12 and confirms the mechanism: #701 merged first, #703's CI check count silently collapsed to four because a CONFLICTING pull request runs no workflows at all, and #703 renumbered AL-296..AL-298 to AL-308..AL-310 and UW-C223..UW-C226 to UW-C224..UW-C227 inside the merge commit. Worth noting for the next occurrence: the collapse in check count is the only signal, since a conflicting PR reports no failure anywhere, so a branch can sit indefinitely looking merely un-run. The migration prefix is the exception, since prefixes carry no density rule and #703 could move to 1700xx immediately. Build one uniqueness check that reads the other OPEN pull requests rather than only origin/main, covering migration prefixes and AL-/UW- ids in the same pass (gh pr list --state open then gh pr diff per PR; note gh pr diff takes no pathspec, so filter its output rather than passing one). Generalised check: a uniqueness invariant enforced against the trunk is not enforced at all while more than one branch is open, and pairing it with a no-gap rule converts an avoidable collision into a mandatory merge conflict. Whichever of the two rules is load-bearing, they should not both be. open UW-C226
    AL-311 2026-08-12 Building the Stage D reading-level loop, lifting the whole-book aggregate into the validator validator A script that imports a checker's private primitives and reimplements the formula around them has not shared the implementation, and its docstring said it could not drift while it already had. scripts/check_reading_level.py imported _WORD_RE, _SENTENCE_RE, and _count_syllables from validator/reading_level.py and stated that because it reused RL-13's primitives, its whole-book measure "cannot drift from RL-13". It had drifted in two places: it counted sentences with _SENTENCE_RE.split() where the validator uses .findall() (an empty trailing segment after a final period inflates the count by one, which lowers the reported grade on every book), and it gated on a _WORD_RE-based word count where the validator gates on body.split() (they disagree on hyphenates and possessives, so the two disagreed about which nodes were even scorable). Both differences are small enough that no book flipped verdict, which is exactly why the divergence survived: the invariant was asserted in prose and checked by nothing. Share the function, not its ingredients. The validator now exports measure_book() and score_body(), and the script calls them; score_body() is the single definition of "scorable" that RL-13, the script, and the new generation-time loop all use. Generalised check: a claim of the form "X cannot drift from Y" is only true when X calls Y. When a module imports another's privates, treat that as the signal that the shared thing has not been factored out yet, and read it as a to-do rather than as evidence of consistency. applied src/cyo_adventure/validator/reading_level.py (adds score_body, measure_book, BookReadingLevel, and routes RL-13's own loop through score_body); scripts/check_reading_level.py (deletes the reimplementation and the false claim); tests/unit/test_guard_gating.py, whose floor assertion now names the validator constant so the script cannot reacquire a private copy unnoticed
    AL-312 2026-08-12 Building the Stage D reading-level repair prompt tooling The generation prompt templates carry untrusted content inside a delimiter fence that nothing sanitises, which moderation/stages.py does sanitise for exactly this reason. Adding a prompt that carries model-written node prose surfaced the asymmetry: moderation/stages.py has _sanitize_delimited, while every template in generation/prompts.py interpolates into <<<UNTRUSTED_USER_INPUT ... >>>END_UNTRUSTED_USER_INPUT with no treatment at all. A body holding the literal terminator closes the fence early and addresses the model directly, and prose is a worse channel than the JSON briefs the other templates carry, because json.dumps escapes quotes and newlines but not that token. The narrower residual hole is real too: build_fill_prompt and build_bound_fill_prompt interpolate {theme_brief} into the same fence, and although an attacker must first get the string through screening intake, nothing between intake and the prompt would stop it. Neutralise the terminator at every site that interpolates into the fence, not only the new one. Done here for the reading-level prompt (_neutralize_fence); the two fill prompts are tracked separately rather than widened into this change. Generalised check: a delimiter is a security boundary only if something enforces that the delimiter cannot appear in the delimited content, and the enforcement belongs next to the interpolation, not in the instructions asking the model to be careful. open UW-C228
    AL-313 2026-08-12 Wiring the reading-level loop into the orchestrator tooling The obvious way to add a reading-level repair pass, widening the existing repair loop's finding filter, would have disabled that loop's termination control. orchestrator._gate_signature filters to Severity.ERROR deliberately, and the comment above it says why: the signature is what the no-progress abort compares, so a prose-only edit must not be able to change it. RL-13 findings are WARNING and embed the computed score in their message, so including them would give every cosmetic re-word a fresh signature and let the loop run to its full budget on stories it was not improving. The stage was therefore attached separately, with a different and stronger abort: a revision is accepted only when abs(grade - target) strictly decreases, so the loop cannot oscillate and cannot spin on an unchanged reply, and the whole pass is rolled back if the re-gate regresses. Before extending a loop's stopping condition, read what that condition is load-bearing for. Generalised check: when a control's scope looks arbitrarily narrow, the narrowness is often the control; the cheap test is to ask what becomes possible once it is widened. A second stage with its own convergence rule is usually cheaper than making one loop serve two convergence criteria. applied src/cyo_adventure/generation/reading_level_loop.py (_accept's monotone rule and the post-splice re-gate discard); src/cyo_adventure/generation/orchestrator.py Stage D, attached after the structural loop rather than inside it; tests/unit/test_reading_level_loop.py::test_reading_level_rejects_a_revision_that_scores_worse and ::test_reading_level_gate_regression_discards_the_whole_pass
    AL-314 2026-08-12 Building the cross-vendor comparison harness tooling An OpenRouter model slug is not a vendor, so every cross-model comparison this project could have run so far would have been unattributable. GET /api/v1/models/anthropic~claude-sonnet-4.6/endpoints returns four distinct backends for that one slug: Anthropic, Google, Amazon Bedrock and Azure. openai/gpt-5.4 is served by OpenAI, Azure and Amazon Bedrock; google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview by Google (Vertex) and Google AI Studio. OpenRouter picks per request, so two runs of nominally the same model can be answered by different backends at different quantizations, and a difference attributed to a vendor's house style might be a routing difference instead. The adapter sent no provider field at all, so nothing in the codebase constrained this. Send provider: {order: [...], allow_fallbacks: false} whenever a run's purpose is measurement, so a backend that cannot serve the request fails visibly instead of being substituted. Generalised check: before comparing two model configurations, establish that the thing being varied is the only thing that varies; for a routed API that means asking the routing layer what it would have chosen, not assuming the slug determines it. applied src/cyo_adventure/generation/providers/openrouter.py (provider_order, emitting the pin with allow_fallbacks: false); src/cyo_adventure/generation/provider.py::build_openrouter_leg; tests/unit/test_openrouter_provider_pin.py, whose default-path test asserts an unpinned body stays byte-identical so production routing is untouched; the verified backend lists are recorded in docs/planning/vendor-comparison/vendors.json
    AL-315 2026-08-12 Designing the cross-vendor shared-gram comparison tooling Bucketing book pairs by vendor alone would have credited shared premise wording to vendor agreement. The comparison generates one book per (vendor, brief), so a cross-vendor pair is usually a same-brief pair while a within-vendor pair is always a different-brief pair. Two vendors handed the same premise share proper nouns, setting vocabulary and plot-beat wording for reasons that have nothing to do with house style, so a one-axis split would have inflated the cross-vendor floor and made every real vendor effect look smaller than it is, in the direction of concluding model choice does not matter. Partition pairs on both axes and compare only the different-brief column, reporting same-brief cross-vendor separately as premise convergence.     AL-318 2026-08-12 Sizing skeletons against the single-call fill path tooling Most of the skeleton catalog projects over the orchestrator's output cap, and nothing measures this. fill_skeleton sends the whole skeleton and expects the whole filled document back in one completion under _MAX_TOKENS_PROSE (32,000).     AL-323 2026-08-12 Re-running the credit-blocked legs of the vendor comparison tooling The pre-flight reachability probe asked for a 3-token completion, which condemned every reasoning model on the slate as unreachable while its pin was routing correctly. A reasoning leg emits no content until its hidden reasoning tokens are spent, so a budget sized for the answer returns finish_reason='length' with empty content, which the provider surfaces as openrouter response had no message content. That text is indistinguishable from a dead pin, and it is what the probe reported. Measured across the candidate slate on 2026-08-12: Kimi K3 spends up to 197 reasoning tokens before its first content token, Gemini 3.1 Pro 183, GLM 5.2 98, and both Anthropic legs zero. The defect therefore selects precisely against the newer reasoning models and would have silently shrunk the slate to the labs that do not reason, while looking like a clean availability result. The probe added in AL-321 was right to exercise the real permission path; it was wrong about what a completion costs. Size a liveness probe by what the model must spend before it can answer, not by the answer, and record the measurement next to the constant so the next person widens it on evidence rather than by doubling. Separate the two failure texts in the operator hint: a bare 404 No endpoints found means re-probe with provider.only, whereas no message content means the pin routed and the budget starved. Generalised check: a probe that reports a binary reachable/unreachable must be tested against the slowest-to-first-token member of the population it screens, or it is a filter on latency wearing the costume of a filter on availability. applied scripts/compare_vendors.py::_PREFLIGHT_MAX_TOKENS (512, with the measurements in preflight's docstring) and the extended operator hint in ::_report_preflight; tests/unit/test_compare_vendors.py::test_preflight_budget_clears_measured_reasoning_overhead asserts the constant against the 197-token worst case rather than restating it; verified end to end by re-probing the unchanged Kimi K3 pin at 3 tokens (UNREACHABLE) and at 512 (reachable)

    AL-325 2026-08-12 Building the two-axis evaluator over the vendor comparison's books validator The deterministic gate cannot fail a book that was never written, because every checker that meets a <<FILL ...>> directive skips it rather than failing on it. validator/choice_grammar.py skips any body carrying the marker (it is a directive, not prose), validator/sentinel_integrity.py documents the same tolerance, and validator/reading_level.py::measure_book returns None for directive-only bodies rather than a failing grade. Each skip is locally correct, because these same checkers run over unfilled skeletons at catalog time where directives are exactly what is expected. Collectively they mean a document consisting entirely of directives clears topology, safety, choice grammar and reading level without producing a single finding: abstention aggregates to clean. With Stage 1 off (AL-324) there is then no deterministic floor at all, and the harness's own reading-level column reports in_band=None for such a book, which is the same value it reports for a book that merely declares no band. Give the gate a context rather than a new checker: a document validated as a fill result must fail on any retained <<FILL marker, while the identical checkers keep their present tolerance when validating a skeleton. One distinction converts four independent abstentions into one hard failure, needs no model to make it, and cannot regress the catalog-time path. Separately, stop encoding "could not measure" and "nothing to measure" as the same None.     AL-326 2026-08-12 Watching two paid vendor runs that had not yet written anything tooling A paid run holds every book it has generated in memory until the last one lands, so an interruption at any point loses everything already bought. _write_outputs is called once, at the end of main, and creates books/ and report.json together. That also makes an early book inspectable, which is what turns an ambiguous progress line into an abandon-or-continue decision while the money is still unspent.     AL-327 2026-08-12 Tracing why four vendor-comparison books were byte-identical to their skeletons tooling When a fill produces no parseable document the orchestrator seeds the repair loop with the unfilled skeleton, and if repair makes no progress it returns that skeleton as a passing book. orchestrator.py:1000 sets last_valid_doc = current_doc if current_doc is not None else skeleton, and :1013 seeds repair from it, so a None fill (invalid JSON, non-dict, truncated output) enters repair as the authoring skeleton. The loop's no-progress rule then stops after exactly one attempt, which is the attempts: 1 recorded against all four failed books in run-1 and run-3. A skeleton is structurally valid by construction, so the gate certifies it and the outcome is status='passed', error=None, with the input handed back as the output. Four books across two labs did this (Sonnet 5 on briefs 0 to 2, Gemini 3 Flash on brief 3), and every one is 0 filled nodes of 37, 59, 35 and 38 respectively: never partial. That totality is the tell that a seed fallback rather than a partial write produced the artifact, but it does not rule out truncation upstream: a completion cut off mid-JSON does not parse at all, so it yields current_doc = None and therefore a perfectly empty book by exactly this path. Truncation against the 32,000-token cap is in fact the reproduced upstream cause for three of these four books (AL-328); this fallback is the mechanism that hides it. Combined with AL-325, nothing anywhere in the deterministic path can object. A fill that never produced a parseable document must terminate as failed, whatever the gate later says about the fallback artifact, because the gate is being asked about a document the model never wrote. Do not seed a repair loop with the unfilled input: repairing a skeleton toward validity is a no-op that consumes the budget and ends in a false pass; seed from the last document the model actually produced, or refuse to enter the loop. Generalised check: when a failure path substitutes a default value, ask whether that default can satisfy the success test, because a fallback that passes validation converts every failure into a silent success. open Reproduced 2026-08-12: anthropic-sonnet-5__00/01/02.json and google-gemini-3-flash__03.json compare byte-identical to skeletons/5-8/*.json on both node ids and node bodies, including titles; report.json records status='passed', error=None, attempts=1 for all four, against attempts=0 for books that filled cleanly on the first pass. That inference is wrong and was falsified by reproduction the same day: truncated JSON does not parse at all, so it produces the empty book by this very path.     AL-328 2026-08-12 Diagnosing why two vendor-comparison legs returned unfilled books tooling A fixed max_tokens is not a neutral condition in a cross-vendor comparison, because models differ roughly fivefold in completion budget for identical work, so one cap silently converts a verbose model into a failing one. The relationship is dose-response rather than anecdotal: ranking Sonnet 5's four briefs by budget consumed gives 100, 91, 83 and 78 percent of cap, and the single brief it filled in run-1 is the cheapest of the four. Cost spreads even wider than tokens, twelvefold, at 2.9 cents against 34 cents for one book. Report the cap as a measured condition of any cross-vendor comparison, and refuse to publish a leg's fill rate when its completions land within a stated margin of the cap; a leg running at 91 percent of budget is a leg whose next run is a coin toss, so its number describes the harness.     AL-329 2026-08-12 Diagnosing why two vendor-comparison legs returned unfilled books tooling GenerationProvider.complete returns a bare string, so the usage object is discarded at the provider boundary and two opposite failures reach the caller as one indistinguishable sentence.     AL-331 2026-08-12 Reading the quality panel's per-criterion output across eight vendor legs authoring-craft The pipeline produces near-dialogue-free stories whatever model fills them, so the absence is a property of the skeletons and the fill prompt rather than of any vendor.     AL-332 2026-08-12 Measuring what the vendor comparison actually cost, to price story generation product Reasoning tokens are billed at the output rate and produce no prose, so book length does not predict what a book costs and a cost model built from $/MTok x book words under-recovers by roughly half. The spread across legs asked for the identical book is 17x, and it tracks reasoning share rather than output quality: Kimi spent 28,247 of 32,000 completion tokens thinking and returned a truncated book, so 88% of that call bought nothing and the remainder did not finish. The error is one-directional and worst exactly where retries are most likely, because a starved reasoning model burns the full cap and delivers nothing. Price story generation from measured per-model cost, never from projected output words, and treat reasoning share as a first-class model-selection criterion alongside compliance and quality: it is the largest single term in the bill and is invisible in the finished book.     AL-334 2026-08-12 Writing a probe to measure per-leg fill cost outside the pipeline tooling A measurement harness that reimplements a pipeline step rather than importing it measures a different pipeline, and the divergence is silent because both sides look correct in isolation.     AL-336 2026-08-12 The cost probe crashing on its final leg after 33 paid calls tooling An error boundary drawn around the request but not around the response parse leaves the whole run exposed to a transport-truncated body.
