    AL-337 2026-08-12 An external review checking what our strongest published claim actually rests on validator A gate assembled from measures that all abstain on dimension X reports a clean verdict on X, and the aggregate silence reads as verification. This is AL-325 one layer up and one abstraction wider. There, four checkers each correctly skipped a <<FILL>> body and four correct skips composed into a pass on an unwritten book. Here, the research brief's requirement table claims "safe and age-appropriate, verifiably" at the highest confidence we assign anything, but the age-appropriateness half is carried by validator/reading_level.py's Flesch-Kincaid grade plus validator/band_profile.py's floors, whose own module docstring says only the bands near 9-12 are research-measured. Flesch-Kincaid is one leg of a three-leg construct; the qualitative dimensions (levels of meaning, text structure, language conventionality, knowledge demands) are observed by nothing in the pipeline, so a book can sit centre-band and still assume a concept its reader has not got. The failure is not that the checkers are wrong. Each is correct about what it measures. The failure is that a claim was stated over a dimension no instrument in the composition observes. Any claim of the form "verifiably P" must name the instruments that would fail if P were false, and a dimension with no such instrument must be stated as uninstrumented rather than folded into a neighbouring claim that is instrumented. Concretely: split the requirement row so "safe" keeps its rating and "age-appropriate" drops to low with the uninstrumented dimensions named. Generalised check applicable beyond this row: for each gate composed of independent checkers, enumerate the properties on which every checker abstains, and treat that set as the gate's blind spot rather than as its clean bill. open Reviewed 2026-08-12 in docs/planning/cyo-review-response-2026-08-12b.md section 2.1; extends AL-325
    AL-338 2026-08-12 Building the W1 covering-set path enumerator and running it over the real skeleton catalogue tooling Enumerate-and-filter is the wrong shape for a covering set, and a budget-capped depth-first search reports an honest coverage number that is useless. The first implementation enumerated root-to-ending readings depth first under a 20,000-reading cap and kept each one that covered a new choice. On books with more readings than budget it spent the entire budget inside whichever subtree it entered first, so 16+/the-tenfold-siege reported 30.5% edge coverage; 35 of 61 catalogue books came in below full coverage and 24 reported themselves incomplete. Nothing was lying: the number was exactly what the algorithm achieved. The defect is that coverage was a residue of the search rather than a constructed property, so it inherited the search's arbitrary order. Rebuilding it as targeted construction (index the configuration closure once, forward-BFS for a shortest route in, backward-BFS from endings for a shortest route out, then splice an O(1) reading through each still-uncovered choice) moved all 61 books to 1.000 coverage and cut whole-catalogue time to 2.666 s, because cost now scales with the configuration graph rather than with the reading count. A 55-node book can admit 262,144 distinct readings; any algorithm whose cost tracks that number fails at catalogue scale by construction. When a metric must be guaranteed rather than estimated, construct one witness per target instead of filtering an enumeration, and treat any coverage figure produced under a budget cap as a statement about the budget until proven otherwise. Generalised check: for any coverage-style number, ask what it would report if the budget were halved. If the answer is "a smaller number" rather than "the same number, or an explicit incomplete", the number is measuring the search. applied src/cyo_adventure/validator/paths.py _build_config_graph / _reading_through; measured 61/61 at edge_coverage == 1.0, 61/61 complete, 2.666 s total, recorded in cyo-measurement-workplan-2026-08-12.md W1 Outcome
    AL-339 2026-08-12 Both real defects in the W1 enumerator surviving 16 green unit tests and appearing on first contact with the 61-book catalogue tooling Hand-built fixtures cannot reveal budget-exhaustion defects, because a fixture small enough to hand-build never exhausts a budget. The W1 plan prescribed exactly the right fixtures (a linear book, a diamond, a reconverging spine with a carried flag) and they all passed while the module contained a recursion overflow and a covering set that reached 30% on real input. Both defects are functions of scale relative to a cap, and every fixture sat orders of magnitude below every cap. A related gap in the same test file was self-inflicted and caught only by asking the mutation question directly: the cap=1 test looked like it covered WalkResult.capped propagation, but deleting walk_complete and from both return statements would have left all 16 tests green, because no test ever capped the underlying configuration walk. Any module with a budget, cap, or recursion bound gets a test whose scale is derived from that bound rather than chosen by hand, plus a smoke run over the real corpus before the module is called done; the corpus run is the acceptance step, not a nicety.     AL-340 2026-08-12 A RecursionError on skeletons/10-13/the-winter-of-the-wolf-queen.json that the obvious hypothesis did not explain validator A finer deduplication key is not a safer key. The enumerator originally keyed readings on the full visit_set, on the reasoning that a strictly finer key can only over-count, never under-count, and over-counting is the safe direction. It is not safe, it is wrong: revisiting a node in an identical variable state offers identical choices, so the finer key preserves no reading a child could actually take, it merely fails to notice a loop. The measured consequence on a 250-node book was a 1,730-step "reading" and 28,512 configurations. The first hypothesis (depth scales with node count) was falsified by a 400-node chain fixture that passed, and only instrumenting maximum depth revealed the mechanism. walk.ConfigKey's semantics, visit_set intersected with the set of nodes carrying once on-enter effects, were correct all along and are what the module now uses. Reuse the established key semantics rather than deriving a new one, and when tempted to refine a dedup key, state which distinct behaviour the extra discrimination preserves; if the answer is none, the refinement is a loop detector being switched off. Independently: when a regression fixture built from a hypothesis passes unexpectedly, instrument the real failing input instead of building a second fixture from a second hypothesis. applied src/cyo_adventure/validator/paths.py _path_key, matching validator/walk.py::_config_key; the rejected reasoning is recorded inline at the _PathKey definition so it is not re-derived
    AL-341 2026-08-12 Running pydoclint tree-wide while clearing findings on a new validator module tooling A quality gate scoped to changed files accumulates an invisible backlog, and this one contradicts the house style it is supposed to enforce. pydoclint is configured with arg-type-hints-in-docstring = true, runs only at the pre-push stage, and pre-commit only ever hands it the files in the push diff, so nobody sees the tree-wide state. Run over all of src/ it reports 3,713 findings across 416 files: DOC105/DOC109/DOC110 on essentially every documented function in the package, validator/walk.py included. No GitHub Actions workflow invokes pydoclint at all, so CI never observes it either. Meanwhile CLAUDE.md's own Google-style docstring example writes input_path: Path to input file, with no type hint, which is precisely what the setting rejects. The result is a gate that is satisfied by approximately nothing, enforced only against whoever touches a file next, and pointed the opposite way from the documented convention. Decide which convention is house style and make the config agree with it: either set arg-type-hints-in-docstring = false (matching CLAUDE.md, and avoiding a second copy of every type that can drift from the signature, given arg-type-hints-in-signature = true already holds) or keep the setting and schedule the 416-file cleanup. Either way, add pydoclint to a CI job so the tree-wide count is observable rather than discovered per-file at push time.     AL-342 2026-08-12 Running the W2 decision measurement, where the told-emotion measure cleared the 10 percent bar for the wrong reason validator A rate threshold calibrated at one unit of analysis does not transfer to a smaller unit, and it fails silently by ceasing to bind rather than by erroring. check_prose_craft's told-emotion band is 0.5 hits per 1000 narration words, calibrated on whole books running 2,344 to 24,601 words. Re-unitted onto covering paths it produced disagreement on 13.2% of the corpus, apparently clearing W2's 10 percent bar. Every one of the six book-passes-path-fails cases turned out to be exactly k x 1000 / path_words, the smallest nonzero rate that path length admits: 1,075 words gave 0.930, 570 gave 1.754, 1,856 gave 0.539, 643 gave 1.555, 536 gave 1.866, and 1,187 gave 2.527 at three hits. On a 600-word path a single hit already scores 1.67, so the band never binds and the measure has quietly stopped being a rate and become "does this path contain the phrase at all". The same arithmetic applies to every per-1000 and per-cent measure we own the moment the denominator shrinks. Re-derive the band whenever a measure changes unit, and before trusting any re-unitted rate, compute the smallest nonzero value the new denominator admits and check the band sits above it. If the band sits below that floor, the threshold is inert and the measure has silently become presence/absence; say so rather than reporting the rate. Generalised check applicable to every threshold in the checker suite: record the denominator range the calibration was taken over, so a later re-unit is a visible change of premise rather than an invisible one. open Measured 2026-08-12 over 53 filled books; band and its calibration at scripts/check_prose_craft.py --max-told-per-1000 (default 0.5, calibrated frontier 0.00/0.00/0.00, Sonnet 0.35/0.00/0.00, Haiku 1.10/0.00/0.31); recorded in cyo-measurement-workplan-2026-08-12.md W2 Outcome
    AL-343 2026-08-12 The W2 decision rule scoring moral tags a keep at 17 percent when per-path measurement there is strictly weaker process A disagreement rule that counts either direction as evidence will recommend keeping a finer-grained measure that can only ever be more lenient than its parent. W2's rule keeps a per-path measure when, for at least 10 percent of books, a path falls outside the band while the book falls inside "or the reverse". Moral tags scored 17.0% entirely on the reverse: 0 book-inside-path-outside against 9 book-outside-path-inside. That split is structural rather than incidental. A path's endings are a subset of the book's endings, so its moral-tag count can never exceed the book's, and a per-path check can only ever pass books the book-level check fails. Tense instability showed the same signature at smaller scale, 0 against 2. The rule's own stated purpose is to find measures that "change a verdict", and it silently admits verdict changes in the direction of weakening the gate. Split the rule by direction: only book-inside-path-outside counts toward the threshold, since only that direction represents a defect the book aggregate hides. Before running the rule on any new measure, ask whether the path-scope value can exceed the book-scope value at all; for a count over a subset of nodes or endings it cannot, so the per-path version is weaker by construction and the rule should not be run. Generalised check: for every "finer unit catches more" proposal, establish monotonicity first, because a monotone measure re-unitted downward is a laxer gate wearing the language of a stricter one. applied Refinement recorded and applied in cyo-measurement-workplan-2026-08-12.md W2 Outcome and Rule refinement; carried forward as a precondition for W3 and W6
    AL-344 2026-08-12 Verifying the sixth external review's vendor figures before writing W14 against them process Verifying a proposal's quoted numbers is worth doing even when they all check out, because a vendor's operating constraints are found in the same pass and one of them can invalidate an experiment's design. Every figure the review quoted verified to the digit across three vendors: the Tinker catalogue (DeepSeek-V3.1 $3.718/MTok, Qwen3-8B $0.44/MTok), Modal GPU pricing (H100 SXM5 $3.95/hr, H200 SXM $4.54/hr, A100 80GB $2.50/hr, L40S $1.95/hr, B200 $6.25/hr, Starter $30/month), and the DeepSeek-V4 model cards (V4-Pro 1.6T total / 49B activated, V4-Flash 284B / 13B, 1M context, MoE experts FP4 with the rest FP8, temperature 1.0 / top_p 1.0). The value was not the confirmation. It was two facts found alongside: the catalogue contains no V4 variant at all, which turns every fine-tuning item into "train a different, smaller model", and the card's 384K minimum context window for Think Max reasoning mode, which couples context size to reasoning mode. W14 was drafted to ablate context composition holding everything else fixed; run naively it would have varied reasoning effort at the same time and attributed the difference to context. Verify quoted vendor figures before writing an item against them, and read the surrounding operating constraints in the same pass rather than only the cited number, because the constraint that breaks an experiment is rarely the figure that was quoted. Generalised check for any ablation: for each variable held fixed, ask whether the vendor imposes a threshold that the varied dimension can cross; a mode that switches on a size boundary makes two intended-independent variables dependent, and the resulting confound reads as a clean effect. applied Confound recorded as a design constraint in cyo-measurement-workplan-2026-08-12.md W14 (hold the configured window at or above 384K across all three regimes); the verified Tinker catalogue result is recorded in the same document's deferred table
    AL-345 2026-08-13 The run-6 vendor comparison logging reading_level_repair_discarded nodes_revised=50 on a paid run validator A #CRITICAL comment asserted a branch was unreachable, a mock was built on the same belief, and the belief was false. reading_level_loop's post-splice discard carried the annotation "it should be unfalsifiable (the model never saw the graph and can only return body strings), so a block means an assumption broke". The reasoning conflates cannot change the graph with cannot fail the gate. run_gate sets blocked from CH, L1, L2 and PL findings, and several of those are computed from body text, PL-19's words-per-node wall among them. So a body-only splice can block, and on run-6 it did: 50 revised nodes discarded, the book shipping at grade 5.61 with 12 percent of nodes in band while still reporting status=passed (RL-13 is advisory, and discarded_for_gate reaches no caller). The guard test test_reading_level_gate_regression_discards_the_whole_pass patched run_gate and said so in its docstring: "the real gate cannot be made to fail on a body swap, which is precisely the point". The mock is what kept the branch from ever being exercised for real, so the false premise survived in three places at once: the comment, the docstring and the test. When a comment claims a branch is unreachable, write the test that reaches it before trusting the claim, and reach it through the real collaborator. A mock built on an unreachability assumption cannot falsify that assumption; it re-asserts it. Generalised check applicable to every "this cannot happen" annotation in the tree: name the mechanism that makes it impossible and check that the mechanism is a property of the code, not of the author's mental model of the code. Here the sentence "the model can only return body strings" was true and the conclusion drawn from it was false. applied Comment, attribute docstring, _splice docstring and test docstring all corrected; test_reading_level_one_capped_node_does_not_discard_the_others now drives the branch through the real gate with no patching. Reproduced deterministically in tests/unit/test_reading_level_loop.py
    AL-346 2026-08-13 Bisecting which body-only edit could block the gate on deepseek-v4-pro-fp4 brief 1 validator A relative tolerance cannot enforce an absolute ceiling, and pairing the two silently converts a local pass into a global failure. _preserves_contract bounds a revision's word drift at 10 percent of the original body; PL-19 caps a node at an absolute 155 words for band 5-8 (220 for 8-11). A node already near the wall satisfies the relative check and breaches the absolute one: 147 words plus the permitted 10 percent is 161.7, over a 155 wall. Reading-level repair makes this the likely direction rather than a remote one, because simplifying prose splits long sentences and adds words. Measuring the shipped corpus: exactly 1 node in 845 sat close enough to the wall for its permitted drift to cross it, f_steps at 147 words, and it was in the one book of twenty whose repair was discarded. The uniform-shrink hypothesis was falsified first (shortening every body by up to 50 percent never blocks), which is what narrowed it to a direction-specific ceiling. Whenever a guard and a rule bound the same quantity, check they are expressed in the same units before trusting the guard. A relative bound composes with an absolute one only if it is also clamped to it. _accept now takes the PL-19 wall directly. Generalised check for the rest of the suite: enumerate every pair of (harness guard, validator rule) that constrains one quantity, and for each pair state whether both are relative, both absolute, or mixed; the mixed pairs are where a locally-legal edit produces a globally-illegal document. applied _per_node_cap reads the band profile and _accept refuses a revision over the wall; test_reading_level_rejects_a_revision_that_would_breach_the_word_cap pins it with a 209-word body, a 225-word revision, 7.7 percent drift and an improving grade, so every other acceptance condition says yes
    AL-347 2026-08-13 The same run-6 discard, where one bad node cost 49 good ones validator An all-or-nothing adopt-or-reject on a batched repair gives one unusable item the blast radius of the whole batch. The splice adopted 50 accepted revisions as a single unit and re-gated once; one node over the PL-19 wall rejected all 50. The information needed to do better was already in hand and being thrown away: the blocking findings name node_id, so the offender was identifiable without any extra work. The cost of not using it was the difference between a book at grade 5.61 with 12 percent in band and one repaired on 49 of 50 nodes. Where a batch is validated as a unit but composed of independently-valid items, use the validator's own attribution to drop the named items and re-validate the remainder, rather than discarding the batch. Let the re-run be the authority on whether the salvage worked, so the salvage logic never has to reimplement which rules block. Generalised check: for every place the codebase rejects a composite artifact, ask whether the rejection reason identifies a subset, and if it does, whether the code uses that. applied _drop_offenders plus a salvage re-gate in reading_level_loop; a story-level finding that names no node, and a block that names every accepted node, both still fall through to the full discard. Covered by test_reading_level_one_capped_node_does_not_discard_the_others
    AL-348 2026-08-13 Reading run-6's report and finding cost: null on all 20 books process A paid measurement run was executed on a branch that structurally cannot record the thing it was partly run to measure. Run-6 spent 20 book generations across five vendor legs, and every book carries cost: null with cost_unavailable_reason explaining that per-call token usage is discarded by GenerationProvider.complete on this branch. The capture landed in #701, which is merged to main (154d44f5) but is not in feat/reading-level-repair-loop; core/pricing.py does not exist here at all. The harness was honest, it recorded why the field was empty rather than reporting a zero, and that honesty is the only reason this was caught rather than mistaken for free generation. The waste is nonetheless real: the cost axis of run-6 has to be re-run. Before launching a paid run, check that every axis the run is meant to measure has working instrumentation on the branch the run will execute from, not merely somewhere in the repository. A merged capability is not an available one. Cheap mechanical version: read the run's own output schema and confirm no field the run exists to populate is structurally unpopulable before spending. open Measured 2026-08-13 in out/vendor-comparison/run-6/report.json: 20 of 20 books carry cost: null; src/cyo_adventure/core/pricing.py is absent on this branch. Re-run the cost axis after main merges in, tracked as UW-C245
    AL-349 2026-08-13 Running evaluate_books.py over all four real run dirs after fixing the discard process A harness defect presented as a clean finding about the variable under test, and the finding was false. Run-6 existed to isolate quantisation, and its compliance table reads deepseek-v4-pro-fp4 in-band 0.70 against deepseek-v4-pro-fp8 0.92 and unquantised deepseek-v4-pro 0.85. Read at face value that is a 22-point band-compliance penalty for fp4 and the headline of the run. It is one book. Book 1 of the fp4-pro leg is the book whose reading-level repair was discarded (AL-345), at grade 5.61 and in-band 0.12; its three siblings are 0.92, 0.86 and 0.89. Recomputing each leg with its worst book dropped puts fp4-pro at 0.89, fp8-pro at 0.94 and unquantised pro at 0.88, so fp4 is indistinguishable from no quantisation at all and the entire apparent penalty was the harness marking its own failure as status=passed. The shape is general: with n=4 per leg, one silently-degraded book moves a leg mean by roughly 20 points, which is larger than any real effect this design can resolve. Before attributing a leg-level difference to the variable under test, recompute the comparison with each leg's worst book dropped and report both figures. Where the two disagree by more than the between-leg spread, the difference is a single-book artefact and must be traced to that book before it is written up. Add the drop-worst column to evaluate_books.py's compliance table so the check is automatic rather than remembered, and flag any leg whose mean moves more than one between-leg standard deviation when its worst book is removed. open Measured 2026-08-13 from out/vendor-comparison/evaluation/evaluation.json: fp4-pro all=0.70 drop-worst=0.89 worst=0.12; fp8-pro all=0.92 drop-worst=0.94; pro all=0.85 drop-worst=0.88. Scheduled as UW-C246
    AL-350 2026-08-13 Trying to find which run-6 books were affected and discovering no artifact records it tooling The pipeline recorded its own degradation nowhere durable, so the only evidence of a 50-node repair being thrown away was one line of stdout on a run that had already finished. The persisted book carries metadata with band, topology and reading level but no stage log; books.jsonl carries status, grade, in_band, latency_s and cost, and the affected book's row reads status='passed'. discarded_for_gate exists on the loop result and is consumed by nothing. Grepping all 52 books across four run dirs for any discard marker returns zero hits, not because it did not happen but because nothing writes it down. A post-hoc analyst therefore cannot distinguish a book that was repaired from a book whose repair was discarded, which is precisely the distinction that decides whether run-6's quantisation result means anything (AL-349). Persist the pipeline's self-reported degradation alongside its outcome. Surface discarded_for_gate and the partial-salvage count from the reading-level loop into the orchestrator result, and add them to books.jsonl so a leg comparison can exclude or flag affected books without re-running anything. Generalised: any stage that can silently do less than it was asked must record how much less in the artifact, because a stage-level warning that reaches only a log is invisible to every consumer of the output. open Verified 2026-08-13: books.jsonl keys are attempts, brief_index, cost, cost_unavailable_reason, error, family, file, grade, in_band, latency_s, leaf_words, status, vendor; the affected deepseek-v4-pro-fp4 book 1 row reads status='passed'. Scheduled as UW-C247
    AL-351 2026-08-14 Reviewing an external agent's PR #708 findings against scripts/compare_vendors.py tooling A vendor label doubled as the only artifact-identity component, so a duplicate label in vendors.json would silently overwrite an already-paid-for book with another vendor's. _book_filename names each output {vendor}__{brief_index:02d}.json; _load_vendors validated label and model as strings but never checked uniqueness across entries. A slate with two vendors sharing a label, a copy-paste typo in the spec file being the likeliest cause, would not error at load time. Both legs would run, and the second leg's persist_book call would overwrite the first leg's file for every shared brief_index, leaving books.jsonl the only record that two different legs ever existed. Reject a duplicate label at _load_vendors load time, before any provider call is billed, rather than discovering the collision from a missing book after the run. Generalised check: whenever a field doubles as both a display label and a storage-identity key, validate its uniqueness at the point it is first accepted, not at the point it is used to name a file. applied scripts/compare_vendors.py::_load_vendors (duplicate-label rejection); tests/unit/test_compare_vendors.py::test_load_vendors_rejects_a_duplicate_label
    AL-352 2026-08-14 Reviewing an external agent's PR #708 findings against src/cyo_adventure/validator/paths.py tooling The AL-338 covering-set rebuild fixed budget-dependent coverage but left one shape uncovered: a book whose start node is itself an ending has no choice edges, so the edge-covering loop never runs and the covering set came back empty. covering_paths builds reachable from graph.by_edge, populated only for non-ending configurations; for a one-node book the initial configuration is itself an ending, by_edge is empty, and the function returned paths=[] while reporting edge_coverage=1.0 (vacuously true over zero choices). That reads as "fully covered", not "recorded nothing", and the asymmetry with reader_sample_paths is what made it easy to miss: that function's per-draw loop checks is_ending before it ever needs an edge, so it already emitted the trivial one-node reading draw.count times. Cover the zero-edge case explicitly: when the initial configuration is itself an ending, seed the covering set with that one-node reading before the edge loop runs. Generalised check: when a covering-set construction is keyed off "things that need coverage", audit what happens when there are zero of them but the underlying object still exists and still needs exactly one reading recorded. applied src/cyo_adventure/validator/paths.py::covering_paths; tests/unit/test_paths.py::test_covering_paths_keeps_the_zero_edge_reading_when_the_start_node_is_an_ending
